In Chapters 13 and 16 we surveyed node **P18** — the 257 theorem headers in
`UpperTheory.LiteratureTheorems.*` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/EveryCharacterOfLiteratureOfVWin.lean:215–813`)
that assemble propositions in `GlobalTheory.Published.*` and `UpperTheory.V.Published.*` and discharge
every hypothesis of `OpenLiteratureStatements` on the path to `src/Main.lean` [Lean, read]. We
stressed a foundational principle of formal proof auditing: **Lean's kernel checks logical validity,
not English naming.** For the five top-level theorems of `src/Main.lean`, statement fidelity is
already settled (Chapter 1), because their types mention only vetted Mathlib definitions. Inside the
archive, however, intermediate interfaces carry classical literature names (`GlobalArtinReciprocity`,
`FerreroWashington`, `Kato`, `PoitouTateNormalised`, `BaseChangeDefiniteUnitary`,
`DeRhamPotSemistable`, …). Whether each internal `Prop` states the textbook theorem its name evokes,
a harmless specialization tailored to CM host fields, a weaker corollary that still suffices
downstream, or a vacuous placeholder can only be answered by opening the `.lean` file and comparing
every quantifier, typeclass, and custom structure against the published theorem [Expository].

In this closing chapter of Part IV, we turn that principle into a concrete graduate-level casebook.
We select **three load-bearing sub-clusters** spanning **14 named `Published` declarations** across
class field theory, Iwasawa theory and Galois cohomology, and automorphic / $p$-adic Hodge theory,
read their full `Prop` definitions and surrounding local deduction files line by line (`R3`), test
each against explicit small examples where boundary hypotheses bite, and record a reusable five-step
audit protocol for specialists examining the remaining `[Open]` entries of `P18`.

---

## 18.1 Motivation: logical polarity and how internal `Prop`s can diverge from their names

### 18.1.1 Why `src/Main.lean` remains unconditional while internal names still require auditing

Suppose an archive defines an internal proposition `P_lean : Prop`, proves a theorem `h_prod : P_lean`
in one sub-cone, and proves an implication `h_cons : P_lean → Goal` in another sub-cone, combining
them by modus ponens `h_cons h_prod : Goal` [Expository]. What does kernel verification guarantee,
and what does it *not* guarantee?

1. **Unconditional soundness of `Goal`:** Because `P_lean` is **proved** (not assumed as an `axiom`
   or left as `sorry`), modus ponens `h_cons h_prod` is valid in Lean's logic *even if `P_lean` were
   defined to be `True`*! Indeed, the archive actually contains such a fossil:
   `GlobalTheory.Published.UnusedSlot72 : Prop := True`
   (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/LocalTypeDictionary/DiscreteSpectrumResidualClassification.lean:7`,
   proved by `trivial` in `discrete_spectrum_residual_classification_of_literature`) [Lean, read].
   Because nothing downstream extracts information from `UnusedSlot72`, defining it as `True` does
   not compromise `src/Main.lean` in the slightest.
2. **Why statement-fidelity auditing is nonetheless indispensable:** Human mathematicians cannot
   verify a 17.37-million-line proof by reading all 1,478,243 kernel constants linearly; they verify
   it **modularly**, dividing the argument along classical milestones ("by Artin reciprocity…", "by
   Kato's Euler system…", "by definite unitary base change…"). If an internal milestone `P_lean`
   differs from the published theorem $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$, three distinct phenomena can occur:
   - **Mathematical extraction risk:** A community project hoping to upstream "the archive's proof
     of Theorem X" into Mathlib needs to know whether `P_lean` proves full Theorem X or only a
     special case over CM fields or $\ell$-power conductors.
   - **Architectural misreading risk:** If `P_lean` weakens the conclusion of $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$ (or
     adds an extra hypothesis), a human reader auditing only the downstream file might think Theorem
     X was used in full, whereas an uninspected upstream file might have pushed the hard work into a
     side hypothesis — or conversely, a seemingly alarming omission (such as `Kato` omitting
     $L(f, \chi, r) \neq 0$!) might turn out to be completely sound because a side inequality (`2 * r ≠ k`)
     keeps $(f, \chi, r)$ strictly off the central critical line!
   - **Custom-bundle vacuity risk:** When `P_lean` quantifies over an archive-defined structure
     `∀ B : CustomBundle, Concl B`, `P_lean` is vacuously `True` if `CustomBundle` happens to be
     empty! Auditing fidelity therefore requires checking **non-vacuity** at a genuine consumer or
     instantiation site.

### 18.1.2 Polarity calculus: strengthening vs weakening in hypotheses and conclusions

When comparing a formal proposition $P_{\mathrm{lean}} \equiv (\forall x \in \mathcal D_{\mathrm{lean}},\ H_{\mathrm{lean}}(x) \to C_{\mathrm{lean}}(x))$
against a textbook theorem $P_{\mathrm{lit}} \equiv (\forall x \in \mathcal D_{\mathrm{lit}},\ H_{\mathrm{lit}}(x) \to C_{\mathrm{lit}}(x))$,
keep the **variance (polarity)** of logical implication firmly in view [Expository]:

| Modification in $P_{\mathrm{lean}}$ relative to $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$ | Logical strength of $P_{\mathrm{lean}}$ | Easier to prove upstream? | Harder to use downstream? | Audit verdict category |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Domain restriction** ($\mathcal D_{\mathrm{lean}} \subsetneq \mathcal D_{\mathrm{lit}}$, e.g. CM fields only) | Weaker than $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$ ($P_{\mathrm{lit}} \implies P_{\mathrm{lean}}$) | Yes | Requires consumer $x \in \mathcal D_{\mathrm{lean}}$ | **Special case** (sound if consumer lies in $\mathcal D_{\mathrm{lean}}$) |
| **Added / stronger hypothesis** ($H_{\mathrm{lean}} \implies H_{\mathrm{lit}}$, e.g. $\zeta_\ell \in K$ or $E/E^+$ unramified) | Weaker than $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$ | Yes | Consumer must supply $H_{\mathrm{lean}}$ | **Domain-restricted / harmless base hypothesis** |
| **Weakened hypothesis** ($H_{\mathrm{lit}} \implies H_{\mathrm{lean}}$, e.g. `IsAlgPos` on $E_+^\times$ instead of $E^\times$) | **Stronger** than $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$! | Harder! | Strictly easier to apply | **Faithful (slightly stronger)** |
| **Weakened conclusion** ($C_{\mathrm{lit}} \implies C_{\mathrm{lean}}$, e.g. infinitude without natural density) | Weaker than $P_{\mathrm{lit}}$ | Yes | Consumer must only need $C_{\mathrm{lean}}$ | **Safe weakening** |
| **Placeholder / tautology** ($C_{\mathrm{lean}} \equiv \mathrm{True}$) | Trivial | Trivial (`trivial`) | Useless (carries 0 bits) | **`True` fossil** (`UnusedSlot72`) |

Every single row of this polarity table occurs naturally among the 14 declarations we audit below!

## 18.2 Cluster I — Class field theory, finite-order Hecke characters, and Chebotarev

### 18.2.1 Motivation: separating proof wrappers from `Prop` definitions

In Chapters 10, 13, and 16 we flagged an easy trap for newcomers navigating the archive: the 17-line
file `src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/StandardPublishedResults/StandardTheoremStatements/GlobalArtinReciprocity.lean`
contains definitions such as
`noncomputable def globalArtinReciprocity : GlobalTheory.Published.GlobalArtinReciprocity := ...` (`:8`),
`rayClassFieldSt` (`:11`), and `heckeLAdicCharacter` (`:14`). Those three declarations are **not**
the propositions; they are **proof-term wrappers** (`def` witnessing a `Prop`, importing a cone of
`1,015` modules) [Lean, read]! To audit whether the archive's Artin reciprocity, ray class field
existence, Hecke $\ell$-adic character realization, and Chebotarev density statements match
classical class field theory `[Artin1927]`, `[Tate1967]`, `[Neukirch1999]`, `[Tschebotareff1926]`,
we must open the three files where those `Prop`s are actually defined — and read the downstream
deductions that test their interfaces!

### 18.2.2 Worked example: ray classes and Artin reciprocity on $E = \mathbb Q(i)$ modulo $\mathfrak m = (2 + 2i)$

Test the definitions of Cluster I on the Gaussian field $E = \mathbb Q(i)$ ($\mathcal O_E = \mathbb Z[i]$,
class number $1$, totally complex so no real places exist, $E_+^\times = E^\times$) at the modulus
$\mathfrak m = (2 + 2i)$ of norm $N(\mathfrak m) = (2+2i)(2-2i) = 8$ [Expository]:

1. **Local congruence quotient and global units:** Because $2 = -i(1+i)^2$ and $2 + 2i = 2(1+i) = -i(1+i)^3$,
   the ring $\mathcal O_E / \mathfrak m = \mathbb Z[i]/(2+2i)$ has cardinality $8$, and its unit
   group $(\mathcal O_E / \mathfrak m)^\times$ consists of the $4$ classes coprime to $1+i$:
   $$(\mathbb Z[i]/(2+2i))^\times \;=\; \bigl\{\overline{1},\; \overline{i},\; \overline{-1},\; \overline{-i}\bigr\}.$$
   Check the prime above $5$ carefully! Since $(-1 + 2i) - 1 = -2 + 2i = i(2 + 2i) \in \mathfrak m$, we have
   **$-1 + 2i \equiv 1 \pmod{2+2i}$**, whereas $2 + i = (-i)(-1 + 2i) \equiv -i \not\equiv 1 \pmod{2+2i}$!
   Meanwhile, the global unit group $\mathcal O_E^\times = \{\pm 1, \pm i\}$ maps **bijectively**
   onto $\{\overline{1}, \overline{-1}, \overline{i}, \overline{-i}\} = (\mathbb Z[i]/(2+2i))^\times$!
2. **Ray class group $\mathrm{Cl}_{\mathfrak m}(E)$ and Artin map:** By the exact sequence
   $\mathcal O_E^\times \to (\mathcal O_E/\mathfrak m)^\times \to \mathrm{Cl}_{\mathfrak m}(E) \to \mathrm{Cl}(E) = 1$,
   surjectivity of $\mathcal O_E^\times \to (\mathcal O_E/\mathfrak m)^\times$ forces the ray class
   group $\mathrm{Cl}_{(2+2i)}(\mathbb Q(i))$ to be **trivial**! Consequently, the ray class field
   $L_{\mathfrak m}$ of $\mathbb Q(i)$ modulo $(2 + 2i)$ is $E$ itself (`RayClassFieldSt`), whereas
   raising the modulus to $\mathfrak m' = (4)$ ($(\mathbb Z[i]/(4))^\times$ of order $8$, quotient
   by $\{\pm 1, \pm i\}$ of order $2$) cuts out the quadratic ray class field
   $L_{(4)} = \mathbb Q(i, \sqrt{2}) = \mathbb Q(\zeta_8)$!
3. **Uniformizer Frobenius sign convention (`IsGlobalArtin.frob`):** In `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/AutomorphicForms₂/ExistsUniqueHcharOfFiniteOrder.lean:34–36`,
   for a finite place $v$, a valuation subring $A$ of $\Omega$ over $v$ (`IsPlaceOver A v`) and a local
   uniformizer $\varpi_v \in E_v^\times$ (`IsUnifAt v x`), the Artin map
   $\mathrm{art}_E : \mathbb A_E^\times \to G_E^{\mathrm{ab}}$ sends the single-place idèle
   `localIdele E v x` to `toAb E Ω σ⁻¹`, where `IsFrob A (normV v) σ` says that $\sigma$ lies in the
   decomposition group of $A$ and induces $x \mapsto x^{N(v)}$ on its residue field
   (`src/Formalism/ConjugateSelfDual/Statement.lean:23,28–29`). Thus **uniformizers map to geometric Frobenius $\mathrm{Frob}_v^{\mathrm{geom}} = (\mathrm{Frob}_v^{\mathrm{arith}})^{-1}$**
   — matching Deligne's convention `[Tate1967]` and the algebraic Hecke character package `HeckeLAdicCharacter`!

### 18.2.3 Exact Lean definitions and verbatim literature comparison (Cluster I)

**Definition 18.1 (Cluster I `Published` propositions).** [Lean]

1. **`GlobalTheory.Published.GlobalArtinReciprocity` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/AutomorphicForms₂/ExistsUniqueHcharOfFiniteOrder.lean:70–72`, cone `9`):**
   $$\forall (E : \mathrm{Type})\,[\mathrm{Field}\,E]\,[\mathrm{NumberField}\,E]\,(\Omega : \mathrm{Type})\,[\mathrm{Field}\,\Omega]\,[\mathrm{Algebra}\,E\,\Omega]\,[\mathrm{IsAlgClosure}\,E\,\Omega],\;
   \exists\,\mathrm{art} : \mathrm{Ideles}\,E \to_* \mathrm{Gab}\,E\,\Omega,\; \mathrm{IsGlobalArtin}\,\mathrm{art},$$
   where `Gab E Ω` is the topological abelianization of $G_E=\mathrm{Gal}(\Omega/E)$ (`:15`) and
   `IsGlobalArtin art` (`:25–39`) bundles five fields. In the last three, $v$ is a finite place and
   $A$ is a valuation subring of $\Omega$ lying over $v$ (`IsPlaceOver A v`):
   - `quot : IsOpenQuotientMap art` (continuous open surjection $\mathbb A_E^\times \twoheadrightarrow G_E^{\mathrm{ab}}$, where $G_E^{\mathrm{ab}} = G_E / \overline{[G_E, G_E]}$ carries the quotient Krull topology);
   - `ker : art.ker = ((diagUnits E).range ⊔ infConn E).topologicalClosure` (kernel equals the closure of $E^\times \cdot (E_\infty^\times)^\circ$ inside $\mathbb A_E^\times$);
   - `ine` (the image of the local units $\mathcal O_v^\times$ under $\mathrm{art}\circ\iota_v$ **equals** the image of the inertia group of $A$ in $G_E^{\mathrm{ab}}$; no closure is taken);
   - `frob` (the images of the local uniformizers $\varpi_v$ are **exactly** the elements $\mathrm{toAb}(\sigma^{-1})$ with $\sigma$ an arithmetic Frobenius at $A$; this is an `↔` for each $g$);
   - `dec` (the topological closure of the image of $E_v^\times$ **equals** the image of the decomposition group of $A$).
2. **`GlobalTheory.Published.HeckeLAdicCharacter` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/AutomorphicForms₂/ExistsUniqueHcharOfFiniteOrder.lean:74–78`, cone `9`):**
   For every $E$ and algebraic closure $\Omega$ **there exists** an Artin map `art` with
   `IsGlobalArtin art` such that for every prime $\ell$, comparison isomorphism
   $\iota : \overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell \simeq \mathbb C$, Hecke character $\chi$ and infinity type $n$
   with `IsAlgPos χ n` (`:41–42`), there exists a **unique** continuous
   $r : G_E \to (\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell)^\times$ satisfying `IsLAdicOf art ι χ n r` (`:57–60`).
   The quantifier order is $\forall E\,\Omega,\ \exists\,\mathrm{art},\ \ldots$; the statement does
   not apply to an arbitrary Artin map. `IsLAdicOf` is a relation on all of `Ideles E`: whenever
   $\mathrm{art}(x)=\mathrm{toAb}(\sigma)$, the value $r(\sigma)$ is $\iota^{-1}$ of $\chi(x)$ corrected
   by the archimedean and $\ell$-adic power factors `infPow n x` and `lPow ι n x`. Frobenius values at
   unramified places prime to $\ell$ are a consequence (`IsLAdicOf.frob_unr`, `:296–301`, which also
   uses the `frob` field of `IsGlobalArtin`).
3. **`GlobalTheory.Published.RayClassFieldSt` & `RayClassFieldMaximalSt` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/PublishedFoundations/NonemptyAlgHomOfIsRayOfDvd.lean:17,32`, cone `686`):**
   `RayClassFieldSt` is defined as `GlobalTheory.NT.RayField`
   (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Definitions/GlobalTwisting/LFunctionPositiveConductorFourEight.lean:84–92`):
   for every number field $K$ and nonzero ideal $\mathfrak m$ there is a finite abelian $L/K$,
   unramified at every prime not dividing $\mathfrak m$, whose Frobenius elements at those primes
   generate $\mathrm{Gal}(L/K)$, and such that a nonzero ideal $I$ prime to $\mathfrak m$ has trivial
   value under every character of $\mathrm{Gal}(L/K)$ exactly when $I$ lies in the ray modulo
   $\mathfrak m$. The Artin-isomorphism formulation is the proved equivalent form at `:19–25`.
   In `RayClassFieldMaximalSt` (`:32–36`), $L$ is a field with `IsRay L 𝔪` and
   every candidate finite abelian $L'/K$ must satisfy
   `∀ χ : (L' ≃ₐ[K] L') →* ℂˣ, IsModulus L' χ 𝔪`. Under this character-by-character conductor
   hypothesis, the conclusion is `Nonempty (L' →ₐ[K] L)`.
4. **`GlobalTheory.Published.ChebotarevDegreeOne` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/ChebotarevDegreeOneIffCheb.lean:12–18`, cone `211`):**
   For every number field $F$, finite Galois extension $L/F$, element $\sigma \in \operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$,
   and finite set $S$ of maximal ideals of $\mathcal O_F$, there exists a maximal ideal $v \notin S$
   unramified in $L$ of **absolute inertia degree $1$** ($\mathrm{absNorm}(v) = p$ prime, i.e.
   $\mathcal O_F / v \cong \mathbb F_p$) whose Frobenius conjugacy class in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$
   contains $\sigma$.

### 18.2.4 Fidelity lemma and line-by-line `R3` proofs (Cluster I)

**Lemma 18.1 (Cluster I internal deductions verifying non-vacuity and strength).** [Lean, read]

1. *(Finite-order Galois–Hecke correspondence: `exists_unique_hchar_of_finite_order`, `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/AutomorphicForms₂/ExistsUniqueHcharOfFiniteOrder.lean:381–418`, and `HeckeLAdicCharacter.full`, `:466–491`)*
   *(a) Let `art` satisfy `IsGlobalArtin art`. For every prime $\ell$, $\iota : \overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell \simeq \mathbb C$, $m \ge 1$ and continuous $r : G_E \to_* (\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell)^\times$ with $r^m = 1$, there is a unique Hecke character $\chi$ with `χ.OrderDvd m` and `IsLAdicOf art ι χ 0 r`. This direction uses only `IsGlobalArtin`.*
   *(b) Assume `H : HeckeLAdicCharacter` and let `art` be the Artin map that `H` supplies. Then every $\chi$ with `χ.OrderDvd m` has a unique continuous $r$ with $r^m = 1$ and `IsLAdicOf art ι χ 0 r`. This direction uses `H`.*
   *For the Artin map of `H`, (a) and (b) together make `IsLAdicOf art ι · 0 ·` a bijection between continuous characters $r$ with $r^m=1$ and Hecke characters of order dividing $m$. The relation is the idèlic relation `IsLAdicOf … 0` at infinity type zero, which reads $r(\sigma)=\iota^{-1}(\chi(x))$ whenever $\mathrm{art}(x)=\mathrm{toAb}(\sigma)$; it is not a Frobenius relation.*
2. *(Topological Frobenius density from `ChebotarevDegreeOne`, and unconditional ray class field maximality: `ChebotarevDegreeOne.dense` and `eq_of_frob`, `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/ChebotarevDegreeOneIffCheb.lean:51–68`, and `rayClassFieldMaximalSt_holds`, `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/PublishedFoundations/NonemptyAlgHomOfIsRayOfDvd.lean:204–225`)*
   *Existential `ChebotarevDegreeOne` implies that unramified Frobenius elements are topologically dense in $G_F = \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F)$ and that two continuous representations $G_F \to_* M$ into any $T_2$ group agreeing on Frobenius outside $S$ are identical. Separately, `rayClassFieldMaximalSt_holds : RayClassFieldMaximalSt` holds with no hypotheses.*

*Proof.*
(1)(a) In `exists_unique_hchar_of_finite_order` (`:381–418`), the continuous $r$ factors through
the abelianization as `liftAb r hr` (`:319–323`), and $\psi := \mathrm{liftAb}(r)\circ\mathrm{art}$
is continuous with $\psi^m=1$ (`:384–390`). By `isOpen_ker_of_pow` (`:363–372`) its kernel is
open, so $\chi_0 := \iota\circ\psi$ has open kernel and is continuous (`continuous_of_isOpen_ker'`,
`:374–379`). It is trivial on $E^\times$ because the diagonal units lie in $\ker\mathrm{art}$ by the
`ker` field (`:396–401`). The relation `IsLAdicOf art ι χ₀ 0 r` holds because `infPow 0` and
`lPow ι 0` are identically $1$ (`infPow_zero`, `lPow_zero`, `:191–195`) and
`liftAb r (toAb σ) = r σ` (`:326–327`); see `:407–410`. For uniqueness, take $\chi$ with the same
relation, write $\mathrm{art}(x)=\mathrm{toAb}(\tau)$ via `toAb_surjective` (`:236`), and compare the
two values at $\tau$ (`:411–418`).
(1)(b) In `HeckeLAdicCharacter.full` (`:466–491`), the converse branch obtains $r$ from `H` at
infinity type $0$ (`:489`), using `isAlgPos_zero_of_orderDvd` (`:420–444`) to verify the
algebraicity premise for a character of finite order. Then `IsLAdicOf.pow_eq_one` (`:446–454`)
and surjectivity of `art` give $r^m=1$, and `IsLAdicOf.unique` (`:243–247`) gives uniqueness
(`:490–491`). The other branch (`:484`) is exactly (a).

(2) In `ChebotarevDegreeOne.dense` (`:51–61`), every open $U \subseteq G_F$ in the Krull topology
contains a coset $\tau \cdot \ker(\mathrm{res}_{L/F})$ for some finite Galois $L/F$ (`isTopologicalBasis_krull`).
Applying `ChebotarevDegreeOne` to $L/F$ and $\sigma = \mathrm{res}_{L/F}(\tau)$ produces $v \notin S$
and $\rho \in D_v$ with $\mathrm{res}_{L/F}(\rho) = \mathrm{res}_{L/F}(\tau)$, so $\rho \in U$!
Two continuous maps into a Hausdorff group agreeing on a dense set are equal (`eq_of_frob`, `:63–68`).
In `rayClassFieldMaximalSt_holds` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/PublishedFoundations/NonemptyAlgHomOfIsRayOfDvd.lean:204–225`), given a ray class
field $L/K$ modulo $\mathfrak m$ and an abelian $L'/K$ satisfying the characterwise modulus
hypothesis, the proof embeds both in a compositum $M$ inside an algebraic closure of $K$
(`:206–222`) and applies `range_le` (`:141–198`) with the input
`closure_isFrob_eq_top M hm` (`:223`). The latter (`:118–139`) shows that Frobenius elements at
primes prime to $\mathfrak m$ generate $\mathrm{Gal}(M/K)$. It argues by contradiction: from a
nontrivial $\sigma$ (`:123`), `exists_two_primes_of_isFrob_one` produces two distinct primes above
each prime outside a finite set (`:124–126`), and the zeta-pole lemma
`ZetaPole.not_injective_double_primes` rules this out (`:130–139`). These two lemmas are used at
statement level here [Lean]. No `RayClassFieldSt` or `ChebotarevDegreeOne` hypothesis enters. `range_le` compares restrictions of Frobenius using the ray class and modulus conditions
and passes to the required inclusion of ranges. This gives the $K$-algebra embedding.

## 18.3 Cluster II — Iwasawa theory, $p$-adic $L$-values, Kato's Euler system, and Poitou–Tate duality

### 18.3.1 Motivation: where $p$-adic and cohomological edge hypotheses hide

Cluster II audits five `Published` declarations powering Chapters 10 and 11 (`P11` and `P12` `VWin`):
`FerreroWashington`, `SiegelKlingen` / `DeligneRibet`, `Kato`, and `PoitouTateNormalised` /
`PoitouTateDuality`. In algebraic number theory, every one of these theorems has notorious edge cases:
$\ell = 2$ vs odd $\ell$, real archimedean places where $H^2(\mathbb R, \mu_2) \neq 0$, the denominator
$\frac{1}{12}$ of $\zeta(-1)$ before Deligne–Ribet smoothing, and central vs off-center critical points
of modular $L$-functions! Reading the five files at `R3` shows that the archive navigated all four
edge cases explicitly — and in `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/Divisors₂/SiegelKlingenDeligneRibet.lean:223–245`, even included an internal
machine-checked `example` sanity-checking its own smoothing formula at $s = -1$!

### 18.3.2 Worked examples: Deligne–Ribet smoothing at $\zeta_{\{2\}}(-1) = \frac{1}{12}$ and the $\ell = 2$ real-place obstruction

For $F=\mathbb Q$, $\ell=2$, $n=1$, $k=2$, $\varepsilon=1$ and $\mathfrak c=(3)$,
the modulus is $(2)$. The example computes the ray value
$(1-2)\zeta(-1)=1/12$ at $1-k=-1$. Its $2$-adic norm is $4$, so the unsmoothed value fails
`InZl`. Translation fixes the constant function and smoothing gives
$1/12-3^2/12=-2/3$, of $2$-adic norm $1/2$. This is an explicit counterexample to
unshifted integrality. [Lean, read] See
`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/Divisors₂/SiegelKlingenDeligneRibet.lean:223–245`.

For Poitou–Tate, $F=\mathbb Q$ and $\ell=2$ fail the guard `l ≠ 2 ∨ IsTotallyComplex F`.
The real Galois group has order two, so its Tate cohomology with trivial $\mathbb F_2$
coefficients is nonzero. A totally complex field such as $\mathbb Q(i)$ satisfies the guard
even at $\ell=2$. [Expository]

### 18.3.3 Exact Lean definitions and verbatim literature comparison (Cluster II)

**Definition 18.2 (Cluster II interfaces).**

1. **`UpperTheory.V.Published.FerreroWashington`.** For every prime $\ell$ and abelian number
   field $K/\mathbb Q$ that contains a primitive root of unity of order $4$ if $\ell=2$ and of
   order $\ell$ otherwise (the premise `(∃ ζ : K, IsPrimitiveRoot ζ (if l = 2 then 4 else l)) →`,
   `:9`), there exists $r\in\mathbb N$ such that for every $n\in\mathbb N$ and every
   cyclotomic extension $K'/K$ of order $\ell^n$ in the sense of `IsCyclotomicExtension {l ^ n} K K'`,
   the class group $A=\mathrm{Cl}(\mathcal O_{K'})$ satisfies $|A/A^\ell|\le\ell^r$.
   The universal tower-level quantifier follows the existential bound. There is no localization
   premise. The root-of-unity premise is present in the statement, but the archive's proof
   `UpperTheory.V.FW.ferreroWashington` introduces it anonymously in `intro l _ K _ _ _ _` and never
   uses it, so the same argument bounds every abelian $K$
   (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/GaloisLibrary/FerreroWashingtonIndex/FerreroWashington.lean:104–106`).
   This is a bounded mod-$\ell$ rank formulation of a consequence of the classical $\mu=0$ theorem
   [FerreroWashington1979]. [Lean]
   See `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/PublishedTheorems/FerreroWashington.lean:7–11`.
2. **`GlobalTheory.Published.SiegelKlingen`, `DeligneRibet` and `SiegelKlingenDeligneRibet`.**
   Siegel–Klingen provides a continuation with rational values at $1-k$ for $k\ge1$,
   over totally real fields and nonzero moduli, for rational-valued ray functions.
   Deligne–Ribet uses $\mathfrak f=(\ell^n)$ with $n\ge1$. For every ray function $\varepsilon$
   whose values on ideals coprime to $\mathfrak f$ are in $\mathbb Z_{(\ell)}$ and every
   $\mathfrak c$ coprime to $\mathfrak f$, it takes two rational values as premises.
   `IsRayValue` at $1-k$ specifies $a$ for $\varepsilon$ and $a'$ for
   `translate` by $\mathfrak c$, the function $I\mapsto\varepsilon(\mathfrak c I)$.
   The conclusion is `InZl` for $a-N(\mathfrak c)^k a'$. The combined declaration is a conjunction.
   The integrality interface specializes the modulus and the smoothing test of [DeligneRibet1980].
   [Lean] See `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/Divisors₂/SiegelKlingenDeligneRibet.lean:14–32`
   and `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/Divisors₂/SiegelKlingenDeligneRibet.lean:40–52`.
3. **`UpperTheory.V.Published.Kato`.** For every weight $k\ge2$, `Newform k`, prime $\ell$,
   algebraic closure of $\mathbb Q$, and comparison isomorphism $\iota$, the declaration asserts
   finite dimensionality of `coefField g ι` and existence of a continuous two-dimensional
   representation $V$ over it satisfying `g.Att ι (toBar V)`. This same $V$ must give finite
   `Sel` after cyclotomic twist by $r$ and restriction to every abelian $K/\mathbb Q$ in
   the closure, for every $1\le r\le k-1$ with $2r\ne k$ and every `IsLat` lattice for the twist.
   `IsLat` includes stability. `Sel` uses the quotient by this lattice and the away, above and
   archimedean conditions in `IsSel`. The prime quantifier includes $2$, and residual irreducibility
   is absent. Off-center critical twists restrict the conclusion's domain. A full comparison of
   the attachment and local conditions with [Kato2004, Thm. 14.2] remains [Open]. [Lean]
   See `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/PublishedTheorems/QuotRepMk.lean:27–38`,
   `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/PublishedTheorems/QuotRepMk.lean:44–59`
   and `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/PublishedTheorems/QuotRepMk.lean:65–73`.

4. **`GlobalTheory.Published.PoitouTateNormalised` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/PoitouTateNormalised.lean:12–28`, cone `11`) & `PoitouTateDuality` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/ExistsPrimitiveRootPow.lean:13–27`, cone `10`):**
   For any number field $F$, prime $\ell$ with **`l ≠ 2 ∨ IsTotallyComplex F`**, finite set $S$
   containing all primes above $\ell$, $r \ge 1$, and finite continuous $G_{F,S}$-module $M$ killed by
   $\ell^r$ (`∀ m : M, (l ^ r) • m = 0`), with dual $M' := \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z}(M, \mu_{\ell^r}(F_{S,\mathrm{max}}))$
   (`DualMod`), there exists a $9$-term exact sequence
   $$0 \to H^0(G_{F,S}, M) \to \prod_{v \in S} H^0(G_v, M) \to H^2(G_{F,S}, M')^\vee \to H^1(G_{F,S}, M) \to \prod_{v \in S} H^1(G_v, M) \to H^1(G_{F,S}, M')^\vee \to H^2(G_{F,S}, M) \to \prod_{v \in S} H^2(G_v, M) \to H^0(G_{F,S}, M')^\vee \to 0$$
   whose restriction maps are `loc0`, `loc1` and `loc2` in `NineTermExact`. The three pairing arrows are fixed and the two connecting maps are supplied as witnesses. [§18.3.5](#1835-local-conditions-and-the-selmer-exact-sequence).

### 18.3.4 Fidelity lemma and line-by-line `R3` proofs (Cluster II)

**Lemma 18.2 (Cluster II consumer alignment and Greenberg–Wiles cancellation).**

1. **Off-center non-vanishing.** In the classical normalization, either $r$ or $k-r$ is at least
   $(k+1)/2$ when $2r\ne k$. Absolute Euler-product convergence handles the strict inequality,
   boundary non-vanishing handles equality, and the functional equation transfers the result
   at $k-r$ to $r$. This addresses the non-vanishing premise raised in the community discussion.
   `KatoUse` is a separate `Prop`. It assumes $\ell\ne2$, odd weight $2e+1$ with $e\ge1$,
   continuity, an attachment for the inverse cyclotomic twist of $W$, and `KillsMu M φ`
   with $M\ge1$. It concludes `BlochKatoSelmerVanishes` for the twist of $W$ by $\varphi$.
   This records the consumer interface [Lean]. See
   `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/UnitaryThreeGroups/KatoUse.lean:13–17`.

2. **Global degree bound and Selmer count.** [Lean, read] `cohDimTwoGlobal` uses the separate higher-vanishing output of `PoitouTateNormalised` (`ExistsPrimitiveRootPow.lean:70–85`). For the Greenberg–Wiles count assume the odd-prime guard, local finiteness and duality, and the local and global Euler identities. Let $S_L$ and $S'$ be the Selmer groups for $M$ and $M'$ using $L_v$ and $L_v^\perp$, respectively. Write $H^0=H^0(G_{F,S},M)$, $H^{0\prime}=H^0(G_{F,S},M')$ and $A=\prod_{w\ {\rm real}}|M^{c_w}|\cdot|M|^{r_2(F)}$. Then
   $$
   |S_L|\,|H^{0\prime}|\,\Bigl(\prod_{v\in S}|H^0(G_v,M)|\Bigr) A
   = |S'|\,|H^0|\,\prod_{v\in S}|L_v|.
   $$

   `GreenbergWilesOfPoitouTate.lean:45–106` counts the degree-one pullback using the intersection with $L=\prod_v L_v$. It uses kernel duality to identify the degree-one and degree-two kernel orders at line 84. The Euler identities supply the remaining factors. The final lemma `arith` rewrites the products and cancels a positive factor. [§18.3.5](#1835-local-conditions-and-the-selmer-exact-sequence) gives the definitions, exact sequence and cancellation in detail.

### 18.3.5 Local conditions and the Selmer exact sequence

**Motivation.** A Selmer condition asks that each component of a localized global class belong to a prescribed submodule. The obstruction to realizing a tuple modulo these submodules is detected by pairing with dual global classes. Counting the obstruction leaves a degree-two localization kernel, which cancels against its dual degree-one kernel in the Greenberg–Wiles formula.

**Example [Expository].** Take two local spaces $k^2$ over $k=\mathbb F_3$, with dot-product duals, and use the sum pairing $B(y,x)=\sum_{i=1}^4y_ix_i$. Let $H=H'=k^3$ and take
$$f(a,b,c)=(a,0,b,0),\qquad g(d,e,h)=(0,d,0,e).$$
Their images are exact annihilators. In the first and second local spaces choose $L_1=ke_1$ and $L_2=ke_4$, where the coordinates at the two places are numbered $(1,2)$ and $(3,4)$. Then $L=L_1\times L_2$ and $L^\perp=ke_2\oplus ke_3$. The two Selmer spaces are $\{(a,0,c)\}$ and $\{(d,0,h)\}$. The quotient by $L$ has coordinates $(x_2,x_3)$ and maps to the dual of the second Selmer space by $(x_2,x_3)\mapsto[(d,0,h)\mapsto d x_2]$. The image of global localization in the quotient is the $x_3$-axis. The pairing arrow's cokernel is a line represented by the functional $h$.

For a model of the degree-two tail, write $(u,v)$ for the functional coefficients on $d,h$. Attach the maps $(u,v)\mapsto(v,0)$ into $k^2$, $(s,t)\mapsto(t,0)$ into another $k^2$, and $(x,y)\mapsto y$ into $k$. The image of each arrow is the next kernel. The counts below read $9\cdot3\cdot81=9\cdot27\cdot9$ and $3\cdot9=9\cdot3$. This example starts with exact annihilation and a chosen tail. The arithmetic proof of these inputs belongs to global duality.

**Definitions.** Let $F$ be a number field with algebraic closure $\Omega$, $N=\ell^r$ with $\ell$ prime and $r>0$, and $R=\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$. The archive indexes $S$ by finite places, includes every place above $\ell$, and chooses a place $A_v$ of $\Omega$ above each $v$ to obtain the decomposition group $G_v$ and its map to $G_{F,S}$. Let $M$ be a finite discrete $R$-module with continuous action of $G_{F,S}$. Its Tate dual is $M'=\operatorname{Hom}(M,\mu_N)$ with natural action. In Lean, `twDual` equips the linear dual with the contragredient action twisted by the character $\mu$ descending the absolute cyclotomic character modulo $N$. A primitive root $\zeta$ gives coordinates on $\mu_N$. For a finite $R$-module $D$, use $D^\vee=\operatorname{Hom}_R(D,R)$. Sending $a$ to $a/N$ modulo $\mathbb Z$ identifies this with the character dual. Characters on a submodule extend to $D$: adjoin one generator $x$ at a time and, if $m$ is its order modulo the current subgroup, choose a value whose $m$-fold multiple is the assigned value on $mx$. The extended character lands in the $N$-torsion of $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. Cyclic decomposition gives $|D^\vee|=|D|$.

Write $H^i=H^i(G_{F,S},M)$, $H^{i\prime}=H^i(G_{F,S},M')$, $P_i=\prod_{v\in S}H^i(G_v,M)$, $H=H^1$, $H'=H^{1\prime}$ and $P=P_1$. Let $P'$ be the degree-one local product for $M'$ and let $f:H\to P$ and $g:H'\to P'$ be localization. The local pairing $B_v(y,x)$ is the cup product with the dual class first followed by the invariant $j_v$ into $R$. Choose a submodule $L_v\subset H^1(G_v,M)$ and put $L_v^\perp=\{y:B_v(y,x)=0\text{ for all }x\in L_v\}$. For the sum pairing $B=\sum_v B_v$, the annihilator of $L=\prod_v L_v$ is $\prod_v L_v^\perp$, as can be seen by testing tuples supported at one place. The Lean definition `perpCond` tests vanishing before the invariant, which is equivalent by injectivity of $j_v$. The Selmer groups are $S_L=f^{-1}(L)$ and $S'=g^{-1}(L^\perp)$. Write $\mathrm{Sha}^i$ and $\mathrm{Sha}^{i\prime}$ for the kernels of localization in degree $i$ for $M$ and $M'$.

**Proposition 18.3.5 (Selmer exact sequence and Greenberg–Wiles count).** [Expository] Assume the normalized nine-term sequence and kernel duality supplied by `PoitouTateNormalised`, local finiteness in degrees zero through two for both coefficient modules, and perfect local degree-one pairings. Then this sequence of finite modules is exact:
$$0\to S_L\to H^1\to P_1/L\to (S')^\vee\to H^2\to P_2\to (H^{0\prime})^\vee\to 0.$$
[Lean, read] Assume also the local and global Euler identities used in `greenbergWiles_of_poitouTate` and its guard $\ell\ne2$. Set $A=\prod_{w\ {\rm real}}|M^{c_w}|\cdot |M|^{r_2(F)}$, where $c_w$ acts as complex conjugation and $r_2(F)$ is the number of complex places. Then
$$|S_L|\,|H^{0\prime}|\,\Bigl(\prod_{v\in S}|H^0(G_v,M)|\Bigr) A
=|S'|\,|H^0|\,\prod_{v\in S}|L_v|.$$

**Proof.** The two clauses of kernel duality give finiteness of $\ker f$, $\ker g$ and $\mathrm{Sha}^2$. The corresponding local targets are finite, hence so are $H,H',H^2$. Invariants are subgroups of the finite coefficients. Define $T:P\to(H')^\vee$ by $T(x)(z)=B(gz,x)$. This is `tr1`, so degree-one exactness gives $\ker T=\operatorname{im}f=(\operatorname{im}g)^\perp$. The perfect sum pairing identifies $P$ with $(P')^\vee$. Character extension then gives $\operatorname{im}T=(\ker g)^\perp$ in $(H')^\vee$. Moreover $T(L)$ is the annihilator of $S'$ in $(H')^\vee$. Indeed a character killing $S'$ factors through $H'/S'$, which embeds via $g$ into $P'/L^\perp$. Extend the character to that quotient and pull it back to $P'$. Under the perfect pairing this is an element of $(L^\perp)^\perp=L$. The last equality follows because characters of $P/L$ separate points.

The arrow from $P_1/L$ is $T$ followed by restriction to $S'$. Its kernel is $(L+\operatorname{im}f)/L$, since $T(L)$ is the kernel of restriction. Its image consists of characters of $S'$ killing $\ker g$, by extending those characters to $H'/\ker g$. The original connecting arrow $\delta_2:(H')^\vee\to H^2$ kills $T(L)$, so descends to $(S')^\vee$ by surjectivity of restriction. Its descended kernel is the image of $P_1/L$, by $\ker\delta_2=\operatorname{im}T$. Its image is $\mathrm{Sha}^2$. The initial kernel is $S_L$ by definition and the remaining arrows are exact by the original tail. This is the seven-term sequence of Wiles, with the five-term segment through $(S')^\vee$ ending in $\mathrm{Sha}^2\to0$. [Wiles1995, Proposition 1.6, pp. 472–473](https://mat.puc-rio.br/~nicolau/olimp/Wiles.pdf)

The pairing arrow has image of order $|S'|/|\ker g|$. Counting at $P/L$ therefore gives
$$|S_L|\,|\ker g|\,|P|=|S'|\,|H^1|\,|L|.$$
Lean proves this count using pullbacks, double annihilators and the intersection of $\operatorname{im}f$ with $L$. For the tail, counting $\operatorname{im}(H^2\to P_2)$ as a quotient and as a kernel gives $|\mathrm{Sha}^2|\,|P_2|=|H^2|\,|H^{0\prime}|$.

Write $a=|S_L|$, $b=|S'|$, $s=|\ker g|=|\mathrm{Sha}^2|$, $p_i=|P_i|$, $h_i=|H^i|$, $h'_0=|H^{0\prime}|$, $t=|L|$, and $m=|M|^{[F:\mathbb Q]}$. The two counts and the Euler identities give
$$a s p_1=b h_1 t,\qquad s p_2=h_2 h'_0,\qquad p_1=p_0p_2m,\qquad h_0h_2m=h_1 A.$$
The third relation multiplies the local Euler identities using $\sum_{v\mid\ell}[F_v:\mathbb Q_\ell]=[F:\mathbb Q]$. The other finite places contribute exponent zero. The fourth is the global Euler identity. Multiply $a h'_0 p_0 A$ by $h_1$ and substitute the fourth, second, third and first relations in order to get $h_0a s p_1=b h_0h_1t$. Cancel $h_1>0$. When $r=1$, taking exponents of $\ell$ yields
$$\dim S_L-\dim S'=\dim H^0-\dim H^{0\prime}
+\sum_{v\in S}(\dim L_v-\dim H^0(G_v,M))-\log_\ell A.$$
For general $r$, use composition lengths instead of dimensions, since every simple $R$-module has order $\ell$. In the example the degree-one count alone gives $\dim S_L-\dim S'=3-1+2-4=0$. $\square$

**Remarks.** [Paper] The seven-term row is Wiles's Selmer sequence over $\mathbb Q$. Over a general number field the calculation above uses the stated local and global Euler identities. In the nine-term row the classical convention uses Tate cohomology at infinity, so those modified groups vanish for odd-primary coefficients at real places and at every complex place. The factor $A$ in the count uses ordinary degree-zero invariants. The archive's normalized nine-term interface has the guard $\ell\ne2$ or $F$ totally complex. The Greenberg–Wiles consumer uses $\ell\ne2$. [Tate, §§1–3](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/BSDseminar/refs/TateICM.pdf) · [Milne, I.4.10 and I.5.1](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf) · [Wiles, Proposition 1.6, pp. 472–473](https://mat.puc-rio.br/~nicolau/olimp/Wiles.pdf)

**Normalization.** The archive uses the uniformizer test $\kappa_\varpi\cup\chi$, where $\chi$ takes value $1$ on arithmetic Frobenius. Milne's invariant of this class is $-1/N$. The archive requires $1$ in $R$, so $j_v=-\nu\circ\operatorname{inv}_v$ in the chosen root coordinates, with $\nu$ taking $a/N+\mathbb Z$ to $a\bmod N$. Reversing the test cup to $\chi\cup\kappa_\varpi$ gives Milne's value $1/N$. A simultaneous sign reversal on the local pairing arrows preserves their kernels and images. `NineAdd` allows unit signs on the six fixed maps, and the Greenberg–Wiles instance uses exactness, bijectivity of the invariant and kernel duality. The sign calculation and a local example are in [§18.3.6](#1836-the-unramified-local-condition-and-its-dual). [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, III §§1, 3, pp. 99–100, 109–110](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf)

### 18.3.6 The unramified local condition and its dual

**Motivation.** In [§18.3.5](#1835-local-conditions-and-the-selmer-exact-sequence), the degree-one comparison arrow $P_1/L \to (S')^\vee$ descends from the transgression `tr1` by pairing a local tuple $x = (x_v)_{v \in S}$ modulo $L = \prod_{v \in S} L_v$ against the localizations of a dual Selmer class $z \in S'$ via $B_v(\operatorname{res}_v z, x_v) = j_v(C(\operatorname{res}_v z, x_v))$ [Lean, read]. At any finite place $v \in S$ of residue characteristic $p \ne \ell$ where the finite $\ell$-primary module $M$ is unramified, the canonical Selmer condition is the unramified subgroup $L_v = U(M) := \ker[H^1(G_v, M) \to H^1(I_v, M)]$. Computing `(S')^\vee` and testing obstructions at $v$ therefore requires two local supplies that §18.3.5 left open: first, how the archive constructs and normalizes the local invariant isomorphism $j_v : H^2(G_v, \mu_{\ell^n}) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Z/\ell^n\mathbb Z$ used in `tr1` (and how its sign relates to Milne's arithmetic-Frobenius convention); second, why the exact annihilator $L_v^\perp$ (`perpCond`) of $U(M)$ under the local Tate pairing is precisely the unramified subgroup $U(M^D)$ of the Tate dual $M^D = \operatorname{Hom}(M, \mu_{\ell^n})$ (`ReciprocityTheory.Gal.UnramifiedOrthogonality`, one of the 27 non-`Published` literature-layer constants flagged as unreviewed in LUMI `docs/07-named-theorems.md` [Community]). [Expository]

**Examples (two worked pairings over $\mathbb Q_7$ at $\ell = 3$).** Fix $K = \mathbb Q_7$ ($p = q = 7$) and odd prime $\ell = 3$ with $n = 1$, $N = 3$, and $R = \mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z$ [Expository]. Since $7 \equiv 1 \pmod 3$ and $2^3 = 8 \equiv 1 \pmod 7$ with $2 \not\equiv 1 \pmod 7$, Hensel's lemma supplies a unique primitive cube root of unity $\zeta \in \mathbb Z_7^\times \subset \mathbb Q_7^\times$ reducing to $2 \in \mathbb F_7^\times$. Because $\zeta \in K^\times$, $G = \operatorname{Gal}(K^{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ acts trivially on $\mu_3$, and $a \mapsto \zeta^a$ identifies $R \xrightarrow{\sim} \mu_3$ as $G$-modules. Let $I \triangleleft G$ be the inertia subgroup and $\phi \in G/I$ the arithmetic Frobenius ($x \mapsto x^7$ on $\overline{\mathbb F}_7$).

1. **Kummer cube classes versus cyclic characters ($M = R$ trivial, $M^D = \operatorname{Hom}_R(R, \mu_3) \simeq \mu_3$).** Every element of $K^\times$ factors uniquely as $7^v \cdot \omega(c) \cdot u$ with $v \in \mathbb Z$, Teichmüller representative $\omega(c) \in \mu_6(\mathbb Z_7)$ ($c \in \mathbb F_7^\times$), and principal unit $u \in 1 + 7\mathbb Z_7$. Because $f(X) = X^3 - u$ satisfies $f(1) \equiv 0 \pmod 7$ and $f'(1) = 3 \not\equiv 0 \pmod 7$, Hensel's lemma makes $1 + 7\mathbb Z_7 \subset (K^\times)^3$. In $\mathbb F_7^\times$, $3$ is a primitive root ($3^1 \equiv 3$, $3^2 \equiv 2$, $3^3 \equiv 6 \pmod 7$) and $(\mathbb F_7^\times)^3 = \{\pm 1\}$, while $\operatorname{ord}_7$ separates $7$ from $\mathbb Z_7^\times$. Hence $K^\times/(K^\times)^3 \simeq H^1(K, \mu_3)$ has $R$-basis $([3], [7])$, represented by Kummer cocycles $\kappa_3, \kappa_7 : G \to R$ with $g(t_a)/t_a = \zeta^{\kappa_a(g)}$ for $t_a^3 = a$.

   Let $\chi : G \to R$ be the normalized unramified character ($\chi|_I = 0$, $\chi(\phi) = 1$). For $t_3^3 = 3$, the extension $K(t_3)/K$ is unramified because $X^3 - 3$ is separable over $\mathbb F_7$; in $\overline{\mathbb F}_7^\times$, arithmetic Frobenius acts by
   $$\frac{\phi(\bar t_3)}{\bar t_3} = \bar t_3^{7 - 1} = (\bar t_3^3)^2 = 3^2 = 9 \equiv 2 \equiv \bar\zeta \pmod 7.$$
   Since reduction modulo $7$ is injective on $\mu_3$, we obtain $\phi(t_3)/t_3 = \zeta^1$, so $\kappa_3 = \chi$ in root coordinates. Next, $K(t_7)/K$ is totally and tamely ramified of degree $3$ and Galois (as $\zeta \in K$), so $\kappa_7|_I : I \to R$ is surjective. Pick a tame inertia element $\tau \in I$ with $\kappa_7(\tau) = 1$, and adjust our Frobenius lift $\phi \in G$ by a power of $\tau$ so that $\kappa_7(\phi) = 0$ (if an initial lift $\phi_0$ has $\kappa_7(\phi_0) = c$, replace $\phi_0$ by $\phi_0 \tau^{-c}$). Put $\psi = \kappa_7 \in H^1(K, R)$. Then $(\chi, \psi)$ is an $R$-basis of $H^1(K, R) = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, R)$, and $(\kappa_3, \kappa_7)$ is an $R$-basis of $H^1(K, \mu_3)$. Since $I$ acts trivially on both $R$ and $\mu_3$, coboundaries on $I$ vanish, and restriction to $I$ is detected by evaluation at $\tau$:
   $$U(R) = \ker(\operatorname{res}_I) = R\chi, \qquad H^1(K, R)/U(R) = R\overline\psi, \qquad U(\mu_3) = \ker(\operatorname{res}_I) = R\kappa_3, \qquad H^1(K, \mu_3)/U(\mu_3) = R\overline{\kappa_7}.$$

   Write $C(y, x)(g, h) = y(g)\,(g \cdot x(h))$ for the dual-first cup product into $H^2(K, \mu_3)$. In root coordinates ($\mu_3 \simeq R$ trivially), $C(\alpha, \beta)(g, h) = \alpha(g)\beta(h)$ for characters $\alpha, \beta : G \to R$. For any character $\alpha : G \to R$, the $1$-cochain $c(g) = \alpha(g)^2$ has differential $(d_1 c)(g, h) = \alpha(h)^2 - (\alpha(g) + \alpha(h))^2 + \alpha(g)^2 = -2\alpha(g)\alpha(h) = \alpha(g)\alpha(h)$ in $\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z$ (since $-2 \equiv 1 \pmod 3$), so $C(\kappa_3, \chi) = \chi \cup \chi = 0$ and $C(\kappa_7, \psi) = \psi \cup \psi = 0$ in $H^2(K, \mu_3)$. Off the diagonal, Lemma 18.3.6a below gives $\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_7}(\kappa_7 \cup \chi) = -\operatorname{ord}_7(7)/3 = -1/3$ and $\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_7}(\chi \cup \kappa_7) = +\operatorname{ord}_7(7)/3 = 1/3$ in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, while the archive's normalized invariant $j_v : H^2(K, \mu_3) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$ sends $[\kappa_7 \cup \chi] \mapsto 1$ and hence equals $-\nu \circ \operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_7}$ under $\nu(a/3 \bmod 1) = a \bmod 3$. In row order $(\kappa_3, \kappa_7)$ and column order $(\chi, \psi)$, the pairing matrices are
   $$\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_7} \circ C = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1/3 \\ -1/3 & 0 \end{pmatrix} \text{ in } \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z, \qquad B_v = j_v \circ C = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & -1 \\ 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 2 \\ 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix} \text{ in } R = \mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z.$$
   Row $1$ shows $U(\mu_3)^\perp = R\chi = U(R)$, column $1$ shows $U(R)^\perp = R\kappa_3 = U(\mu_3)$, and the induced pairings $B_v(\kappa_3, \overline\psi) = -1$ and $B_v(\overline{\kappa_7}, \chi) = 1$ are non-degenerate.

2. **Rank-two module with non-trivial unramified Frobenius ($M = R e_1 \oplus R e_2$ over $\mathbb Q_7$).** Over the same $K = \mathbb Q_7$ and $R = \mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z$, let $M = R e_1 \oplus R e_2$ carry trivial inertia action and unipotent order-$3$ arithmetic Frobenius
   $$A = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 1 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad A e_1 = e_1, \quad A e_2 = e_2 + e_1, \qquad A^{-1} e_1 = e_1, \quad A^{-1} e_2 = e_2 - e_1.$$
   In the dual basis $(e_1^\vee, e_2^\vee)$ of $M^D = \operatorname{Hom}_R(M, \mu_3) \simeq \operatorname{Hom}_R(M, R)$ (via $\zeta$, since $q = 7 \equiv 1 \pmod 3$), the contragredient action $A^D f = f \circ A^{-1}$ satisfies $A^D e_1^\vee = e_1^\vee - e_2^\vee$ and $A^D e_2^\vee = e_2^\vee$. Consequently,
   $$H^0(K, M) = \ker(A - 1) = R e_1, \quad (A - 1)M = R e_1, \quad U(M) \simeq M/(A - 1)M = R\overline{e}_2,$$
   $$H^0(K, M^D) = \ker(A^D - 1) = R e_2^\vee, \quad (A^D - 1)M^D = R e_2^\vee, \quad U(M^D) \simeq M^D/(A^D - 1)M^D = R\overline{e}_1^\vee.$$
   Notice how the invariant and coinvariant lines swap roles between $M$ and $M^D$: on $M$, $e_1$ spans both $H^0(K, M)$ and the Frobenius coboundary image $(A - 1)M$, while $\overline{e}_2$ generates $U(M)$; on $M^D$, $e_2^\vee$ spans $H^0(K, M^D)$ and $(A^D - 1)M^D$, while $\overline{e}_1^\vee$ generates $U(M^D)$.

   Because $(A - 1)^2 = 0$ and $(A^D - 1)^2 = 0$, the geometric sums $S_A(k) e_2 = \sum_{i=0}^{k-1} A^i e_2 = k e_2 + \binom{k}{2} e_1$ and $S_{A^D}(k) e_1^\vee = k e_1^\vee - \binom{k}{2} e_2^\vee$ vanish at $k = 3$ in characteristic $3$, so lifting $\chi(g) \in R$ to $s(g) \in \{0, 1, 2\}$ defines inflated unramified $1$-cocycles $x_u(g) = S_A(s(g)) e_2 \in Z^1(G, M)$ ($x_u(\phi) = e_2$) and $y_u(g) = S_{A^D}(s(g)) e_1^\vee \in Z^1(G, M^D)$ ($y_u(\phi) = e_1^\vee$). Because $e_1 \in H^0(K, M)$ and $e_2^\vee \in H^0(K, M^D)$ are $G$-fixed, $x_r(g) = \psi(g) e_1$ and $y_r(g) = \psi(g) e_2^\vee$ are ramified $1$-cocycles with $x_r(\tau) = e_1$ and $y_r(\tau) = e_2^\vee$. If $a[x_u] + b[x_r] = 0$ in $H^1(K, M)$, restricting to $I$ (where $B^1(I, M) = 0$ and $x_u|_I = 0$) forces $b e_1 = 0$, so $b = 0$, and then $a\overline{e}_2 = 0$ in $M/(A - 1)M$ forces $a = 0$. Under the inputs of Theorem 18.3.6 below, $|H^2(K, M)| = |H^0(K, M^D)| = 3$ and $|H^1(K, M)| = |H^0(K, M)|\,|H^2(K, M)| = 9 = |H^1(K, M^D)|$, so $([x_u], [x_r])$ and $([y_u], [y_r])$ are $R$-bases with
   $$U(M) = R[x_u], \quad H^1(K, M)/U(M) = R\overline{[x_r]}, \quad U(M^D) = R[y_u], \quad H^1(K, M^D)/U(M^D) = R\overline{[y_r]}.$$

   We compute all four evaluated cups $C(y, x)(g, h) = y(g)(g \cdot x(h))$:
   - *$(y_u, x_u)$:* On the cyclic unramified powers $\phi^k$, Lemma 18.3.6b's cochain sum $T(k) = \sum_{i=0}^{k-1} y_u(\phi^i)(A^i e_2)$ takes values $T(0) = 0$, $T(1) = 0$, $T(2) = e_1^\vee(e_2 + e_1) = 1$, and $T(3) = 1 + (2e_1^\vee - e_2^\vee)(e_2 + 2e_1) = 4 \equiv 1 \not\equiv 0 \pmod 3$, before reaching $T(9) = 3 T(3) = 0$ in $R$. Thus $-T$ becomes periodic on the degree-$9$ unramified quotient $\mathbb Z/9\mathbb Z$ (demonstrating concretely why Lemma 18.3.6b multiplies the cocycle period by a second factor of $N$), killing $[C(y_u, x_u)] = 0$ in $H^2(K, \mu_3)$.
   - *$(y_u, x_r)$:* Since $g \cdot e_1 = e_1$ and $e_2^\vee(e_1) = 0$, we have $C(y_u, x_r)(g, h) = y_u(g)(\psi(h) e_1) = y_u(g)(e_1)\,\psi(h) = \chi(g)\,\kappa_7(h) = (\chi \cup \kappa_7)(g, h)$, pairing the coinvariant class $y_u$ against the fixed vector $e_1$. Thus $\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_7}(C(y_u, x_r)) = 1/3$ and $B_v([y_u], [x_r]) = -1$.
   - *$(y_r, x_u)$:* Since $e_2^\vee$ is $G$-invariant ($e_2^\vee(g \cdot m) = e_2^\vee(m)$) and $e_2^\vee(e_1) = 0$, we have $C(y_r, x_u)(g, h) = \psi(g)\,e_2^\vee(x_u(h)) = \kappa_7(g)\,\chi(h) = (\kappa_7 \cup \chi)(g, h)$, giving $\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_7}(C(y_r, x_u)) = -1/3$ and $B_v([y_r], [x_u]) = 1$.
   - *$(y_r, x_r)$:* $C(y_r, x_r)(g, h) = \psi(g)\psi(h)\,e_2^\vee(e_1) = 0$ identically as a $2$-cochain.

   In row basis $([y_u], [y_r])$ and column basis $([x_u], [x_r])$, the classical and archive matrices are therefore again $\begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1/3 \\ -1/3 & 0 \end{pmatrix}$ and $\begin{pmatrix} 0 & -1 \\ 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix}$, confirming $U(M)^\perp = U(M^D)$ and $U(M^D)^\perp = U(M)$.

**Definitions (Tate dual, Frobenius directions, evaluated cup product, and local invariants).** Let $\ell$ be a prime and let $K = F_v$ be the completion of a number field $F$ at a finite place $v$ of residue cardinality $q = \operatorname{normV}(v)$ and residue characteristic $p \ne \ell$ (`((l : ℕ) : 𝓞 F) ∉ v.asIdeal`). Write $G = \operatorname{Gal}(K^{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ (represented in the archive by the decomposition subgroup `Dec (F := F) A` of a valuation subring `A` of $\Omega$ above $v$), $I \triangleleft G$ for inertia (`Ine (F := F) A`), and $\Gamma = G/I \simeq \widehat{\mathbb Z}$. Fix $n \ge 1$ (written `r` with `0 < r` in Lean), $N = \ell^n$, and $R = \mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$ (`ZMod (l ^ r)`). Let $M$ be a finite discrete $R$-module with continuous $G$-action `rho` that is unramified (`∀ σ ∈ Ine A, rho σ = 1`). [Lean, read]

1. **Tate dual and unramified cyclotomic twist.** The local Tate dual of $M$ is $M^D = \operatorname{Hom}_R(M, \mu_N)$ (the restriction of §18.3.5's global $M'$ to $G$), with natural Galois action $(g \cdot f)(m) = g(f(g^{-1} m))$. Choosing a primitive $N$-th root of unity $\zeta$ (`muEquiv` in `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/LocalInvariantCupUniformizer.lean:20–53`) identifies $\mu_N$ additively with $R$ carrying the cyclotomic action `muLoc` ($g \cdot a = \chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g) a$, where $g(\zeta) = \zeta^{\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)}$). In these coordinates, $M^D$ is `twDual χ rho` on `TDual R M = Module.Dual R M` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/Hypotheses/GalCohDefs.lean:31–69`), with
   $$(g \cdot \varphi)(m) = \chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\,\varphi(g^{-1} m).$$
   Because $p \nmid N$, $X^N - 1$ is separable over the residue field, so Hensel lifting makes $\mu_N$ unramified (`cycZ_of_mem_Ine` in `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/GlobalProofs/ContinuousCompOfIsOpenKer.lean:563–568`). Hence $I$ acts trivially on both $\mu_N$ and $M^D$. [Lean, read]

2. **Arithmetic versus geometric Frobenius.** In `src/Formalism/ConjugateSelfDual/Statement.lean:28–29`, `IsFrob A q σ` specifies **arithmetic Frobenius** $\phi$: $\sigma \in \operatorname{Dec}(A)$ and $|\sigma(x) - x^q|_A < 1$ whenever $|x|_A \le 1$. Because reduction modulo the maximal ideal is injective on $\mu_N$, $\phi(\zeta) = \zeta^q$, so $\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\phi) \equiv q \pmod N$. If $\phi$ acts on $M$ by $A \in \operatorname{Aut}_R(M)$, then on $M^D$ (in $\zeta$-coordinates) $\phi$ acts by $A^D(\varphi) = q\,\varphi \circ A^{-1}$, whereas **geometric Frobenius** $\phi^{-1}$ acts on $M$ by $A^{-1}$ and on $M^D$ by $(A^D)^{-1}(\varphi) = q^{-1}\,\varphi \circ A$. An unramified additive character $\chi : G \to R$ normalized by $\chi(\phi) = 1$ satisfies $\chi(\phi^{-1}) = -1$. More generally, for any unramified $1$-cocycle $z \in Z^1(G, M)$, the cocycle identity $0 = z(1) = z(\phi \phi^{-1}) = z(\phi) + A z(\phi^{-1})$ gives $z(\phi^{-1}) = -A^{-1} z(\phi)$. Since $\ker(A^{-1} - 1) = \ker(A - 1)$ and $(A^{-1} - 1)M = (1 - A)A^{-1} M = (A - 1)M$, both $H^0(K, M)$ and $U(M)$ are unchanged by inverting the Frobenius generator, while the evaluation isomorphism $U(M) \xrightarrow{\sim} M/(A - 1)M$ is multiplied by the unit $-A^{-1}$. [Lean, read] [Expository]

3. **Continuous cochains and dual-first cup product.** In `src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/SeparatedDvrSecondCohomologyMem.lean:38–103`, `Z1 rho` consists of continuous maps $z : G \to M$ satisfying $z(gh) = z(g) + g z(h)$ modulo coboundaries $(d_0 m)(g) = g m - m$, and `Z2 rho` consists of continuous maps $f : G \times G \to M$ satisfying $g f(h, k) - f(gh, k) + f(g, hk) - f(g, h) = 0$ modulo continuous coboundaries $(d_1 c)(g, h) = g c(h) - c(gh) + c(g)$. Writing $b(\varphi, m) = \varphi(m)$ (`evalPair.flip`, equivariant by `evalPair_equiv`, `src/ReciprocityTheory/Hypotheses/GalCohDefs.lean:70–82`), `cupCocycleZ1Z1` and `cupH1H1TwistedDual` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/ContinuousConstSMulQuotient.lean:30–81`, `GalCohDefs.lean:108–112`) define the dual-first cup product on $y \in Z^1(G, M^D)$ and $x \in Z^1(G, M)$ by
   $$C(y, x)(g, h) = b(y(g), g x(h)) = y(g)(g x(h)).$$
   Using $g b(y(h), h x(k)) = b(g y(h), gh x(k))$ and $y(gh) = y(g) + g y(h)$, we find $g C(y, x)(h, k) - C(y, x)(gh, k) = -b(y(g), gh x(k))$, while $x(hk) = x(h) + h x(k)$ gives $C(y, x)(g, hk) - C(y, x)(g, h) = +b(y(g), gh x(k))$, so $C(y, x) \in Z^2(G, \mu_N)$. Moreover, if $y = d_0 \varphi$ and $c_1(h) = b(\varphi, x(h))$, then
   $$(d_1 c_1)(g, h) = b(g\varphi, g x(h)) - b(\varphi, x(g) + g x(h)) + b(\varphi, x(g)) = b(g\varphi - \varphi, g x(h)) = C(d_0 \varphi, x)(g, h);$$
   if $x = d_0 m$ and $c_2(g) = -b(y(g), g m)$, then
   $$(d_1 c_2)(g, h) = -b(g y(h), gh m) + b(y(g) + g y(h), gh m) - b(y(g), g m) = b(y(g), g(h m - m)) = C(y, d_0 m)(g, h).$$
   Thus $C$ descends bilinearly to $H^1(K, M^D) \times H^1(K, M) \to H^2(K, \mu_N)$. [Lean, read]

4. **Classical and archive local invariants.** Classically, the Kummer sequence $1 \to \mu_N \to (K^{\mathrm{sep}})^\times \xrightarrow{(\cdot)^N} (K^{\mathrm{sep}})^\times \to 1$ and Hilbert's Theorem 90 ($H^1(K, (K^{\mathrm{sep}})^\times) = 0$) identify $H^2(K, \mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Br}(K)[N]$, and local class field theory supplies $\operatorname{inv}_K : \operatorname{Br}(K) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, defined on classes inflated from $K^{\mathrm{un}}/K$ by applying $\operatorname{ord}_K : H^2(\Gamma, (K^{\mathrm{un}})^\times) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2(\Gamma, \mathbb Z)$, inverting the Bockstein connecting map $\delta : H^1(\Gamma, \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2(\Gamma, \mathbb Z)$ of $0 \to \mathbb Z \to \mathbb Q \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z \to 0$, and evaluating at arithmetic Frobenius $\phi$ [Paper; MilneCFT, III Thms. 1.7 & 2.1, pp. 99–100, 103, and Lem. 3.7, p. 110]. Inside the archive (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:28–37,91–94`), `IsNormalised ζ A v inv` requires `inv [z] = 1` in $R$ whenever $\varpi \in \mathfrak p_v \setminus \mathfrak p_v^2$ is a uniformizer, $y^N = \varpi$, $g(y) = \zeta^{\kappa(g)} y$, $\chi : \operatorname{Dec}(A) \to R$ is an unramified homomorphism with $\chi(\phi) = 1$ on `IsFrob`, and $z(g, h) = \kappa(g)\chi(h)$. Transporting along the identity-induced isomorphism `toMuLoc` gives `j v = invAt ... v`, and the local pairing in `tr1` is $B_v(y, x) = j_v(C(y, x))$. [Lean, read]

**Lemma 18.3.6a (Sign of the Kummer–unramified cup and unit-rescaled invariant producer).**
1. *(Classical sign versus archive normalization.)* [Expository] For any $a \in K^\times$, choose $t \in K^{\mathrm{sep}}$ with $t^N = a$, define $\kappa_a : G \to R$ by $g(t)/t = \zeta^{\kappa_a(g)}$, and let $\chi : G \to R$ be the unramified homomorphism with $\chi(\phi) = 1$. Then in $(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[N]$,
   $$\operatorname{inv}_K(\kappa_a \cup \chi) = -\frac{\operatorname{ord}_K(a)}{N} \pmod 1, \qquad \operatorname{inv}_K(\chi \cup \kappa_a) = +\frac{\operatorname{ord}_K(a)}{N} \pmod 1.$$
   Writing $\nu : (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[N] \xrightarrow{\sim} R$ for $c/N \bmod 1 \mapsto c \bmod N$, any linear bijection $j_v : H^2(K, \mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$ satisfying `IsNormalised` agrees in $\zeta$-coordinates with $-\nu \circ \operatorname{inv}_K$.
2. *(Archive invariant producer, `exists_bijective_isNormalised` and `exists_invFam_normalised`.)* [Lean, read] Assume:
   - `local_brauer_roots_unity_equiv`: there exists an $R$-linear isomorphism $e : H^2(\mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$;
   - `local_invariant_cup_uniformizer` (`span_class_eq_top`): for every uniformizer test cocycle $z(g, h) = \kappa_\varpi(g)\chi(h)$, its cohomology class $c = [z]$ spans $H^2(\mu_N)$ over $R$;
   - `local_invariant_cup_kummer` (`cup_class_eq`): any two uniformizer test cocycles $z, z'$ at $(A, v)$ define the same cohomology class $[z] = [z']$ in $H^2(\mu_N)$.

   Then there exists an $R$-linear bijection $f : H^2(\mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$ satisfying `IsNormalised ζ A v f`, and selecting such a map at the unique place $v$ below $A$ whenever one exists (and $0$ otherwise) produces a globally indexed family `inv : InvFam F Ω l r` that is bijective and normalized at every `(A, v)` with `IsPlaceOver A v`.

**Proof.**
(1) Lift $\chi(g) \in R$ to its representative $s(g) \in \{0, \dots, N - 1\}$, so $\hat\chi(g) := s(g)/N \bmod 1$ is the character in $H^1(\Gamma, \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)$ with $\hat\chi(\phi) = 1/N \bmod 1$, and its Bockstein cocycle in $Z^2(\Gamma, \mathbb Z)$ is $(\delta\hat\chi)(g, h) = (s(h) - s(gh) + s(g))/N$. Consider the continuous $1$-cochain $u : G \to (K^{\mathrm{sep}})^\times$ given by $u(g) = t^{s(g)}$. Its multiplicative coboundary is
$$(d_1 u)(g, h) = \frac{g(t)^{s(h)} \, t^{s(g)}}{t^{s(gh)}} = \left(\frac{g(t)}{t}\right)^{s(h)} t^{s(g) + s(h) - s(gh)} = \zeta^{\kappa_a(g)\chi(h)} \cdot a^{(\delta\hat\chi)(g, h)}.$$
Passing to cohomology in $\operatorname{Br}(K) = H^2(K, (K^{\mathrm{sep}})^\times)$ yields $[\zeta^{\kappa_a \cup \chi}] = -[a^{\delta\hat\chi}]$. By Milne's valuation formula [Paper; MilneCFT, III Prop. 3.6 & Lem. 3.7, pp. 109–110], $\operatorname{ord}_K(a^{\delta\hat\chi}) = \delta(\operatorname{ord}_K(a)\hat\chi)$, whose preimage under $\delta$ evaluates at arithmetic Frobenius $\phi$ to $\operatorname{ord}_K(a)\hat\chi(\phi) = \operatorname{ord}_K(a)/N \bmod 1$. Hence $\operatorname{inv}_K(\kappa_a \cup \chi) = -\operatorname{ord}_K(a)/N \bmod 1$.

For the reversed cup $(\chi \cup \kappa_a)(g, h) = \chi(g)\,(\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\kappa_a(h))$ in additive root coordinates on $\mu_N$, take the $1$-cochain $c(g) = \chi(g)\kappa_a(g) \in \mu_N$. Using $\chi(gh) = \chi(g) + \chi(h)$ and $\kappa_a(gh) = \kappa_a(g) + \chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\kappa_a(h)$, its differential expands as
$$(d_1 c)(g, h) = \chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\chi(h)\kappa_a(h) - (\chi(g) + \chi(h))(\kappa_a(g) + \chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\kappa_a(h)) + \chi(g)\kappa_a(g) = -\kappa_a(g)\chi(h) - \chi(g)\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\kappa_a(h),$$
so $d_1 c = -\kappa_a \cup \chi - \chi \cup \kappa_a$ and $\operatorname{inv}_K(\chi \cup \kappa_a) = -\operatorname{inv}_K(\kappa_a \cup \chi) = +\operatorname{ord}_K(a)/N \bmod 1$. When $a = \varpi$ is a uniformizer ($\operatorname{ord}_K(\varpi) = 1$), $\nu(\operatorname{inv}_K(\kappa_\varpi \cup \chi)) = -1 \in R$, whereas `IsNormalised` requires $j_v([\kappa_\varpi \cup \chi]) = +1 \in R$. Since $[\kappa_\varpi \cup \chi]$ has order $N$ and generates $H^2(K, \mu_N)$ over $R$, $R$-linearity forces $j_v = -\nu \circ \operatorname{inv}_K$ (equivalently, evaluating Milne's valuation character at geometric Frobenius $\phi^{-1}$ would reverse $\operatorname{inv}_K$ and match $j_v$ directly; the archive instead keeps arithmetic Frobenius in `IsFrob` and normalizes by the Kummer-first test $\kappa_\varpi \cup \chi$).

(2) Following `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/NormalisedLocalInvariantExists.lean:17–49` [Lean, read], let $e : H^2(\mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$ be given by `local_brauer_roots_unity_equiv`. If $e$ already satisfies `IsNormalised ζ A v e`, take $f = e$. Otherwise, negating the universal quantifiers of `IsNormalised` (`:21–22`) produces a witness $(\varpi, y, \kappa, \chi, z)$ satisfying all eight test premises with $e(c) \ne 1$, where $c = [z] \in H^2(\mu_N)$. By `local_invariant_cup_uniformizer`, $R \cdot c = H^2(\mu_N)$, so $e^{-1}(1) = u \cdot c$ for some $u \in R$, and applying $e$ gives $u \cdot e(c) = 1$ (`:26–33`). Set $f = u \cdot e : H^2(\mu_N) \to_R R$. Because $u$ is a two-sided unit with inverse $e(c)$, $u \cdot e(x) = u \cdot e(x')$ implies $e(x) = e(c)(u e(x)) = e(c)(u e(x')) = e(x')$, giving injectivity (`:35–40`), and $f(e^{-1}(e(c) t)) = u(e(c) t) = t$, giving surjectivity (`:41–43`). For any other valid test tuple $(\varpi', y', \kappa', \chi', z')$, `local_invariant_cup_kummer` gives $[z'] = [z] = c$, so $f([z']) = u \cdot e(c) = 1$ (`:44–48`). Finally, in `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/ExistsInvFamNormalised.lean:17–29` [Lean, read], if $\exists v, \operatorname{IsPlaceOver} A v$ holds, `Classical.choose` picks a place $v_0$ and a normalized bijection $f_{A, v_0}$; for any $v$ with $\operatorname{IsPlaceOver} A v$, `eq_of_isPlaceOver` proves $v_0 = v$ (`:23`), rewriting the chosen specification to $v$. $\square$

**Lemma 18.3.6b (Unramified degree-one cohomology and cyclic-quotient cup coboundary).** [Lean, read] Let $W$ be any finite discrete continuous $R$-module on which $I$ acts trivially and arithmetic Frobenius $\phi \in G$ acts by $D \in \operatorname{Aut}_R(W)$, and assume that $\langle \phi \rangle \cdot I$ is dense in $G$ and that for every $m \ge 1$ there exists a continuous homomorphism $\operatorname{deg}_m : G \to \mathbb Z/m\mathbb Z$ with open kernel vanishing on $I$ and sending $\phi \mapsto 1 \bmod m$. Set $U(W) = \ker[H^1(K, W) \to H^1(I, W)]$ (`H1ur`). Then:
1. A class $[z] \in H^1(K, W)$ belongs to $U(W)$ if and only if $z(\sigma) = 0$ for all $\sigma \in I$, and evaluation $z \mapsto z(\phi) \bmod (D - 1)W$ induces an $R$-linear isomorphism
   $$U(W) \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} W/(D - 1)W, \qquad H^0(K, W) = \ker(D - 1), \qquad |U(W)| = |H^0(K, W)|.$$
2. Under the dual-first cup product $C : H^1(K, M^D) \times H^1(K, M) \to H^2(K, \mu_N)$, $C(y, x) = 0$ for all $y \in U(M^D)$ and $x \in U(M)$.

**Proof.** We transcribe `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/GlobalProofs/ContinuousCompOfIsOpenKer.lean:17–422` [Lean, read].

(1) Because $I$ acts trivially on $W$, every coboundary $(d_0 w)(\sigma) = \sigma w - w$ vanishes on $I$. Thus $[z] \in U(W)$ means $z|_I \in B^1(I, W) = \{0\}$, i.e. $z(\sigma) = 0$ for all $\sigma \in I$ (`mk_mem_H1ur`, `:203–220`). Similarly, for $w \in W$, if $D w = w$, the stabilizer $\{g \in G : g w = w\}$ is a closed subgroup containing $\phi$ and $I$, hence equals $G$ by density of $\langle \phi \rangle \cdot I$, proving $H^0(K, W) = \ker(D - 1)$ (`H0_eq_ker_frobenius_sub_one`, `:238–267`). Applying $|W| = |\operatorname{im}(D - 1)|\,|W/\operatorname{im}(D - 1)| = |\operatorname{im}(D - 1)|\,|\ker(D - 1)|$ and cancelling $|\operatorname{im}(D - 1)| > 0$ gives $|W/(D - 1)W| = |\ker(D - 1)|$ (`card_quot_range_eq_card_ker`, `:228–236`).

For any coboundary $z = d_0 w$, $z(\phi) = (D - 1)w$, so evaluation at $\phi$ descends to an $R$-linear map $\operatorname{eval}_\phi : H^1(K, W) \to W/(D - 1)W$ (`evalH`, `:222–226`). If $[z] \in U(W)$ has $z(\phi) = (D - 1)w$, then $z - d_0 w \in Z^1(G, W)$ vanishes at $\phi$ and on $I$; its zero locus $\{g \in G : (z - d_0 w)(g) = 0\}$ is an open-and-closed subgroup of $G$ (by continuity into discrete $W$ and the cocycle law) containing $\phi$ and $I$, hence equals $G$ (`cocycle_eq_zero`, `:83–104`), so $z = d_0 w$ and $\operatorname{eval}_\phi|_{U(W)}$ is injective (`:282–302`).

For surjectivity, since $W$ is finite, $\operatorname{End}_R(W)$ is finite, so $D^{d_W} = 1$ for some $d_W \ge 1$ (`exists_pow_eq_one`, `:183–190`). Put $M_0 = N d_W \ge 1$. For $w \in W$ and $k \in \mathbb N$, define the geometric sum $S_D(k) w = \sum_{i=0}^{k-1} D^i w$ (`geo`, `:17`). It satisfies $S_D(a + b) w = S_D(a) w + D^a S_D(b) w$ (`geo_add`, `:25–27`). Iterating at $d_W$ gives $S_D(M_0) w = S_D(N d_W) w = N \cdot S_D(d_W) w = 0$ because $N W = 0$ (`geo_mul`, `geo_eq_zero`, `:29–36,191–193`), so $k \mapsto S_D(k) w$ has period $M_0$ (`geo_periodic`, `:37–39`). Choose $\operatorname{deg}_{M_0} : G \to \mathbb Z/M_0\mathbb Z$ and lift its values to $\{0, \dots, M_0 - 1\} \subset \mathbb N$. Because $D^{M_0} = 1$ and $I$ acts trivially, the closed subgroup $\{g \in G : \rho(g) = D^{\operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(g)}\}$ contains $\phi$ and $I$, so $\rho(g) = D^{\operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(g)}$ for all $g \in G$ (`rho_eq_pow`, `:106–150`). Therefore $z_w(g) := S_D(\operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(g)) w$ is locally constant (hence continuous) and satisfies
$$z_w(gh) = S_D(\operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(g) + \operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(h)) w = z_w(g) + D^{\operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(g)} z_w(h) = z_w(g) + g z_w(h)$$
(`geometric_sum_mem_cocycles`, `:152–161`), with $z_w|_I = 0$ and $z_w(\phi) = S_D(1) w = w$ (`:303–310`). Thus $\operatorname{eval}_\phi : U(W) \xrightarrow{\sim} W/(D - 1)W$ is bijective and $|U(W)| = |H^0(K, W)|$ (`card_H1ur`, `:269–310`). Moreover, any $z \in Z^1(G, W)$ with $z|_I = 0$ satisfies $z(g) = S_D(\operatorname{deg}_{M_0}(g)) z(\phi)$ (`eq_geo`, `:170–181`).

(2) Let $D_1, D_2, D_0$ be the actions of $\phi$ on $M^D, M, \mu_N$, choose $d = d_1 d_2 d_0 \ge 1$ with $D_1^d = 1$, $D_2^d = 1$, $D_0^d = 1$, and set $m_0 = N d$ and $P = N^2 d$ (`:371–391`). Given $y = [y'] \in U(M^D)$ and $x = [x'] \in U(M)$, put $m_1 = y'(\phi) \in M^D$ and $m_2 = x'(\phi) \in M$, and define the triangular sum (`tri`, `:323–324`)
$$T(k) = \sum_{t=0}^{k-1} b\bigl(S_{D_1}(t) m_1, \, D_2^t m_2\bigr) \in \mu_N \qquad (k \in \mathbb N).$$
By induction on $c$, splitting $\{0, \dots, a + c - 1\}$ at $a$ and applying $S_{D_1}(a + i) m_1 = S_{D_1}(a) m_1 + D_1^a S_{D_1}(i) m_1$ together with Frobenius equivariance $b(D_1^a u, D_2^a v) = D_0^a b(u, v)$ (`b_pow`, `:326–333`) yields the addition law (`tri_add`, `:335–352`):
$$T(a + c) = T(a) + D_0^a T(c) + b\bigl(S_{D_1}(a) m_1, \, D_2^a S_{D_2}(c) m_2\bigr).$$
At $c = m_0$, we have $S_{D_2}(m_0) m_2 = 0$ and $D_0^{m_0} = 1$, so induction on $a$ gives $T(a m_0) = a \cdot T(m_0)$ (`:403–408`), and therefore $T(P) = T(N m_0) = N \cdot T(m_0) = 0$ since $N \mu_N = 0$ (`:409–410`). Setting $c = P$ in the addition law (where $T(P) = 0$ and $S_{D_2}(P) m_2 = 0$) shows $T(a + P) = T(a)$, so $T$ is $P$-periodic (`:411–412`). Choose $\operatorname{deg}_P : G \to \mathbb Z/P\mathbb Z$ and define the continuous $1$-cochain $c(g) = -T(\operatorname{deg}_P(g))$ (`:413–414`). For any $g, h \in G$, writing $a = \operatorname{deg}_P(g)$ and $q = \operatorname{deg}_P(h)$, periodicity modulo $P$ together with $y'(g) = S_{D_1}(a) m_1$, $x'(h) = S_{D_2}(q) m_2$, $\rho_2(g) = D_2^a$, and $\rho_{\mu_N}(g) = D_0^a$ gives
$$(d_1 c)(g, h) = -D_0^a T(q) + T(a + q) - T(a) = b\bigl(S_{D_1}(a) m_1, \, D_2^a S_{D_2}(q) m_2\bigr) = b(y'(g), g x'(h)) = C(y', x')(g, h)$$
(`:415–421`). Thus $C(y, x) = 0$ in $H^2(K, \mu_N)$. $\square$

**Theorem 18.3.6 (Conditional local orthogonality of unramified conditions).** [Lean, read] Keep the hypotheses above ($p \ne \ell$, $M$ finite discrete continuous $R$-module with $N = \ell^n$, $n \ge 1$, and trivial inertia action). Assume the archive's three local cohomology inputs at $(A, v)$:
1. **`LocalFinitenessAt`:** $H^1(K, M)$ and $H^1(K, M^D)$ are finite;
2. **`LocalTateDualityAt`:** there exists an $R$-linear isomorphism $e : H^2(K, \mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$, both $C : H^1(K, M^D) \to_R \operatorname{Hom}_R(H^1(K, M), H^2(K, \mu_N))$ and $C^{\mathrm{flip}} : H^1(K, M) \to_R \operatorname{Hom}_R(H^1(K, M^D), H^2(K, \mu_N))$ are bijective, and degree-$(2, 0)$ cup product $\operatorname{cup}_{2,0} : H^2(K, M) \to_R \operatorname{Hom}_R(H^0(K, M^D), H^2(K, \mu_N))$ is bijective;
3. **`LocalEulerCharAt` away from $\ell$:** since $p \ne \ell$, $|H^1(K, M)| = |H^0(K, M)| \cdot |H^2(K, M)|$.

Then $U(M)$ and $U(M^D)$ are exact mutual annihilators:
$$U(M)^\perp = U(M^D), \qquad U(M^D)^\perp = U(M),$$
where orthogonality may be tested equivalently by $C(y, x) = 0$ in $H^2(K, \mu_N)$, $\operatorname{inv}_K(C(y, x)) = 0$ in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, or $B_v(y, x) = j_v(C(y, x)) = 0$ in $R$. Moreover, $B_v$ induces perfect $R$-bilinear pairings $U(M^D) \times (H^1(K, M)/U(M)) \to R$ and $(H^1(K, M^D)/U(M^D)) \times U(M) \to R$. [Paper; Tate1962, Thm. 2.4, p. 290; Milne2006ADT, I Thms. 2.1, 2.6, 2.8 & Lem. 2.9, pp. 26–32]

**Proof.** We transcribe `annihilators_of` and `unramifiedOrthogonality_of` in `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/GlobalProofs/ContinuousCompOfIsOpenKer.lean:427–576` [Lean, read]. First, `exists_isFrob`, `closure_frob_sup_Ine`, and `exists_degMod` supply the arithmetic Frobenius lift $\phi$, density of $\langle \phi \rangle \cdot I$ in $G$, and the finite cyclic degree maps $\operatorname{deg}_m$, while `cycZ_of_mem_Ine` (since $p \ne \ell$) shows that $I$ acts trivially on $\mu_N$ and hence on $M^D$ (`:511–517,553–568`).

Applying Lemma 18.3.6b(1) to $W = M$ and $W = M^D$ gives $|U(M)| = |H^0(K, M)|$ and $|U(M^D)| = |H^0(K, M^D)|$ (`:518–519`). Post-composing the bijection $\operatorname{cup}_{2,0}$ with $e : H^2(K, \mu_N) \xrightarrow{\sim} R$ identifies $H^2(K, M) \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Hom}_R(H^0(K, M^D), R)$. Because every finite $R = \mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$-module $X$ decomposes into cyclic $R$-summands and thus satisfies $|\operatorname{Hom}_R(X, R)| = |X|$ (`card_dual_eq`), we obtain $|H^2(K, M)| = |H^0(K, M^D)|$ (`:520–523`). Combining these three equalities with the away-from-$\ell$ local Euler identity gives the exact product relation (`:540–541`)
$$|U(M)| \cdot |U(M^D)| = |H^0(K, M)| \cdot |H^0(K, M^D)| = |H^0(K, M)| \cdot |H^2(K, M)| = |H^1(K, M)|.$$

Set $P = H^1(K, M)$, $Q = H^1(K, M^D)$, and $B = e \circ C : Q \to_R \operatorname{Hom}_R(P, R)$. By `bijective_compr₂` (`:482–485`), both $B$ and $B^{\mathrm{flip}} : P \to_R \operatorname{Hom}_R(Q, R)$ are bijective (`:525–527`), so in particular $|Q| = |\operatorname{Hom}_R(P, R)| = |P|$ (`:457–458`). For any $R$-submodule $U \subset P$, a linear form in $\operatorname{Hom}_R(P, R)$ vanishes on $U$ if and only if it factors through $P/U$ (`dualQuotEquivDualAnnihilator`), so its preimage $U^{\perp_B} := B^{-1}(\operatorname{Ann}(U)) \subset Q$ has cardinality
$$|U^{\perp_B}| = |\operatorname{Hom}_R(P/U, R)| = |P/U| = |P| / |U|, \qquad |U| \cdot |U^{\perp_B}| = |P|$$
(`card_mul_card_perp`, `:435–449`). By Lemma 18.3.6b(2), $B(y, x) = e(C(y, x)) = e(0) = 0$ for all $y \in V := U(M^D)$ and $x \in U := U(M)$, so $V \subset U^{\perp_B}$ (`:464–465,528–539`). Comparing $|U| \cdot |U^{\perp_B}| = |P| = |U| \cdot |V|$ and cancelling $|U| > 0$ gives $|U^{\perp_B}| = |V|$, whence the inclusion $V \subset U^{\perp_B}$ of finite submodules is an equality $U(M^D) = U(M)^{\perp_B}$ (`eq_of_le_of_card`, `:427–434,463–466`). Symmetrically, $|V| \cdot |V^{\perp_{B^{\mathrm{flip}}}}| = |Q| = |P| = |V| \cdot |U|$ and $U \subset V^{\perp_{B^{\mathrm{flip}}}}$ force $U(M) = U(M^D)^{\perp_{B^{\mathrm{flip}}}}$ (`exact_annihilators`, `:467–476`).

Finally, because $e$, $\operatorname{inv}_K$, and the normalized archive invariant $j_v$ are all injective on $H^2(K, \mu_N)$, the conditions $e(C(y, x)) = 0$, $C(y, x) = 0$, $\operatorname{inv}_K(C(y, x)) = 0$, and $B_v(y, x) = j_v(C(y, x)) = 0$ are equivalent for every $(y, x)$ (`:542–543`). Replacing $e$ by $j_v$, any class $y \in U(M^D)$ inducing the zero functional on $H^1(K, M)/U(M)$ pairs to zero against all of $H^1(K, M)$, so $y \in \ker(j_v \circ C) = \{0\}$ by bijectivity of $j_v \circ C$; since $|U(M^D)| = |H^1(K, M)/U(M)| = |\operatorname{Hom}_R(H^1(K, M)/U(M), R)|$, this injective map between finite modules of equal cardinality is an isomorphism, and symmetrically for $(H^1(K, M^D)/U(M^D)) \times U(M) \to R$. $\square$

**Remark (Return to the Selmer comparison arrow, cyclotomic residue variations, and scope boundaries).** [Expository]

- **Closing the comparison arrow in §18.3.5.** Let $v \in S$ be any finite place of $F$ with $v \nmid \ell$ at which the global coefficient module $M$ of Proposition 18.3.5 is unramified, and choose $L_v = U(M|_{G_v}) \subset H^1(G_v, M)$. Theorem 18.3.6 identifies the dual local condition `perpCond ... L v` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/Hypotheses/GalCohDefs.lean:113–124`) with the unramified subgroup $L_v^\perp = U(M'|_{G_v}) \subset H^1(G_v, M')$. Consequently, in the seven-term sequence of Proposition 18.3.5, every dual Selmer class $z \in S'$ satisfies $\operatorname{res}_v z \in U(M'|_{G_v})$, so $\overline{x}_v \mapsto B_v(\operatorname{res}_v z, x_v)$ is a well-defined $R$-linear functional on $H^1(G_v, M)/U(M|_{G_v})$ determined uniquely by $\operatorname{res}_v z$ under the perfect quotient pairing. Summing these functionals across $v \in S$ recovers the restriction of `tr1` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:119–142`) to $P_1/L \to (S')^\vee$, while $|L_v| = |U(M|_{G_v})| = |H^0(G_v, M)|$ makes the local factor $|L_v|/|H^0(G_v, M)|$ in the Greenberg–Wiles count equal to $1$ at every such good place.
- **Two invariant conventions united by cup-zero.** Notice how cleanly the archive separates normalization from orthogonality: `Bridge.lean:24` chooses the `IsNormalised` family `inv` ($j_v = -\nu \circ \operatorname{inv}_K$) so that the global sum $\sum_{v \in S} j_v(\cdots)$ in `tr0`, `tr1`, `tr2` cancels on global classes by reciprocity, whereas `UnramifiedOrthogonalityAt` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/Hypotheses/GalCoh.lean:36–52`) uses an arbitrary `inv` from `LocalTateDualityAt` internally for counting and exports the raw cup-zero equivalence `C y x = 0`, matching `perpCond` directly. Switching globally from $j_v$ to $+\nu \circ \operatorname{inv}_K$ multiplies `tr1` by $-1$, leaving `Sel`, `S'`, and the kernel, image, and cardinality of $P_1/L \to (S')^\vee$ unchanged. In `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/EveryCharacterOfLiteratureOfVWin.lean:577–580,626–639`, `literature_cohomology` packs both `uo := unramified_orthogonality_of_literature O` and `gw := greenberg_wiles_of_literature O` side by side into `LitCoh` [Lean, read].
- **When $\mu_N \not\subset K$ ($q \not\equiv 1 \pmod \ell$) and why $\ell$ is odd.** Over $K = \mathbb Q_7$ at $\ell = 3$ we had $q = 7 \equiv 1 \pmod 3$, so $|U(R)| = |U(\mu_3)| = 3$. When $q \not\equiv 1 \pmod \ell$ — for instance $K = \mathbb Q_5$, $\ell = 3$, $N = 3$, where $q = 5 \equiv 2 \pmod 3$ — arithmetic Frobenius acts by $1$ on $M = R$ and by $2$ on $M^D = \mu_3$: Lemma 18.3.6b gives $|U(R)| = |H^0(K, R)| = 3$ and $|U(\mu_3)| = |\ker(2 - 1)| = 1$, while $|H^1(\mathbb Q_5, R)| = |H^0|\,|H^2| = 3 \cdot 1 = 3$, so $U(R) = H^1(\mathbb Q_5, R)$ and $U(\mu_3) = \{0\}$ are still exact annihilators ($3 \cdot 1 = 3$). Likewise, `UnramifiedOrthogonalityAt` itself holds for all primes $\ell \ne p$ including $\ell = 2$; restricting to odd $\ell$ enters only when combining with the odd-prime branch of `GreenbergWilesAt` (§18.3.5) to avoid archimedean higher Tate cohomology at real places (and in Example 1 it also gave $d_1(\alpha^2) = -2\alpha \cup \alpha$ with $2 \in R^\times$).
- **Places above $\ell$ and ramified coefficients.** At $v\mid\ell$ the Euler identity has the factor $|M|^{[K:\mathbb Q_\ell]}$ (`LocalEulerCharAt`, `GalCoh.lean:57–58`), and the root-of-unity action may be ramified. The unramified calculation above does not identify exact annihilators in this setting. For ramified $M$ away from $\ell$, the abstract annihilator $L_v^\perp$ of [§18.3.5](#1835-local-conditions-and-the-selmer-exact-sequence) still defines the dual local condition. The Euler identity away from the residue prime is the subject of [§18.3.7](#1837-local-euler-characteristics-away-from-the-residue-prime).

Local R3 applies to the listed bodies. Independent review accepted the conditional orthogonality and sign comparison.
