Reading the archive's supply of Theorem 18.3.8 is not a check of the Mathlib cone below it. The calculations in the two examples, the decomposition groups in $\mathbb Q(\vartheta,\zeta)$ and the unit-group counts are proved here and are not Lean declarations. The examples use the [Paper] facts cited in them.

### 18.3.11 Frobenius elements at a place: existence, inertia cosets and conjugation

**Motivation.** Two interfaces of Cluster I quantify over Frobenius elements. `ChebotarevDegreeOne` (Definition 18.1(4); `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/ChebotarevDegreeOneIffCheb.lean:12–18`) takes a finite Galois extension $L/F$ inside $\Omega$, an element $g\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ and a finite set $S$ of primes of $F$. It asks for a prime $v\notin S$ of degree one, a valuation ring $B$ of $\Omega$ over $v$ whose inertia group fixes $L$, and a Frobenius element at $B$ whose restriction to $L$ is $g$. The field `frob` of `IsGlobalArtin` matches uniformizers with inverses of Frobenius elements (§18.2.2, item 3). In the archive's proof `chebotarevDegreeOne'`, which reaches the main path through `cheb_holds` ([Chapter 10, §10.4.7](10-congruence-modules-to-sha.md#1047-where-does-adm2-come-from)), the Frobenius element is produced by `exists_isFrob` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/Global.lean:345–347`). That theorem restates `exists_frob_over` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/GlobalB.lean:238–310`).

The classical existence proof works in a finite Galois extension of number fields, whose residue fields are finite [Milne, *ANT*, p. 141, footnote 2]. Here the group is $G_F=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F)$, which is infinite, and the residue field of a valuation ring of $\Omega$ over $v$ is an algebraic closure of $\mathbb F_p$. Mathlib's finite theorem `IsArithFrobAt.exists_of_isInvariant` needs a finite group and a finite residue field, so it does not apply. The archive calls the profinite theorem `Ideal.Quotient.stabilizerHom_surjective_of_profinite` instead. It lifts an automorphism of the residue field through every finite quotient of $G_F$ and chooses the lifts compatibly by a compactness argument. This subsection reads that production chain and the archive's proof around it. It then describes all Frobenius elements at a valuation ring as one coset of inertia, relates the Frobenius elements at different valuation rings over the same place by conjugation, and follows the element into its consumers. The helpers `primeBelow`, `under_primeBelow`, `isInvariant_intCl` and `continuousSMul_intCl` are those of [§18.3.10](#18310-integral-elements-the-prime-below-a-valuation-ring-and-conjugation-over-a-place) and are used as proved there.

Two points need care. A Frobenius element is never unique in $G_F$, because inertia is an infinite subgroup of $G_F$, so every use of one involves a choice. And the archive's Frobenius elements are arithmetic, acting on residues as $x\mapsto x^q$, while its Artin map sends uniformizers to their inverses.

**Setting.** Keep the notation of §18.3.10. $B$ is the ring of algebraic integers of $\Omega$ (`IntCl F Ω`), and $A$ is a valuation ring of $\Omega$, with valuation $v_A$ and maximal ideal $\mathfrak m_A$, lying over a maximal ideal $w$ of $\mathcal O_F$. Its prime below is $\mathfrak p_A=\mathfrak m_A\cap B$. The groups $D_A$ and $I_A$ are the decomposition and inertia groups of §18.3.8 (`Dec` and `Ine`, `Global.lean:15–18,45–64`; the inertia condition is `Inertia`, `src/Formalism/ConjugateSelfDual/Statement.lean:25–26`). Put $q=N(w)=\lvert\mathcal O_F/w\rvert$ (`normV`, `Statement.lean:23`), a power of the residue characteristic $p$. Informally, a Frobenius element at $A$ is an element of $D_A$ that acts on residues of $A$ as the $q$-th power map. For a finite Galois extension $L/F$ inside $\Omega$ put $Q_L=\mathfrak m_A\cap\mathcal O_L$, and let $S_L(A)$ be the set of $\tau\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ with $\tau(Q_L)=Q_L$ and $\tau x\equiv x^q\pmod{Q_L}$ for all $x\in\mathcal O_L$. All three examples take $F=\mathbb Q(i)$, with the three kinds of prime already met: the split prime $(2+i)$ and the inert prime $(3)$ of §18.3.8, and the ramified prime $(1+i)$ of §§18.3.9–18.3.10.

**First example — a split prime and a non-abelian layer.** Let $w=(2+i)$, one of the two primes above $5=(2+i)(2-i)$, so $q=N(w)=5$. As in §18.3.8 choose $\iota\in\mathbb Z_5$ with $\iota^2=-1$ and $\iota\equiv3\pmod5$. As in §18.3.10 choose $r\in\mathbb Z_5$ with $r^3=2$ and $r\equiv3\pmod5$, and a primitive cube root of unity $z$, so that $\mathbb Q_{25}=\mathbb Q_5(z)$ has closed ball $\mathbb Z_5[z]$. Let $\varphi$ be the Frobenius automorphism of $\mathbb Q_{25}/\mathbb Q_5$. It fixes $\mathbb Q_5$, sends $z$ to $z^2$ and preserves absolute values. For $y=a+bz$ with $a,b\in\mathbb Z_5$,
$$\varphi(y)=a+bz^2\equiv a^5+b^5z^5\equiv y^5\pmod{5\,\mathbb Z_5[z]},$$
by Fermat's little theorem and because $5$ divides the binomial coefficients $\binom5j$ for $0<j<5$.

Let $\vartheta^3=2$ and $\zeta^2+\zeta+1=0$ in $\Omega$, and put $L=F(\vartheta,\zeta)$. The field $\mathbb Q(\vartheta,\zeta)$ is Galois over $\mathbb Q$ with group $S_3$, and its only quadratic subfield is $\mathbb Q(\zeta)\ne F$. So $[L:\mathbb Q]=12$, and restriction identifies $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ with $S_3$. It is generated by $\rho\colon\vartheta\mapsto\zeta\vartheta,\ \zeta\mapsto\zeta$ and $\kappa\colon\vartheta\mapsto\vartheta,\ \zeta\mapsto\zeta^2$, both fixing $i$, and $\kappa\rho=\rho^2\kappa$. Counting degrees, $L\simeq\mathbb Q[X,Y,Z]/(X^2+1,\,Y^3-2,\,Z^2+Z+1)$. So for $k=0,1,2$ the assignments $i\mapsto\iota$, $\vartheta\mapsto z^kr$ and $\zeta\mapsto z$ define embeddings $j_k\colon L\to\mathbb Q_{25}$. Extend each to $\Omega\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_5$, and put $A_k=\{x\in\Omega:\lvert j_k(x)\rvert\le1\}$ and $Q_k=\mathfrak m_{A_k}\cap\mathcal O_L$. As $\lvert j_k(2+i)\rvert<1$, every $A_k$ lies over $w$. By Lemma F(1) of §18.3.10, $\lvert j_k(x)\rvert\le1$ for $x\in\mathcal O_L$, so $j_k(\mathcal O_L)\subset\mathbb Z_5[z]$. The maximal ideal of $\mathbb Z_5[z]$ is $5\,\mathbb Z_5[z]$, so reduction gives ring maps $\psi_k\colon\mathcal O_L\to\mathbb F_{25}=\mathbb F_5(\bar z)$ with kernel $Q_k$ and
$$\psi_k(i)=3,\qquad\psi_k(\vartheta)=3\bar z^{k},\qquad\psi_k(\zeta)=\bar z .$$

*The primes over $w$.* The residues of $\vartheta-3$ are $0$, $3\bar z-3$ and $3\bar z^2-3$, so $\vartheta-3$ lies in $Q_0$ only. The element $\zeta\vartheta-3$ lies in $Q_2$ only. So $Q_0,Q_1,Q_2$ are distinct. For $g\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$, $g(Q_k)$ is the set of $x\in\mathcal O_L$ with $\lvert j_k(g^{-1}x)\rvert<1$. A field embedding of $L$ is determined by its values on $i$, $\vartheta$ and $\zeta$. Since $\rho^{-1}\vartheta=\zeta^2\vartheta$, this gives $j_0\circ\rho^{-1}=j_2$ and $j_2\circ\rho^{-1}=j_1$, so $\rho(Q_0)=Q_2$ and $\rho(Q_2)=Q_1$. Also $j_0\circ\kappa=\varphi\circ j_0$, and $\varphi$ preserves absolute values, so $\kappa(Q_0)=Q_0$. Hence the orbit of $Q_0$ is $\{Q_0,Q_1,Q_2\}$. The group $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ acts transitively on the primes over $w$ [Stacks Project, Lemma 15.112.8 (0BRI)](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BRI), so these three are all of them. Each residue field contains $\bar z\notin\mathbb F_5$, so $f(Q_k/w)\ge2$. With three primes, $\sum e_kf_k=[L:F]=6$ forces $f=2$ and $e=1$ [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 3.34, p. 59](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf). So $w$ is unramified in $L$.

*The Frobenius elements.* The automorphism $\varphi$ maps $j_k(L)=\mathbb Q(\iota,r,z)$ onto itself and fixes $j_k(F)=\mathbb Q(\iota)$. So $\tau_k=j_k^{-1}\circ\varphi\circ j_k$ lies in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$. It preserves $Q_k$, because $\varphi$ preserves absolute values, and for $x\in\mathcal O_L$
$$\lvert j_k(\tau_kx)-j_k(x)^5\rvert=\lvert\varphi(j_kx)-(j_kx)^5\rvert<1 .$$
Thus $\tau_k\in S_L(A_k)$. On generators, $j_k(\tau_k\vartheta)=\varphi(z^kr)=z^{2k}r=j_k(\zeta^k\vartheta)$ and $j_k(\tau_k\zeta)=z^2$, so $\tau_k=\rho^k\kappa$. Conversely, let $\tau\in S_L(A_k)$ with $\tau\vartheta=\zeta^a\vartheta$ and $\tau\zeta=\zeta^e$. Then $\psi_k(\tau\zeta)=\bar z^e$ must equal $\bar z^5=\bar z^2$, so $e=2$. And $\psi_k(\tau\vartheta)=3\bar z^{a+k}$ must equal $(3\bar z^k)^5=3\bar z^{2k}$, so $a\equiv k\pmod3$. Hence
$$S_L(A_0)=\{\kappa\},\qquad S_L(A_1)=\{\rho\kappa\},\qquad S_L(A_2)=\{\rho^2\kappa\}.$$
By Lemma J(2) below, every Frobenius element at $A_k$ restricts to $\rho^k\kappa$ on $L$. The three primes above the split prime $w$ have the three transpositions of $S_3$ as Frobenius elements. Changing the prime conjugates them. For instance $\rho(Q_0)=Q_2$ and $\rho\kappa\rho^{-1}=\rho^2\kappa$, as Lemma M(3) predicts. The certificate checks the identity $\operatorname{Frob}(gQ_k)=g\operatorname{Frob}(Q_k)g^{-1}$ for all six $g$ and all three $k$.

*A smaller layer and the consumer.* On $L_1=F(\zeta)$ each $\rho^k\kappa$ restricts to $\zeta\mapsto\zeta^2$. This is the only element of $S_{L_1}(A_k)$, because the identity fails the congruence at $\zeta$: $\bar z\ne\bar z^5$. Restriction maps the singletons to each other, as Lemma J(3) requires. The computation on $\zeta$ is also the smallest case of Corollary 18.3.11.1(1). Since $3\notin w$, the residues $\bar z$ and $\bar z^2$ are distinct, and the congruence $\tau\zeta\equiv\zeta^5$ forces the equality $\tau\zeta=\zeta^5=\zeta^2$. Finally, $N(w)=5$ is prime. By Corollary 18.3.11.1(2) with base field $\mathbb Q$ and $Z=F$, a Frobenius element at $A_k$ for $G_F$ is also one for $G_{\mathbb Q}$ at the same ring. This is the step that `chebotarevDegreeOne'` takes at `ChebotarevDegreeOne.lean:40`. The next example shows why the norm must be prime.

**Second example — residue degree two.** Let $p=3$. Since $X^2+1$ has no root modulo $3$, $w=3\mathcal O_F$ is prime and $\mathcal O_F/w=\mathbb F_9=\mathbb F_3(\bar\imath)$ with $\bar\imath^2=-1$. So $q=N(w)=9$, while the residue characteristic is $3$. Let $L=F(\zeta_5)=\mathbb Q(\zeta_{20})$, where $\zeta_{20}=i\zeta_5^{-1}$, so that $i=\zeta_{20}^5$ and $\zeta_5=\zeta_{20}^4$. Its ring of integers is $\mathbb Z[\zeta_{20}]=\mathcal O_F[\zeta_5]$, and $3$ is unramified in $L$ [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 6.4, p. 99]. The group $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ consists of the maps $\sigma_a\colon\zeta_5\mapsto\zeta_5^a$ for $a\in(\mathbb Z/5)^\times$, all fixing $i$. Over $\mathbb F_9$,
$$\Phi_5(X)=X^4+X^3+X^2+X+1=\bigl(X^2-(1+\bar\imath)X+1\bigr)\bigl(X^2-(1-\bar\imath)X+1\bigr),$$
and neither factor has a root in $\mathbb F_9$, because $9\not\equiv1\pmod5$. The Dedekind–Kummer theorem for $\mathcal O_L=\mathcal O_F[\zeta_5]$ gives $w\mathcal O_L=Q_+Q_-$ with $Q_\pm=(3,\ \zeta_5^2-(1\pm i)\zeta_5+1)$, each of residue degree $2$ over $w$ [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 3.41, pp. 62–63]. Let $\psi_\pm\colon\mathcal O_L\to\mathbb F_{81}$ be reduction modulo $Q_\pm$. It sends $i$ to $\bar\imath$ and $\zeta_5$ to a root $\beta_\pm$ of the corresponding factor. The other root of that factor is $\beta_\pm^{-1}=\beta_\pm^9$.

*The Frobenius element.* Both $x\mapsto\psi_\pm(\tau x)$ and $x\mapsto\psi_\pm(x)^9$ are ring maps $\mathcal O_L\to\mathbb F_{81}$, so $\tau\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ satisfies the congruence modulo $Q_\pm$ for all $x\in\mathcal O_L$ exactly when it does so on the generators $i$ and $\zeta_5$. On $i$ the condition $\bar\imath=\bar\imath^9$ holds for every $\tau$. For $\tau=\sigma_a$, the condition on $\zeta_5$ reads $\beta_\pm^a=\beta_\pm^9=\beta_\pm^4$. As $\beta_\pm$ has order $5$, this means $a=4$. Hence $S_L(A)=\{\sigma_4\}$ at both primes. The Frobenius element is $\zeta_5\mapsto\zeta_5^9=\zeta_5^{-1}$, as in Milne's Example 8.18 with $N(w)$ in place of $p$ [Milne, *ANT*, Example 8.18, p. 143].

*Why the exponent is $N(w)$.* No element of $G_F$ satisfies the congruence with exponent $3$. On $x=i$ it would require $i\equiv i^3=-i$, that is $2i\in\mathfrak m_A$, which is false. The arithmetic Frobenius over $\mathbb Q$ at the same ring acts on $\mathbb Q(\zeta_{20})$ by $\tau_3\colon\zeta_{20}\mapsto\zeta_{20}^3$. It sends $i=\zeta_{20}^5$ to $\zeta_{20}^{15}=-i$, so no lift of $\tau_3$ lies in $G_F$. Its square $\zeta_{20}\mapsto\zeta_{20}^9$ fixes $i$ and sends $\zeta_5=\zeta_{20}^4$ to $\zeta_{20}^{36}=\zeta_5^4$, so $\tau_3^2=\sigma_4$. This is Milne's rule $(Q,M/K)^{f(P/p)}=(Q,M/L)$ with $f(w/3)=2$ [Milne, *ANT*, 8.15, p. 142]. It is why `exists_frob_over` uses the exponent `Nat.card (𝓞 F ⧸ v.asIdeal)` rather than the residue characteristic. It is also why the restriction of scalars in Corollary 18.3.11.1(2) needs a prime norm.

*An infinite unramified layer.* The argument used for $\zeta_5$ works for every root of unity of order prime to $3$. By Corollary 18.3.11.1(1), every Frobenius element at $A$ sends each $\zeta\in\mu_{5^\infty}$ to $\zeta^9$. So on the infinite extension $F(\mu_{5^\infty})$, which is unramified at $w$, all Frobenius elements at $A$ agree. The compatible system of their restrictions is forced: $\zeta_{5^n}\mapsto\zeta_{5^n}^9$ at every level $n$. `cycZ_of_isFrob` records this as the value $9\in\mathbb Z_5^\times$ of the $5$-adic cyclotomic character. At level $5$ it is $9\equiv4$, the value $a=4$ found above. No choice is made on unramified layers.

**Third example — a ramified layer, where the lift is not unique.** Let $w=(1+i)$, the ramified prime of §§18.3.9–18.3.10, so $q=2$. Choose $\zeta_{24}\in\Omega$ with $\zeta_{24}^6=i$, and put $\zeta_3=\zeta_{24}^8$ and $\zeta_8=\zeta_{24}^3$. Let $L=F(\zeta_3,\zeta_8)=\mathbb Q(\zeta_{24})$, with ring of integers $\mathbb Z[\zeta_{24}]$ [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 6.4, p. 99]. Here $\Phi_{24}=X^8-X^4+1\equiv(X^2+X+1)^4\pmod2$. So $2\mathcal O_L=Q^4$ for the single prime $Q=(2,\ \zeta_{24}^2+\zeta_{24}+1)$, whose residue field is $\mathbb F_4$ [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 3.41, pp. 62–63]. Over $F$ this gives $e(Q/w)=2$ and $f(Q/w)=2$, and $Q$ is the only prime over $w$. Reduction $\psi\colon\mathcal O_L\to\mathbb F_4$ sends $\zeta_{24}$ to a root of $X^2+X+1$. Write $\omega=\psi(\zeta_3)$. Then $\psi(\zeta_{24})=\omega^2$, because $\zeta_3=\zeta_{24}^8$, and $\psi(i)=\psi(\zeta_{24}^6)=1$ and $\psi(\zeta_8)=\psi(\zeta_{24}^3)=1$. The group $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ consists of the maps $\sigma_a\colon\zeta_{24}\mapsto\zeta_{24}^a$ with $a\in\{1,5,13,17\}$, the units modulo $24$ that are $1$ modulo $4$, so that $\sigma_a(i)=i$. All four preserve $Q$.

*The Frobenius set.* Since $\mathcal O_L=\mathbb Z[\zeta_{24}]$, $\sigma_a$ lies in $S_L(A)$ exactly when $\psi(\zeta_{24})^a=\psi(\zeta_{24})^2$. This reads $\omega^{2a}=\omega$, that is, $a\equiv2\pmod3$. Similarly $\sigma_a$ acts trivially on residues exactly when $\omega^{2a}=\omega^2$, that is, $a\equiv1\pmod3$. Writing $I_L(A)$ for this inertia group of $Q$ in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$,
$$S_L(A)=\{\sigma_5,\sigma_{17}\}=\sigma_5\,I_L(A),\qquad I_L(A)=\{1,\sigma_{13}\}.$$
Here $\sigma_{13}$ fixes $\zeta_3=\zeta_{24}^8$ and sends $\zeta_8=\zeta_{24}^3$ to $\zeta_{24}^{15}=-\zeta_8$. There are two Frobenius elements at this level, and they differ by inertia, as Lemma J(3) and Lemma M(1) say.

*Restriction to the two subfields.* On the unramified layer $F(\zeta_3)$ both elements restrict to $\zeta_3\mapsto\zeta_3^2$, the only element of $S_{F(\zeta_3)}(A)$. Here $w$ is inert, because $X^2+X+1$ has no root modulo $2$. On the totally ramified layer $F(\zeta_8)=F(\sqrt2)$ they restrict to $\zeta_8\mapsto-\zeta_8$ and to the identity, which are the two elements of $\operatorname{Gal}(F(\zeta_8)/F)$. The residue field there is $\mathbb F_2$, and both are inertia. So restriction from $S_L(A)$ to $S_{F(\zeta_3)}(A)$ is two-to-one, and restriction to $S_{F(\zeta_8)}(A)$ is a bijection. In $G_F$ these fibres keep growing through further ramified layers, and an element of $G_F$ is obtained only by choosing a compatible family. Lemma L below makes this choice by compactness, and `frobOf` (`Global.lean:117–120`) makes one more by `Classical.epsilon`.

*Why the consumer needs $m\notin w$.* For the cube roots of unity, $3\notin w$, and both lifts send $\zeta_3$ to $\zeta_3^2=\zeta_3^q$ exactly. For the eighth roots of unity, $2\in w$. Both lifts satisfy the congruence $\sigma\zeta_8\equiv\zeta_8^2=i\pmod Q$, since all residues involved are $1$. But $\sigma_5\zeta_8=-\zeta_8$ and $\sigma_{17}\zeta_8=\zeta_8$, and neither equals $i$. So the exact formula of Corollary 18.3.11.1(1) fails without the hypothesis $m\notin w$. `chebotarevDegreeOne'` proves that hypothesis at `ChebotarevDegreeOne.lean:44–52` before using the formula.

*A consumer that uses the freedom.* At the residue characteristic the cyclotomic character of a Frobenius element is not determined, and one consumer of `exists_isFrob` exploits this. `UpperTheory.Kd.C7.pFrobEx` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/Foundations/IwahoriReduction/LangPolynomials/FrobEx.lean:23–39`) proves `SFrobEx` (`FixedIntegersIsUnitOfNotMemPrimeSpan.lean:15–16`, in the same directory). For an odd prime $\ell$ and a valuation ring $A$ of an algebraic closure $\Omega$ of $\mathbb Q$ lying over $\ell$, its first clause asks for an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ over $\mathbb Q$ with trivial $\ell$-adic cyclotomic character. The proof takes $\sigma$ from `exists_isFrob` with $F=\mathbb Q$ (`:33`), where $N(v)=\ell$ (`frobEx_norm`, `:6–21`). It then chooses $\iota\in I_A$ whose cyclotomic character is the inverse of that of $\sigma$ (`GlobalTheory.CycInertia.cycZ_Ine_surjective`, statement only, `:35`) and returns $\sigma\iota$, a Frobenius element by Lemma M(2) (`:36–39`). The second clause, that two Frobenius elements at $A$ differ by inertia, is Lemma M(1) (`:31`). Over $F=\mathbb Q(i)$ at $w=(1+i)$, the two Frobenius elements found above act on $\mu_8$ through $5$ and $1$ modulo $8$. This is the same phenomenon at $\ell=2$.

**Definition.**
1. An element $\sigma\in G_F$ is an *arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$* if $\sigma\in D_A$ and $v_A(\sigma x-x^q)<1$ for every $x$ with $v_A(x)\le1$. This is `IsFrob A (normV v) σ` (`Statement.lean:28–29`). The inverse of an arithmetic Frobenius element is a *geometric Frobenius element*.
2. For a finite Galois extension $L/F$ in $\Omega$, $S_L(A)$ is the set defined in the setting, and $I_L(A)$ is the set of $\tau\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ with $\tau(Q_L)=Q_L$ and $\tau x\equiv x\pmod{Q_L}$ for all $x\in\mathcal O_L$. When $Q_L$ is unramified over $w$, the unique element of $S_L(A)$ is Milne's Frobenius element $(Q_L,L/F)$ [Milne, *ANT*, p. 141](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf).
3. Let $R_0$ be a commutative ring, $R$ a commutative $R_0$-algebra and $\Gamma$ a group acting on $R$ by $R_0$-algebra automorphisms. Let $Q$ be a prime of $R$ and $P$ its inverse image in $R_0$. An element $\gamma$ with $\gamma(Q)=Q$ induces the $R_0/P$-automorphism $x+Q\mapsto\gamma x+Q$ of $R/Q$. This is Mathlib's `Ideal.Quotient.stabilizerHom` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Over.lean:341–352`). Its kernel is the inertia subgroup $\{\gamma:\gamma x-x\in Q\ \text{for all}\ x\in R\}$ (`ker_stabilizerHom`, `:354–356`).
4. If $k$ is an algebraic extension of a finite field $\mathbb F_q$, the map $x\mapsto x^q$ is an $\mathbb F_q$-algebra endomorphism of $k$ (`FiniteField.frobeniusAlgHom`, `Mathlib/FieldTheory/Finite/Basic.lean:333–343`). Every endomorphism of an algebraic extension is bijective, and the resulting automorphism is `FiniteField.frobeniusAlgEquivOfAlgebraic` (`:363–367`).

**Lemma J (finite layers).** Let $L/F$ be a finite Galois extension inside $\Omega$.
1. $B\cap L=\mathcal O_L$, and $Q_L=\mathfrak p_A\cap\mathcal O_L$ is a maximal ideal of $\mathcal O_L$ over $w$.
2. If $\sigma$ is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$, then $\sigma|_L\in S_L(A)$.
3. $S_L(A)$ is nonempty and is a left coset of $I_L(A)$. For $L\subseteq L'$, restriction maps $S_{L'}(A)$ into $S_L(A)$ and $I_{L'}(A)$ into $I_L(A)$.
4. $\lvert I_L(A)\rvert=e(Q_L/w)$. In particular $S_L(A)$ has exactly one element when $w$ is unramified in $L$.

**Proof.** This lemma is proved here from the cited facts; it is not a Lean declaration. (1) An element of $L$ is integral over $\mathcal O_F$ if and only if it is integral over $\mathbb Z$ (§18.3.10). The ideal $Q_L$ is prime and lies over $w$ by Lemma G(2)–(3) of §18.3.10. It is nonzero, hence maximal in the Dedekind domain $\mathcal O_L$. (2) For $x\in\mathcal O_L$ the hypothesis gives $\sigma x-x^q\in\mathfrak m_A\cap\mathcal O_L=Q_L$, and $\sigma(A)=A$ gives $\sigma(Q_L)=\sigma(\mathfrak m_A)\cap\sigma(\mathcal O_L)=Q_L$. (3) Nonemptiness is Lemma K(2) below, applied to $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ acting on $\mathcal O_L$ and the $q$-th power map of the finite field $\mathcal O_L/Q_L$. The group $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ fixes only $F$, and $\mathcal O_F$ is integrally closed, so $\mathcal O_L$ is invariant over $\mathcal O_F$. If $\tau,\tau'\in S_L(A)$ and $x\in\mathcal O_L$, then $\tau(\tau^{-1}\tau'x-x)=\tau'x-\tau x\equiv x^q-x^q\pmod{Q_L}$, so $\tau^{-1}\tau'\in I_L(A)$. Conversely $\tau\iota\in S_L(A)$ for $\iota\in I_L(A)$. Restriction preserves both congruences because $Q_{L'}\cap\mathcal O_L=Q_L$. (4) [Paper] The decomposition group of $Q_L$ has order $e(Q_L/w)f(Q_L/w)$ [Milne, *ANT*, Proposition 8.10 and the diagram after it, p. 139](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf). It maps onto the automorphism group of the residue extension, which has order $f(Q_L/w)$ [Stacks Project, Lemma 15.112.9 (0BRJ)](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BRJ), and the kernel is $I_L(A)$. $\square$

**Lemma K (lifting through a finite quotient).** [Lean, read] Let $R_0$ be a commutative ring, $R$ a commutative $R_0$-algebra and $\Gamma$ a finite group acting on $R$ by $R_0$-algebra automorphisms. Assume that $R$ is invariant over $R_0$: every element of $R$ fixed by $\Gamma$ is the image of an element of $R_0$. Let $Q$ be a prime of $R$ and $P$ its inverse image in $R_0$.
1. If $k$ is a domain containing $R/Q$ as an $R_0/P$-algebra, every $R_0/P$-automorphism of $k$ maps $R/Q$ onto itself (`Ideal.Quotient.exists_algEquiv_fixedPoint_quotient_under`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/Invariant/Basic.lean:453–475`, from `exists_algHom_fixedPoint_quotient_under`, `:415–446`).
2. Every $R_0/P$-automorphism of $R/Q$ is induced by some $\gamma\in\Gamma$ with $\gamma(Q)=Q$ (`Ideal.Quotient.stabilizerHom_surjective`, `:362–370`).

**Proof.** Line numbers refer to `Invariant/Basic.lean`. (1) Let $\sigma$ be an automorphism of $k$ and $x\in R$. The polynomial $\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(X-\gamma x)$ has coefficients fixed by $\Gamma$, hence coefficients coming from $R_0$ (`charpoly_mem_lifts`, `:135–137`). Its image in $k[X]$ has the root $\bar x$. Since $\sigma$ fixes the image of $R_0$, $\sigma(\bar x)$ is also a root, and since $k$ is a domain, $\sigma(\bar x)=\overline{\gamma x}$ for some $\gamma$. So $\sigma$ maps $R/Q$ into itself (`:425–446`). The same argument for $\sigma^{-1}$ gives the inverse map (`:457–475`).

(2) Mathlib first proves the statement for the fraction fields $K$ of $R_0/P$ and $L$ of $R/Q$ (`IsFractionRing.stabilizerHom_surjective`, `:353–359`). Let $H$ be the stabilizer of $Q$, acting on $L$, and let $f$ be an automorphism of $L$ over $K$. By Artin's theorem on fixed fields (`FixedPoints.toAlgAut_surjective`, statement only), it is enough that $f$ fixes every element of $L$ fixed by $H$. For fractions this reduces to elements of $R/Q$ (`fixed_of_fixed2`, `:330–350`). So let $b_0\in R$ have $H$-fixed residue. A conjugate $\gamma Q\ne Q$ is not contained in $Q$, and $Q$ is prime, so $Q$ does not contain the intersection of the finitely many conjugates $\gamma Q\ne Q$. Choose $b$ in that intersection and outside $Q$ (`:198–205`). From the characteristic polynomial of $b$ one builds a $\Gamma$-fixed $a\notin Q$ and an element $b'$ with $\gamma b'\equiv a$ or $0\pmod Q$, according as $\gamma\in H$ or not (`fixed_of_fixed1_aux1`, `:194–247`). The characteristic polynomial of $b'b_0$ has coefficients coming from $R_0$. Modulo $Q$ it becomes $(X-\bar a\bar b_0)^{\lvert H\rvert}X^{\lvert\Gamma\rvert-\lvert H\rvert}$ (`fixed_of_fixed1_aux2`, `:250–261`, and `fixed_of_fixed1`, `:277–308`). The automorphism $f$ permutes its roots, which are $0$ and $\bar a\bar b_0$, so $f$ fixes $\bar a\bar b_0$ (`fixed_of_fixed1_aux3`, `:264–274`). As $\bar a$ comes from $R_0$ and is nonzero, $f$ fixes $\bar b_0$. Finally, an automorphism of $R/Q$ is determined by its extension to $L$, which gives (2) (`:362–370`). $\square$

The classical proofs choose a generator of the residue field by the Chinese remainder theorem and use the polynomial $\prod(X-\gamma\alpha)$ [Milne, *ANT*, p. 141, footnote 2] · [Stacks Project, Lemma 15.112.9 (0BRJ)](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BRJ). Mathlib's statement assumes nothing about the residue fields; it is not restricted to finite or separable residue extensions.

**Lemma L (lifting through a profinite group).** [Lean, read] Let $R_0$ be a commutative ring, $R$ a commutative $R_0$-algebra and $G$ a compact, totally disconnected topological group acting on $R$ by $R_0$-algebra automorphisms. Assume that $R$ is invariant over $R_0$ and that the action map $G\times R\to R$ is continuous for the discrete topology on $R$. Let $Q$ be a prime of $R$ and $P$ its inverse image in $R_0$. Then every $R_0/P$-automorphism $\varphi$ of $R/Q$ is induced by some $a\in G$ with $a(Q)=Q$. This is `Ideal.Quotient.stabilizerHom_surjective_of_profinite` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/Invariant/Profinite.lean:175–209`).

**Proof.** Line numbers refer to `Profinite.lean`. For an open normal subgroup $N$ of $G$, let $R^N$ be the subring of $N$-fixed elements and $Q^N=Q\cap R^N$. The finite group $G/N$ acts on $R^N$, which is again invariant over $R_0$ (`Basic.lean:80–86`). Let $T_N$ be the set of $\gamma\in G/N$ with $\gamma(Q^N)=Q^N$ whose action on $R^N/Q^N$ agrees with $\varphi$ along the inclusion $R^N/Q^N\to R/Q$ (`stabilizerHomSurjectiveAuxFunctor`, `:129–149`). It is finite because $G/N$ is (`:151–156`). It is nonempty (`:158–172`): Lemma K(1), with $k=R/Q$, restricts $\varphi$ to an automorphism of $R^N/Q^N$, and Lemma K(2) lifts that automorphism to $G/N$. For $N\subseteq N'$ the projection $G/N\to G/N'$ maps $T_N$ into $T_{N'}$ (`:111–124,139–147`). The open normal subgroups form a cofiltered system, and a cofiltered limit of nonempty finite sets is nonempty (`nonempty_sections_of_finite_cofiltered_system`, statement only). A point of the limit is a compatible family $(\gamma_N)$. Since $G$ is the limit of its finite quotients (`ProfiniteGrp.isoLimittoFiniteQuotientFunctor`, statement only), there is $a\in G$ with image $\gamma_N$ in every $G/N$ (`:181–188`). Now let $x\in R$. Its stabilizer is open, because the action is continuous and $R$ is discrete (`stabilizer_isOpen`, `Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/MulAction.lean:277–278`). So it contains an open normal subgroup $N$ (`ProfiniteGrp.exist_openNormalSubgroup_sub_open_nhds_of_one`, statement only), and $x\in R^N$. On $R^N$ the element $a$ acts as $\gamma_N$. Hence $x\in a(Q)$ if and only if $x\in Q$ (`:190–201`), and $ax\equiv\varphi(x)\pmod Q$ (`:202–209`). $\square$

In Galois terms, take $G=G_F$ and $R=B$, and let $N=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L)$ for a finite Galois $L/F$. Then $R^N=\mathcal O_L$ and $G/N=\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$, and for $\varphi$ the $q$-th power map, $T_N=S_L(A)$ (Lemma J). The three examples compute $T_N$ for six such $N$. The Stacks Project proves the infinite Galois case with Zorn's lemma over finite Galois subextensions instead of a limit of finite sets [Stacks Project, Lemma 15.112.10(2) (0BRK)](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BRK). Both proofs reduce to the finite case and make a compatible choice. The continuity of the action is what places every $x$ in some finite layer.

**Theorem 18.3.11 (existence of Frobenius elements).** [Lean, read] Let $A$ be a valuation ring of $\Omega$ lying over the maximal ideal $w$ of $\mathcal O_F$, and let $q=N(w)$. There is $\sigma\in G_F$ with $\sigma(A)=A$ and $v_A(\sigma x-x^q)<1$ for every $x\in A$.

In Lean this is `exists_frob_over` (`GlobalB.lean:238–310`). The wrapper `exists_isFrob` (`Global.lean:345–347`) states it with `IsPlaceOver` and `IsFrob`, which unfold to the hypothesis and the conclusion used here.

**Proof.** Line numbers refer to `GlobalB.lean`.

*Step 1 (the setting of Lemma L).* As in Theorem 18.3.10, give $B$ the discrete topology (`:242–243`). The action of $G_F$ on $B$ is continuous by `continuousSMul_intCl`, and $B$ is invariant over $\mathcal O_F$ by `isInvariant_intCl` (Lemma I, `:244–245`). Let $Q=\mathfrak p_A$ (`:246`). It lies over $w$ by Lemma G(3) of §18.3.10 (`:247–248`). It is maximal, because $B$ is integral over $\mathcal O_F$ and $Q\cap\mathcal O_F=w$ is maximal (`Ideal.isMaximal_of_isIntegral_of_isMaximal_comap`, `:249–251`).

*Step 2 (the residue Frobenius).* The field $k_w=\mathcal O_F/w$ has $q$ elements, and $k_Q=B/Q$ is a field, integral and hence algebraic over $k_w$ (`:252–258`). So $y\mapsto y^q$ is a $k_w$-automorphism $\varphi$ of $k_Q$ (`FiniteField.frobeniusAlgEquivOfAlgebraic`, `:259–260`). The field $k_Q$ is infinite, and its finiteness is never needed.

*Step 3 (lifting).* Lemma L gives $\sigma\in G_F$ with $\sigma(Q)=Q$ inducing $\varphi$ (`:261–263`). So $\sigma b-b^q\in Q$, that is $v_A(\sigma b-b^q)<1$, for every algebraic integer $b$ (`hcong`, `:264–273`).

*Step 4 (the decomposition group).* By Lemma G(4) of §18.3.10, $\mathfrak p_{\sigma(A)}=\sigma(\mathfrak p_A)=\mathfrak p_A$. Lemma H(2) then gives $\sigma(A)=A$ (`hdec`, `:274–278`).

*Step 5 (from $B$ to $A$).* Let $x\in A$. Lemma H(1) gives algebraic integers $s,t$ with $v_A(s)=1$ and $sx=t$ (`exists_den`, `:279–280`). Step 3 applies to $s$ and $t$ (`:281–282`), and $v_A(t)\le1$ (`:283`). Moreover $v_A(\sigma s)=1$. Indeed $v_A(\sigma s)\le1$ because $\sigma s$ is integral, and $v_A(\sigma s)<1$ would give $v_A(s)<1$ by Step 4 and Lemma G(4) (`:284–295`). From $\sigma s\cdot\sigma x=\sigma t$ (`:296`) one obtains the identity
$$s^q\,\sigma(s)\,(\sigma x-x^q)=s^q\,(\sigma t-t^q)+t^q\,(s^q-\sigma s)$$
(`:297–298`). On the right, the first term has valuation $v_A(\sigma t-t^q)<1$ and the second has valuation at most $v_A(\sigma s-s^q)<1$, so the sum has valuation below one (`:299–307`). On the left $v_A(s)^q\,v_A(\sigma s)=1$, hence $v_A(\sigma x-x^q)<1$ (`:308–310`). $\square$

Step 5 is needed because `IsFrob` is stated for all of $A$, not only for algebraic integers. It uses the same denominators as the proof of Theorem 18.3.10.

**Lemma M (inertia cosets and conjugation).** [Lean, read for (1)–(3)]
1. If $\sigma$ and $\tau$ are arithmetic Frobenius elements at $A$, then $\sigma^{-1}\tau\in I_A$ (`inv_mul_mem_Ine_of_isFrob`, `Global.lean:239–250`).
2. If $\sigma$ is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ and $\iota\in I_A$, then $\sigma\iota$ is one (`isFrob_mul_of_mem_Ine`, `:252–261`). So the arithmetic Frobenius elements at $A$ form the coset $\sigma I_A$. It equals $I_A\sigma$ because $I_A$ is normal in $D_A$ (`conj_mem_Ine`, `:71–80`). The geometric Frobenius elements form the coset $\sigma^{-1}I_A$.
3. If $g\in G_F$ and $\tau$ is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $g(A)$, then $g^{-1}\tau g$ is one at $A$ (`conj_frob`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/GaloisLibrary/CrystallineBaseChange/FrobEx.lean:37–41`). Equivalently, $g\sigma g^{-1}$ is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $g(A)$ whenever $\sigma$ is one at $A$.
4. Let $L/F$ be finite Galois and unramified at $w$. The restrictions to $L$ of the arithmetic Frobenius elements at all valuation rings over $w$ form one conjugacy class of $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$.

**Proof.** (1) Both elements lie in $D_A$, hence so does $\sigma^{-1}\tau$. For $x\in A$,
$$\sigma\bigl((\sigma^{-1}\tau)x-x\bigr)=(\tau x-x^q)-(\sigma x-x^q)$$
(`:247–248`). The right side lies in $\mathfrak m_A$, and $\sigma$ preserves $\mathfrak m_A$ because $\sigma\in D_A$ (`valuation_apply_lt_one_iff`, `:36–40`). (2) Here $(\sigma\iota)x-x^q=\sigma(\iota x-x)+(\sigma x-x^q)$ (`:258–259`), and both terms lie in $\mathfrak m_A$. (3) Line numbers refer to `FrobEx.lean`. Since $\tau$ fixes $g(A)$, $g^{-1}\tau g$ fixes $A$ (`conj_dec`, `:25–29`). For $x\in A$ put $y=gx$. Then $g\bigl((g^{-1}\tau g)x-x^q\bigr)=\tau y-y^q$ (`conj_apply`, `:32–34`), and $g$ carries $A$ onto $g(A)$ and $\mathfrak m_A$ onto $\mathfrak m_{g(A)}$ (`val_smul_lt` and `val_smul_le`, `:12–22`). (4) By Theorem 18.3.10, every valuation ring over $w$ is $g(A)$ for some $g\in G_F$. By (1)–(3) the arithmetic Frobenius elements at $g(A)$ form $g\sigma I_Ag^{-1}$. By Lemma J(2) and J(4) their restrictions to $L$ are the single element $\bar g\,\bar\sigma\,\bar g^{-1}$, where bars denote restriction to $L$. As $g$ runs through $G_F$, $\bar g$ runs through $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$. $\square$

Part (4) is Milne's 8.14 [Milne, *ANT*, pp. 141–142](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf). It is proved here from the Lean statements and is not itself a Lean declaration. `conj_frob` is a private lemma of `FrobEx.lean`; the archive proves conjugation locally where it needs it rather than in `Global.lean`. In `ChebotarevDegreeOne` the valuation ring is existentially quantified together with the Frobenius element. So the prescribed $g$ only has to lie in the conjugacy class of part (4).

**Corollary 18.3.11.1 (what the consumers use).** [Lean, read]
1. *Roots of unity.* Let $\sigma$ be an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$, let $m\ge1$ be an integer with $m\notin w$, and let $\zeta\in\Omega$ with $\zeta^m=1$. Then $\sigma\zeta=\zeta^q$.
2. *Degree-one primes.* Let $Z\supseteq F$ be a number field inside $\Omega$, and let $\mathfrak P$ be a prime of $\mathcal O_Z$ with $N(\mathfrak P)$ prime, lying over the prime $\mathfrak p$ of $\mathcal O_F$. Suppose $A$ lies over $\mathfrak P$ and $\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/Z)$ is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ for $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/Z)$. Then $N(\mathfrak p)=N(\mathfrak P)$, the ring $A$ lies over $\mathfrak p$, and $\sigma$, viewed in $G_F$, is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ for $G_F$ (`GlobalTheory.isFrob_restrictScalars_of_prime_absNorm`, `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Proofs/InertiaEigenvalues/IsFrobRestrictScalarsOfPrimeAbsNorm.lean:9–36`).
3. *Cyclotomic character.* For a prime $\ell$ with $\ell\notin w$, the $\ell$-adic cyclotomic character of an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ is $q\in\mathbb Z_\ell^\times$ (`cycZ_of_isFrob`, `Global.lean:364–368`, through `cycZ_toZModPow_eq`, `:199–229`).

**Proof.** (1) Both $\sigma\zeta$ and $\zeta^q$ are $m$-th roots of unity, hence units of $A$ (`valuation_eq_one_of_pow_eq_one`, `src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/GlobalA.lean:73–89`). Their quotient $\xi$ satisfies $\xi^m=1$ and $v_A(\xi-1)<1$. Suppose $\xi\ne1$. Then $\sum_{i<m}\xi^i=0$, while each $\xi^i-1=(\xi-1)\sum_{j<i}\xi^j$ lies in $\mathfrak m_A$. So $-m=\sum_{i<m}(\xi^i-1)$ lies in $\mathfrak m_A$ (`eq_one_of_pow_eq_one`, `:49–71`, and `eq_of_pow_eq_one`, `:91–106`). Because $A$ lies over $w$, this means $m\in w$ (`not_valuation_lt_one_of_isPlaceOver`, `Global.lean:189–197`), a contradiction. (2) Line numbers refer to `IsFrobRestrictScalarsOfPrimeAbsNorm.lean`. One has $N(\mathfrak P)=N(\mathfrak p)^{f(\mathfrak P/\mathfrak p)}$ (Mathlib `Ideal.absNorm_pow_inertiaDeg`, statement only), and a prime power that is prime has exponent one (`:18–22`). The place condition restricts along $\mathcal O_F\subseteq\mathcal O_Z$ (`:24–26`). The decomposition condition is unchanged, because $\sigma$ acts on $\Omega$ in the same way (`:27–33`). The congruence has the same exponent (`:34–36`). (3) Let $\zeta$ be a primitive $\ell^n$-th root of unity. Then $\sigma\zeta=\zeta^c$, where $c$ is the cyclotomic character reduced modulo $\ell^n$. By (1), $\zeta^c=\zeta^q$, so $c\equiv q\pmod{\ell^n}$ for every $n$ (`:207–229`). $\square$

*The call in `chebotarevDegreeOne'`* (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Proofs/InertiaEigenvalues/ChebotarevDegreeOne.lean:17–64`). Given $L/F$ and $g$, the proof chooses a prime $m$, a finite extension $Z$ of $F$ inside $\Omega$ and a class $\tau\in(\mathbb Z/m)^\times$. For $\sigma$ fixing $Z$, the condition $\sigma\zeta=\zeta^{\tau}$ on all $m$-th roots of unity forces $\sigma|_L=g$ (`exists_cyclotomic_crossing_field_deciding_frobenius`, `:23`, statement only). It then takes a prime $\mathfrak P$ of $Z$ outside a finite set, with $N(\mathfrak P)$ prime and $N(\mathfrak P)\equiv\tau\pmod m$ (`:37`, statement only). It takes a valuation ring $A$ over $\mathfrak P$ (`exists_isPlaceOver`, `:38`) and an arithmetic Frobenius element $\sigma$ at $A$ for $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/Z)$ from `exists_isFrob` (`:39`). Part (2), through the wrapper `GlobalTheory.T.isFrob_restrictScalars_of_prime_absNorm'` (`IsFrobRestrictScalarsOfPrimeAbsNormStatement.lean:6–7`), turns $\sigma$ into an arithmetic Frobenius element over $F$ at the prime below (`:40`). This is the element that the conclusion of `ChebotarevDegreeOne` asks for (`:42–43`). If $m$ lay in $\mathfrak P$, the prime $N(\mathfrak P)$ would divide a power of $m$ and so equal $m$, and then $\tau=0$ in $\mathbb Z/m$; so $m\notin\mathfrak P$ (`:44–52`). Part (1) gives $\sigma\zeta=\zeta^{N(\mathfrak P)}=\zeta^{\tau}$ for every $m$-th root of unity (`:53–62`, with `eq_of_pow_eq_one` at `:60`). The crossing-field property then yields $\sigma|_L=g$ (`:63–64`). So `exists_isFrob` supplies exactly two things: membership in the decomposition group, used in part (2), and the congruence, used in parts (1) and (2).

The last row has its own inputs. `𝓞 F ⧸ v.asIdeal` must be a field with a `Fintype` structure (`:253–254`). The field structure on `IntCl F Ω ⧸ Q` comes from the maximality of Step 1 (`:249–252`). Algebraicity comes from `Ideal.Quotient.algebra_isIntegral_of_liesOver` (`:256–258`). The exponent is `Fintype.card`, which `:265` identifies with `Nat.card`, the `normV` of the statement.

**Remark (arithmetic and geometric Frobenius).** `IsFrob` and Mathlib's `IsArithFrobAt` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/Frobenius.lean:54–55,183–185`) are arithmetic: they act on residues as $x\mapsto x^q$. Milne's local reciprocity law uses the same normalization, sending a prime element to the arithmetic Frobenius of every finite unramified extension [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, I, Theorem 1.1(a), p. 20](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf). The archive's `IsGlobalArtin.frob` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/AutomorphicForms₂/ExistsUniqueHcharOfFiniteOrder.lean:34–36`) instead matches the images of uniformizers with the elements `toAb E Ω σ⁻¹`, where $\sigma$ is arithmetic. This is the geometric normalization. By Lemma M(2) these inverses form the coset $\sigma^{-1}I_A$, whose image in $G_F^{\mathrm{ab}}$ is a coset of the image of inertia. The field `ine` of the same structure (`:31–32`) identifies that image of inertia with the image of the local units, which is the ambiguity in the choice of a uniformizer. In the three examples the Frobenius elements at the finite layers have order at most two, so the two normalizations cannot be told apart there. On $\mu_{25}$ in the second example they can. An arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ sends $\zeta_{25}$ to $\zeta_{25}^9$, and its inverse sends it to $\zeta_{25}^{14}$, since $9\cdot14\equiv1\pmod{25}$ and $9$ has order $10$ modulo $25$. So for any `art` with `IsGlobalArtin art`, the image of a uniformizer at $w=(3)$ acts on $\mu_{5^\infty}$ through $9^{-1}\in\mathbb Z_5^\times$, by `frob` and Corollary 18.3.11.1(3). Under Milne's normalization it would act through $9$.

**Remark (inertia in two languages).** Mathlib's inertia subgroup of $Q=\mathfrak p_A$ consists of the $\gamma$ with $\gamma b-b\in Q$ for every $b\in B$ (`ker_stabilizerHom`). The archive's $I_A$ asks for $v_A(\gamma x-x)<1$ for every $x\in A$. The stabilizer of $Q$ is $D_A$, by Lemma G(4) and Lemma H(2) of §18.3.10. For $\gamma\in D_A$ the two inertia conditions agree. One direction holds because $B\subseteq A$. For the other, write $x=t/s$ as in Step 5. Then
$$\gamma x-x=\frac{s(\gamma t-t)-t(\gamma s-s)}{s\,\gamma(s)},$$
and the denominator is a unit of $A$. So the fibre of the stabilizer map over the residue Frobenius is $\sigma I_A$, and Lemma M(1)–(2) is the archive's valuation-theoretic form of `ker_stabilizerHom`. This comparison is proved here and is not a Lean declaration.

**Remark (comparison with Mathlib's finite theory and the literature).** [Lean, read for the cited bodies] For a finite group, Mathlib proves the existence of Frobenius elements in `IsArithFrobAt.exists_of_isInvariant` (`Frobenius.lean:221–239`). It needs `[Finite G]` and `[Finite (S ⧸ Q)]`, lifts the iterated Frobenius of the finite residue field by `Ideal.Quotient.stabilizerHom_surjective`, and does not use the profinite theorem. The same file proves the analogues of Lemma M(1) and (3) (`mul_inv_mem_inertia`, `:200–203`, and `conj`, `:205–212`). It also chooses Frobenius elements so that those at primes over the same prime are conjugate (`arithFrobAt`, `:260–262`). The archive uses that finite theory too. `GlobalTheory.NT.exists_isFrob` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Definitions/GlobalTwisting/SmulCommClassGalIntegers.lean:143–149`) treats finite Galois extensions of number fields through `IsArithFrobAt.exists_of_isInvariant`. `exists_frob_over` is the archive's profinite counterpart, with the extension from $B$ to $A$ of Step 5. In the literature, the existence of Frobenius elements in a finite Galois extension is Milne's footnote [Milne, *ANT*, p. 141, footnote 2]. The infinite statement, that the decomposition group surjects onto the automorphisms of the residue extension, is Lemma 15.112.10(2) of the Stacks Project [0BRK](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BRK). Conjugation and towers are Milne's 8.14–8.16 [Milne, *ANT*, pp. 141–142](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf).
