### 18.3.12 Transporting Galois groups along a field isomorphism: conjugation, decomposition groups and quotients

**Motivation.** Corollary 18.3.8.1(b) transports restriction squares along an isomorphism $e\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma'$ of global groups and isomorphisms $e_{D,i}\colon D_i\to D'_{e_I(i)}$ of local groups. The remark on $e_D$ in §18.3.10 listed what the archive requires of these data: `eG : Γ ≃ₜ* Γ'`, `eI : I ≃ I'`, `eD : ∀ i, D i ≃ₜ* D' (eI i)` and the two equations `hD` and `hσ` (`src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/TateShafarevichTransport.lean:37–46,55–56`). On the main path all of them are built in one proof, `ptConcl_of_iso_of` (`src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/PublishedTheorems/PoitouTateDuality/PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:22–103`). It moves the Poitou–Tate conclusion `PTConclAt` from a number field $F'$ with algebraic closure $\Omega'$ back to a number field $F$ with algebraic closure $\Omega$, along ring isomorphisms $e\colon F\to F'$ and $E\colon\Omega\to\Omega'$ that are compatible with the structure maps. Its caller `ptConclAt_all_of` (`Bridge.lean:14–19`, same directory) uses it to replace an arbitrary pair $(F,\Omega)$ by a subfield of `AlgebraicClosure ℚ`. The local isomorphisms $e_{D,v}$ are restrictions to decomposition groups of an isomorphism $\Phi\colon G_F\to G_{F'}$ of absolute Galois groups. The global isomorphism $e$ is not $\Phi$. It is the isomorphism $\Psi$ that $\Phi$ induces between the quotients $G_{F,S}$ and $G_{F',S'}$ of the absolute groups by the closed normal subgroups generated by inertia outside $S$ and $S'$. Both come from statements that §18.3.10 left unread, `galois_group_transport_isomorphism` and `galois_quotient_equiv_of_matching_places`. This subsection reads their proofs. $\Phi$ is conjugation by $E$. It is continuous because $E^{-1}$ carries finite subextensions of $\Omega'/F'$ to finite subextensions of $\Omega/F$. It carries decomposition and inertia groups at $A$ to those at $E(A)$. It descends to $\Psi$ because it carries the inertia groups that define the quotients onto each other, and for this the sets of places must correspond under $e$. We then supply each datum of Corollary 18.3.8.1(b), follow a nontrivial conjugation and a choice of completion through two examples, and show by five examples that no hypothesis can be dropped. The conjugacy of valuation rings proved in §18.3.10 plays no role in producing $\Phi$, which is built from $E$ alone.

Three distinctions run through the subsection. The decomposition groups are subgroups of the absolute groups $G_F$ and $G_{F'}$, the global group is a quotient of $G_F$, and the examples see only finite quotients of both. The archive asserts only that $\Phi$ and $\Psi$ exist, but each is determined by its defining property once $E$ is fixed; the non-canonical input is $E$. And all the isomorphisms must be continuous in both directions, which is more than being isomorphisms of abstract groups.

**Setting.** Let $F,F'$ be number fields with algebraic closures $\Omega,\Omega'$ and structure maps $\iota\colon F\to\Omega$ and $\iota'\colon F'\to\Omega'$. The group $G_F=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F)$ carries the Krull topology, for which the subgroups $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)$ with $M/F$ finite form a basis of neighbourhoods of $1$ (Mathlib `krullTopology_mem_nhds_one_iff`, `Mathlib/FieldTheory/KrullTopology.lean:143–152`) [Milne, *Fields and Galois Theory*, Proposition 7.6, p. 95](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf). The product is composition, $(\sigma\tau)(x)=\sigma(\tau x)$ (Mathlib `AlgEquiv.aut` and `AlgEquiv.mul_apply`, `Mathlib/Algebra/Algebra/Equiv.lean:637–638,651`). For a valuation ring $A$ of $\Omega$ we use the decomposition group $D_A$, the stabilizer of $A$ (`Dec`, `src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/Global.lean:15`, which is Mathlib's `ValuationSubring.decompositionSubgroup`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/Valuation/RamificationGroup.lean:30–31`), the inertia set $I_A$ of §18.3.8 (`Inertia`, `src/Formalism/ConjugateSelfDual/Statement.lean:25–26`) and the arithmetic Frobenius elements of §18.3.11 (`IsFrob`, `:28–29`). For a set $T$ of maximal ideals of $\mathcal O_F$, let $N_T$ be the closure of the normal subgroup generated by all $I_A$ with $A$ lying over a place $v\notin T$. Put $G_{F,T}=G_F/N_T$ with the quotient topology, and let $\pi_T$ be the projection (`NS`, `GS`, `toGS`, `Global.lean:128–150`). As $N_T$ is closed and normal, $G_{F,T}$ is the Galois group of its fixed field $F_T$ over $F$ [Milne, *FT*, Theorem 7.13(d), p. 98](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf). If a finite Galois extension $L/F$ in $\Omega$ is unramified at every finite place outside $T$, then $L\subseteq F_T$. Indeed, let $A$ lie over such a place. Elements of $D_A$ preserve $\mathfrak m_A\cap\mathcal O_L$, and $\mathcal O_L\subseteq A$ by Lemma G(1) of §18.3.10, so restriction maps $I_A$ into the inertia group $I_L(A)$ of §18.3.11, which is trivial by Lemma J(4) there. So the closed normal subgroup $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L)$ contains every such $I_A$, hence $N_T$. For a finite set $S$ and valuation rings $A_v$ lying over $v\in S$, the local maps are $\lambda_v=\pi_S|_{D_{A_v}}\colon D_{A_v}\to G_{F,S}$ (`locHom`, `Global.lean:152–156`; `locFam`, `src/ReciprocityTheory/Hypotheses/GalCoh.lean:132–139`). They are not injective in general, and their sources are full decomposition groups of the absolute group.

**First example — complex conjugation on $\mathbb Q(i)$: a place that moves and a place that stays.** Let $F=F'=\mathbb Q(i)$, $\Omega=\Omega'$ an algebraic closure, and $e(i)=-i$. Keep $\vartheta^3=2$, $\zeta^2+\zeta+1=0$, $L=F(\vartheta,\zeta)$ and the generators $\rho\colon\vartheta\mapsto\zeta\vartheta,\ \zeta\mapsto\zeta$ and $\kappa\colon\vartheta\mapsto\vartheta,\ \zeta\mapsto\zeta^2$ of $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)\simeq S_3$ from the first example of §18.3.11, with $\kappa\rho=\rho^2\kappa$. The twelve algebraic integers $i^a\vartheta^b\zeta^c$ ($a,c<2$, $b<3$) form a $\mathbb Q$-basis of $L$ of discriminant $(-4)^6(-108)^4(-3)^6$, and the discriminant of $\mathcal O_L$ divides it. So no prime other than $2$ and $3$ ramifies in $L$ [Milne, *Algebraic Number Theory*, Theorem 3.35, p. 60](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf), and $L/F$ is unramified outside $(1+i)$ and $(3)$.

*Choosing $E$ at $2$.* Fix an embedding $\eta\colon\Omega\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_2$ and put $A_\eta=\{x:\lvert\eta(x)\rvert\le1\}$, $\iota_2=\eta(i)$, $\beta=\eta(\vartheta)$ and $z_2=\eta(\zeta)$. A unit of $\mathbb Z_2$ is a square only if it is $1$ modulo $8$, so $-1$, $-3$ and their product $3$ are not squares, and $\mathbb Q_2(\iota_2,z_2)$ has degree $4$. The polynomial $X^3-2$ is Eisenstein, so $\beta$ has degree $3$, and $N=\mathbb Q_2(\iota_2,\beta,z_2)$ has degree $12$. Hence $N\simeq\mathbb Q_2[X,Y,Z]/(X^2+1,Y^3-2,Z^2+Z+1)$, and $N$ has an automorphism with $\iota_2\mapsto-\iota_2$, $\beta\mapsto z_2\beta$ and $z_2\mapsto z_2$. It extends to some $\theta\in G_{\mathbb Q_2}$ [Milne, *FT*, Proposition 7.4, p. 95](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf). Theorem 18.3.8(2) over the base field $\mathbb Q$, where $K_\eta=\mathbb Q_2$, gives a unique $E\in D^{\mathbb Q}_{A_\eta}\subset\operatorname{Aut}(\Omega/\mathbb Q)$ with $\eta\circ E=\theta\circ\eta$. As $\eta$ is injective,
$$E(i)=-i,\qquad E(\vartheta)=\zeta\vartheta,\qquad E(\zeta)=\zeta,\qquad E(A_\eta)=A_\eta .$$
So $E\circ\iota=\iota\circ e$, and $(e,E)$ is a transport datum in the sense of the definition below. On $L$ one checks $E^2=\rho^2$, so $E^{-1}$ sends $\vartheta$ to $\zeta^2\vartheta$ and fixes $\zeta$. For $\sigma\in G_F$ the element $\Phi(\sigma)=E\sigma E^{-1}$ fixes $F$. On $L$ it is conjugation by $\rho$, because $E|_L$ is $\rho$ composed with the automorphism of $L$ that sends $i$ to $-i$ and fixes $\vartheta$ and $\zeta$, and that automorphism commutes with $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$. Thus
$$\Phi(\kappa)|_L=\rho^2\kappa,\qquad\Phi(\rho)|_L=\rho,\qquad\text{whereas}\qquad (E^{-1}\kappa E)|_L=\rho\kappa .$$

*The split place $(2+i)$.* Let $j_0\colon\Omega\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_5$ and $A_0=\{x:\lvert j_0(x)\rvert\le1\}$ be as in the first example of §18.3.11, with $j_0(i)=\iota$, $j_0(\vartheta)=r$ and $j_0(\zeta)=z$, where $\iota,r\in\mathbb Z_5$ are $3$ modulo $5$. The ring $A_0$ lies over $(2+i)$. By §18.3.11 the Frobenius elements at $A_0$ restrict to $\kappa$ on $L$, and the stabilizer of $Q_0=\mathfrak m_{A_0}\cap\mathcal O_L$ in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ is $\{1,\kappa\}$. So the image of $D_{A_0}$ in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ is $\{1,\kappa\}$. The archive transports $A_0$ to $E(A_0)$, the ring `(Av v).comap E.symm` (`PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:39`). An adapted embedding for it is $j'=j_0\circ E^{-1}$, since $x\in E(A_0)\iff\lvert j_0(E^{-1}x)\rvert\le1$. On generators $j'$ is $(i,\vartheta,\zeta)\mapsto(-\iota,z^2r,z)$. So $\lvert j'(2-i)\rvert=\lvert2+\iota\rvert<1$: the ring $E(A_0)$ lies over $(2-i)$, the image of $(2+i)$ under the place map. Let $\varphi$ be the Frobenius automorphism of $\mathbb Q_{25}=\mathbb Q_5(z)$. It maps $j'(L)=\mathbb Q(\iota,r,z)$ onto itself and fixes $j'(F)=\mathbb Q(\iota)$, so $\tau=j'^{-1}\circ\varphi\circ j'$ lies in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$. From $\varphi(z^2r)=z^4r=zr=j'(\zeta^2\vartheta)$ and $\varphi(z)=z^2=j'(\zeta^2)$ we get $\tau=\rho^2\kappa$. As in §18.3.11, $\tau$ satisfies the Frobenius congruence modulo $\mathfrak m_{E(A_0)}\cap\mathcal O_L$, so by Lemma J(2) and J(4) there it is the restriction of every Frobenius element at $E(A_0)$. That prime has a stabilizer of order two, because $E$ maps $L$ onto itself and so $(2-i)$, like $(2+i)$, has three primes in $L$. So the image of $D_{E(A_0)}$ in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ is $\{1,\rho^2\kappa\}$, which is the image of $\{1,\kappa\}$ under conjugation by $\rho$, as part 2 of the theorem below predicts. The other direction gives a different prime. The ring $E^{-1}(A_0)$ has adapted embedding $j_0\circ E=(-\iota,zr,z)$. It also lies over $(2-i)$, and the same computation gives the Frobenius element $\rho\kappa$. The completion fields agree, $K_{j'}=\mathbb Q_5(-\iota)=\mathbb Q_5=K_{j_0}$. If $g\in D_{A_0}$ restricts to $\kappa$, then $i_{j'}(\Phi g)$ and $i_{j_0}(g)$ both restrict to $\varphi$ on the image of $L$. This is the compatible completion of the remark below.

*The ramified place $(1+i)$.* Here $e(1+i)=1-i=-i(1+i)$, so the place map fixes $(1+i)$, and $E(A_\eta)=A_\eta$: neither the index nor the valuation ring moves. By Theorem 18.3.8(4) over the base field $L$, the prime of $L$ under $A_\eta$ has $ef=[\mathbb Q_2(\eta(L)):\mathbb Q_2]=[N:\mathbb Q_2]=12=[L:\mathbb Q]$, so it is the only prime of $L$ above $2$ [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 3.34, p. 59](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf). Put $K=K_\eta=\mathbb Q_2(\iota_2)$. For $h\in G_K$ the element $g=i_\eta^{-1}(h)\in D_{A_\eta}$ of Theorem 18.3.8(2) satisfies $\eta\circ g=h\circ\eta$, so $g|_L$ is $h|_N$ read through $\eta$. This gives an injective homomorphism $\operatorname{Gal}(N/K)\to\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$, since $\eta(L)$ generates $N$; both groups have order $6$, and $G_K\to\operatorname{Gal}(N/K)$ is surjective [Milne, *FT*, Proposition 7.7, p. 96](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf). So $D_{A_\eta}\subset G_F$ maps onto $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$. On this image $e_D=\Phi|_{D_{A_\eta}}$ is conjugation by $\rho$, which sends $\kappa$ to $\rho^2\kappa$; so $e_D$ is not the identity. In local terms, for $g\in D_{A_\eta}$,
$$\eta\circ EgE^{-1}=\theta\circ\eta\circ gE^{-1}=\theta\,i_\eta(g)\circ\eta\circ E^{-1}=\theta\,i_\eta(g)\,\theta^{-1}\circ\eta ,$$
and $\theta$ preserves $K$, so uniqueness in Theorem 18.3.8(2) gives $i_\eta(\Phi g)=\theta\,i_\eta(g)\,\theta^{-1}$, with $\theta\in G_{\mathbb Q_2}$ outside $G_K$. On $N/K$, whose group is again $S_3$, conjugation by $\theta$ sends the transposition fixing $\beta$ to the one fixing $z_2\beta$. This is the choice of completion. The ring $A_\eta$ has the two adapted embeddings $\eta$ and $\eta\circ E^{-1}=\theta^{-1}\circ\eta$. They have the same completion field $K$ but embed $F$ differently, by $i\mapsto\iota_2$ and $i\mapsto-\iota_2$. Read through $\eta$ on both sides, $e_D$ is conjugation by $\theta$. Read through $\eta$ on the source and $\theta^{-1}\circ\eta$ on the target, it is the identity of $G_K$.

*The quotients.* Take $S=\{(1+i),(3),(2+i)\}$, which contains the place above $\ell=3$, and $S'=\{(1+i),(3),(2-i)\}$, its image. As $L/F$ is unramified outside $(1+i)$ and $(3)$, the setting gives $L\subseteq F_S$ and $L\subseteq F_{S'}$. So $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ is a quotient of both $G_{F,S}$ and $G_{F,S'}$, and on it $\Psi$ induces $\bar\sigma\mapsto E\bar\sigma E^{-1}$, conjugation by $\rho$. The equation `hD` reads, on $L$, $\lambda'_{(2-i)}(e_D(d))|_L=\rho^2\kappa=\Psi(\lambda_{(2+i)}(d))|_L$ when $d|_L=\kappa$; at $(1+i)$ it is the same conjugation on all of $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$. One $\Psi$ serves the place that moves and the place that stays. Finally, if $\sigma\xi=\xi^a$ for a root of unity $\xi$, then $\Phi(\sigma)(E\xi)=E(\xi^a)=(E\xi)^a$. So $\Phi(\sigma)$ and $\sigma$ have the same cyclotomic character, which is what the statement `ring_equiv_cyclotomic_character` asserts (`PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:29`); its proof is not read.

**Second example — a cubic field and its conjugate.** Keep $\Omega$ and $E$, and let $F=\mathbb Q(\vartheta)$, $F'=\mathbb Q(\zeta\vartheta)=E(F)$, $\Omega'=\Omega$ and $e=E|_F$, so $e(\vartheta)=\zeta\vartheta$. Now $F\ne F'$ inside the same $\Omega$. On $L_2=\mathbb Q(\vartheta,\zeta)$, with group $S_3=\langle\rho,\kappa\rangle$ over $\mathbb Q$, the subgroups fixing the two fields are $\operatorname{Gal}(L_2/F)=\{1,\kappa\}$ and $\operatorname{Gal}(L_2/F')=\{1,\rho^2\kappa\}$, since $\rho^2\kappa(\zeta\vartheta)=\zeta^2\cdot\zeta^2\vartheta=\zeta\vartheta$. Conjugation by $E$, which is $\rho$ on $L_2$, sends $\kappa$ to $\rho^2\kappa$. So $\Phi(G_F)=G_{F'}$ and $\Phi(\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L_2))=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L_2)$, as Lemma N(5) below predicts. The Krull step of Lemma O is visible here. For $K'=L_2=F'(\zeta)$ with $F'$-basis $1,\zeta$, the field generated over $F$ by $E^{-1}(1)=1$ and $E^{-1}(\zeta)=\zeta$ is $F(\zeta)=L_2$, and the proof shows $\Phi(\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L_2))\subseteq\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L_2)$.

*Places.* As $\vartheta^3=2$, $(\vartheta)$ is the only place of $F$ over $2$, with residue field $\mathbb F_2$, and the place map sends it to $(\zeta\vartheta)$. Over $3$, $(1+\vartheta)^3=3(1+\vartheta+\vartheta^2)$, and $1+\vartheta+\vartheta^2=(\vartheta-1)^{-1}$ is a unit, so $3\mathcal O_F=(1+\vartheta)^3$, which goes to $(1+\zeta\vartheta)$. The ring $A_\eta$ lies over $(\vartheta)$ for $F$ and over $(\zeta\vartheta)$ for $F'$, and $E(A_\eta)=A_\eta$. So the archive's transported ring is $A_\eta$ itself. The two decomposition groups $D_{A_\eta}\cap G_F$ and $D_{A_\eta}\cap G_{F'}$ are nevertheless different subgroups of $\operatorname{Aut}(\Omega/\mathbb Q)$, as their images in $\operatorname{Gal}(L_2/\mathbb Q)$ show. The residue field of $L_2$ above $2$ contains the image of $\zeta$, a root of $X^2+X+1$, which has no root in $\mathbb F_2$. So it contains $\mathbb F_4$, and $(\vartheta)$ is inert in $L_2$. Its Frobenius element is $\kappa$, the element acting on residues as $x\mapsto x^2$, with $q=N((\vartheta))=2$. The decomposition images are $\{1,\kappa\}$ for $F$ and $\{1,\rho^2\kappa\}$ for $F'$. And $e_D$ sends the Frobenius element $\kappa$ for $F$ to the Frobenius element $\rho^2\kappa$ for $F'$ at the same ring, as the third clause of `locHyp` requires.

*The choice of completion.* Through $\eta$ the completion fields are $K=\mathbb Q_2(\beta)$ for $F$ and $K'=\mathbb Q_2(z_2\beta)$ for $F'$. These are different subfields of $\overline{\mathbb Q}_2$, since $z_2$ has degree $2$ and so does not lie in $\mathbb Q_2(\beta)$, which has degree $3$. The computation of the first example gives $i_\eta(\Phi g)=\theta\,i_\eta(g)\,\theta^{-1}$, now in $G_{K'}=\theta G_K\theta^{-1}$. On $N_2=\mathbb Q_2(\beta,z_2)$, conjugation by $\theta$ carries $\operatorname{Gal}(N_2/K)$, generated by the element that fixes $\beta$ and squares $z_2$, onto $\operatorname{Gal}(N_2/K')$. With the adapted embedding $\eta\circ E^{-1}$ for $E(A_\eta)=A_\eta$ instead, the completion of $F'$ becomes $\mathbb Q_2(\eta(E^{-1}(\zeta\vartheta)))=\mathbb Q_2(\beta)=K$, and $e_D$ becomes the identity of $G_K$. So the same valuation ring of $\Omega$ carries two completions of $F'$ at $(\zeta\vartheta)$. One choice makes $e_D$ the identity. The other makes it conjugation by $\theta$ between two different local Galois groups.

*The quotients.* For $\ell=3$ take $S=\{(\vartheta),(1+\vartheta)\}$, which contains the place over $3$, and $S'=\{(\zeta\vartheta),(1+\zeta\vartheta)\}$. The basis $\vartheta^b\zeta^c$ of $L_2$ has discriminant $(-108)^2(-3)^3$. So only primes above $2$ and $3$ can ramify in $L_2$, and $L_2/F$ and $L_2/F'$ are unramified outside $S$ and $S'$. $\Psi$ induces $\{1,\kappa\}\to\{1,\rho^2\kappa\}$, an isomorphism between Galois groups over different fields. As $F$ has a real place, `PTConclAt` needs $\ell\ne2$ here, which is why $\ell=3$.

**Definition.**
1. A *transport datum* is a pair of ring isomorphisms $e\colon F\to F'$ and $E\colon\Omega\to\Omega'$ with $E\circ\iota=\iota'\circ e$. This is the hypothesis `hE` of every statement read below.
2. For a valuation ring $A$ of $\Omega$, $E(A)=\{Ex:x\in A\}$ is a valuation ring of $\Omega'$, characterized by $Ex\in E(A)\iff x\in A$. In Lean it is `A.comap E.symm` (Mathlib `ValuationSubring.comap`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/Valuation/ValuationSubring.lean:858–866`).
3. Let $e_O\colon\mathcal O_F\to\mathcal O_{F'}$ be the restriction of $e$. The *place map* sends a maximal ideal $v$ of $\mathcal O_F$ to $e_V(v)=e_O(v)$.
4. An *isomorphism of topological groups* is a group isomorphism $f$ such that $f$ and $f^{-1}$ are continuous (`ContinuousMulEquiv`, written `≃ₜ*`, `Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/ContinuousMonoidHom.lean:308–319`). A group isomorphism with no continuity condition is an *abstract* isomorphism.

**Lemma N (conjugation by $E$).** [Lean, read for (1)–(4)] Let $(e,E)$ be a transport datum. Line numbers refer to `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/GaloisGroupTransportIsomorphism.lean`.
1. For $\sigma\in G_F$, $\Phi(\sigma)=E\circ\sigma\circ E^{-1}$ fixes $\iota'(F')$, so $\Phi(\sigma)\in G_{F'}$.
2. $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)=E(\sigma x)$ for all $x\in\Omega$, and $\Phi$ is the only map $G_F\to G_{F'}$ with this property.
3. $\Phi$ is a group homomorphism.
4. $(e^{-1},E^{-1})$ is a transport datum, and the map it defines, $\tau\mapsto E^{-1}\tau E$, is inverse to $\Phi$.
5. For every intermediate field $M$ of $\Omega/F$, $E(M)$ is an intermediate field of $\Omega'/F'$ with $[E(M):F']=[M:F]$, and $\Phi(\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M))=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega'/E(M))$.

**Proof.** (1) Let $y\in F'$ and write $y=e(x)$. Then $E^{-1}(\iota'(y))=\iota(x)$ by `hE`, $\sigma$ fixes $\iota(x)$, and $E(\iota(x))=\iota'(y)$. This is the hypothesis of Mathlib's `AlgEquiv.ofRingEquiv` (`Mathlib/Algebra/Algebra/Equiv.lean:627–632`), discharged in `galConjFun` (`:13–20`). (2) The identity is `galConjFun_apply` (`:22–25`). Uniqueness holds because $E$ is surjective. (3) Evaluate both sides at $Ex$ and use (2). This is `galConjHom` (`:27–40`). (4) From `hE`, $E^{-1}\circ\iota'=\iota\circ e^{-1}$ (`hE'`, `:85–87`). Both composites with $\Phi$ are the identity, by (2) evaluated at $Ex$ and at $E^{-1}y$ (`:96–107`). (5) This part is proved here and is not a Lean declaration. $E(M)$ is a subfield containing $E(\iota(F))=\iota'(F')$. $E$ restricts to a ring isomorphism $M\to E(M)$ with $E(\iota(a)m)=\iota'(e(a))E(m)$, so an $F$-basis of $M$ maps to an $F'$-basis of $E(M)$. By (2), $\sigma$ fixes $M$ pointwise exactly when $\Phi(\sigma)$ fixes $E(M)$ pointwise, and (4) gives the reverse inclusion. $\square$

Part (5) with $\Omega'=\Omega$, $e=\mathrm{id}$ and $E\in G_F$ is the classical rule $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/\sigma M)=\sigma\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)\sigma^{-1}$ [Milne, *FT*, Theorem 7.13(c), p. 98](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf).

**Lemma O (continuity for the Krull topology).** [Lean, read] $\Phi$ and $\Phi^{-1}$ are continuous. Hence $\Phi\colon G_F\to G_{F'}$ is an isomorphism of topological groups.

**Proof.** Line numbers refer to `GaloisGroupTransportIsomorphism.lean`. A homomorphism of topological groups is continuous if it is continuous at $1$ (Mathlib `continuous_of_continuousAt_one`, `Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Group/Basic.lean:860–864`). Let $s$ be a neighbourhood of $1$ in $G_{F'}$. It contains $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega'/K')$ for some $K'$ finite over $F'$ (`krullTopology_mem_nhds_one_iff`). Let $b_1,\dots,b_m$ be an $F'$-basis of $K'$ (`Module.finBasis`), and let $L$ be the field generated over $F$ by $E^{-1}b_1,\dots,E^{-1}b_m$. These elements are algebraic over $F$, so $L/F$ is finite (Mathlib `IntermediateField.finiteDimensional_adjoin`, `Mathlib/FieldTheory/IntermediateField/Adjoin/Basic.lean:590–594`), and $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L)$ is a neighbourhood of $1$ in $G_F$. If $\sigma$ fixes $L$, then $\Phi(\sigma)(b_k)=E(\sigma(E^{-1}b_k))=b_k$ by Lemma N(2). As $\Phi(\sigma)$ is $F'$-linear, it fixes $K'$ (`Basis.ext`), so $\Phi(\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L))\subseteq\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega'/K')\subseteq s$. This is `continuous_galConjHom` (`:47–73`). The inverse is the same map for $(e^{-1},E^{-1})$, and `exists_galContinuousMulEquiv` (`:77–107`) assembles the two maps, the identities of Lemma N(4) and the two continuity statements into `Φ : G F Ω ≃ₜ* G F' Ω'`. $\square$

As in the proof of Lemma N(5), $E^{-1}$ maps $K'$ onto the $F$-span of the $E^{-1}b_k$, so the field $L$ of the proof is $E^{-1}(K')$. The proof uses only that $\Omega/F$ and $\Omega'/F'$ are algebraic, through `haveI : Algebra.IsAlgebraic F Ω := IsAlgClosure.isAlgebraic` and its primed copy (`:83–84`). It uses neither that $F$ is a number field nor that $\Omega$ is algebraically closed. The Stacks Project characterizes the Krull topology as the coarsest one for which the action on the discrete field is continuous, and shows that a map from any space into the Galois group is continuous once the resulting action is [Stacks Project, Lemma 9.22.1 (0BMJ)](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BMJ). With that description continuity of $\Phi$ is immediate: $(\sigma,y)\mapsto E(\sigma(E^{-1}y))$ is continuous on $G_F\times\Omega'$, because $E$ is a bijection of discrete sets. Mathlib defines the topology through the neighbourhood basis and does not prove the universal property, as its annotation `@[stacks 0BMJ …]` records (`Mathlib/FieldTheory/KrullTopology.lean:138`), so the archive argues with neighbourhood bases.

**Lemma P (places, decomposition groups and restriction).** [Lean, read] Let $A$ be a valuation ring of $\Omega$ and $A'=E(A)$.
1. $e_V$ is a bijection from the maximal ideals of $\mathcal O_F$ to those of $\mathcal O_{F'}$, with $x\in v\iff e_O(x)\in e_V(v)$ and $N(e_V(v))=N(v)$. The ring $A$ lies over $v$ if and only if $A'$ lies over $e_V(v)$.
2. For $\sigma\in G_F$: $\sigma\in D_A\iff\Phi(\sigma)\in D_{A'}$; $\sigma\in I_A\iff\Phi(\sigma)\in I_{A'}$; and $\sigma$ is an arithmetic Frobenius element at $A$ with exponent $q$ if and only if $\Phi(\sigma)$ is one at $A'$ with exponent $q$. This holds for every map $\Phi$ with the property of Lemma N(2).
3. The restrictions of $\Phi$ and $\Phi^{-1}$ are mutually inverse continuous homomorphisms between $D_A$ and $D_{A'}$ with their subspace topologies. So $e_D=\Phi|_{D_A}\colon D_A\to D_{A'}$ is an isomorphism of topological groups, and $e_D(d)=\Phi(d)$ in $G_{F'}$.

**Proof.** (1) In `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean`, $e_O$ is `RingOfIntegers.mapRingEquiv e` and $e_V$ is `placeEquiv` (`:42–46`). The image ideal $e_O(v)$ is prime because $e_O$ is an isomorphism, and nonzero because $e_O$ is injective (`placeMap`, `:21–29`). Mapping back along $e_O^{-1}$ recovers $v$ (`placeMap_symm_placeMap`, `:34–40`). Membership is `mem_iff_apply_mem_map` (`:11–17`). The norms agree because $e_O$ induces $\mathcal O_F/v\simeq\mathcal O_{F'}/e_V(v)$ (`exists_places_of_ringEquiv`, `:48–60`). For the place condition, $v_{A'}(Ey)<1\iff v_A(y)<1$ for all $y$. Both sides say that $y=0$ or $y^{-1}$ is not in the ring, and $E$ carries one statement to the other (`RingEquivTransportsDecomposition.lean:9–16,21–23`, same directory). For $x\in\mathcal O_F$, `hE` gives $\iota'(e_O(x))=E(\iota(x))$, and $x\in v\iff e_O(x)\in e_V(v)$. So the two conditions in the definition of lying over correspond under the bijection $e_O$ (`isPlaceOver_transfer_fieldIso`, `:18–34`).

(2) This is `locHyp` (`src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/PublishedTheorems/PoitouTateDuality/MemDecompositionSubgroupIff.lean:36–62`), read in §18.3.10. Membership in $D_A$ means $\sigma x\in A\iff x\in A$ for all $x$ (`mem_decompositionSubgroup_iff`, `:10–22`). For $y=Ex$ we have $\Phi(\sigma)(y)=E(\sigma x)$, which lies in $A'$ exactly when $\sigma x\in A$. For inertia and Frobenius, $v_{A'}(Ex)\le1\iff v_A(x)\le1$ and $v_{A'}(Ex)<1\iff v_A(x)<1$ (`:24–34`), together with $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)-Ex=E(\sigma x-x)$ and $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)-(Ex)^q=E(\sigma x-x^q)$. The proof uses $\Phi$ only through the property of Lemma N(2).

(3) This is `subgroupIso` (`src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/NineTermExactIff.lean:84–92`), read in §18.3.10. It sends $a$ to $\Phi(a)$, which lies in $D_{A'}$ by (2), and $b$ to $\Phi^{-1}(b)$, which lies in $D_A$ by (2) because $\Phi(\Phi^{-1}b)=b$. Both maps are continuous, as composites of the inclusion of a subspace, $\Phi$ or $\Phi^{-1}$, and the corestriction (Mathlib `Continuous.subtype_mk`). The equality $e_D(d)=\Phi(d)$ holds by definition (`subgroupIso_coe`, `:94–96`). $\square$

For a finite Galois extension and $E\in G_F$, part (2) is the rule $G_{\tau w}=\tau G_w\tau^{-1}$ for decomposition groups that Milne states before Proposition 8.10 [Milne, *ANT*, p. 139](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf).

**Lemma Q (closed normal closures and quotients).** [Lean, read] Let $\Phi\colon A\to B$ be an isomorphism of topological groups. For a subset $X$ write $\overline{\operatorname{ncl}}(X)$ for the closure of the normal subgroup generated by $X$. Line numbers refer to `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/InertiaQuotientTransport.lean`.
1. If $X\subseteq A$, $Y\subseteq B$ and $\Phi(X)\subseteq Y$, then $\Phi(\overline{\operatorname{ncl}}(X))\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{ncl}}(Y)$.
2. If $H\trianglelefteq A$ and $K\trianglelefteq B$ satisfy $a\in H\iff\Phi(a)\in K$, then $aH\mapsto\Phi(a)K$ is an isomorphism of topological groups $A/H\to B/K$ for the quotient topologies.

**Proof.** (1) The closure $C$ of a normal subgroup is a closed normal subgroup (Mathlib `Subgroup.is_normal_topologicalClosure` and `Subgroup.isClosed_topologicalClosure`, `Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Group/Basic.lean:732,709`). So $\Phi^{-1}(C)$ is a closed normal subgroup of $A$, by continuity of $\Phi$ and `Subgroup.Normal.comap`, and it contains $X$. It therefore contains the normal subgroup generated by $X$ (`Subgroup.normalClosure_le_normal`, `Mathlib/Algebra/Group/Subgroup/Basic.lean:646`), and its closure (`Subgroup.topologicalClosure_minimal`, `Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Group/Basic.lean:713`). This is `map_mem_closedNormal` (`:11–25`). (2) The hypothesis says $\Phi(H)=K$, and Mathlib's `QuotientGroup.congr` (`Mathlib/GroupTheory/QuotientGroup/Defs.lean:396–410`) gives the group isomorphism. The composite of the induced map with the projection $A\to A/H$ is the continuous map $A\to B\to B/K$. As the projection is a quotient map (`QuotientGroup.isQuotientMap_mk`, `Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Group/Quotient.lean:40–41`), the induced map is continuous. The same argument with $\Phi^{-1}$ applies to the inverse. This is `quotientIso` (`:27–43`). $\square$

**Theorem 18.3.12 (transport of Galois groups along a field isomorphism).** [Lean, read] Let $F,F'$ be number fields with algebraic closures $\Omega,\Omega'$, let $(e,E)$ be a transport datum and $\Phi(\sigma)=E\sigma E^{-1}$.
1. $\Phi\colon G_F\to G_{F'}$ is an isomorphism of topological groups, with inverse $\tau\mapsto E^{-1}\tau E$. It is the only map with $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)=E(\sigma x)$, and $\Phi(\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M))=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega'/E(M))$ for every intermediate field $M$.
2. For every valuation ring $A$ of $\Omega$ and $A'=E(A)$, $A$ lies over $v$ if and only if $A'$ lies over $e_V(v)$. Moreover $\Phi(D_A)=D_{A'}$ and $\Phi(I_A)=I_{A'}$, $\Phi$ carries arithmetic Frobenius elements at $A$ to those at $A'$ with the same exponent, and $e_D=\Phi|_{D_A}$ is an isomorphism of topological groups $D_A\to D_{A'}$.
3. Let $T$ and $T'$ be sets of maximal ideals of $\mathcal O_F$ and $\mathcal O_{F'}$ with $v\in T\iff e_V(v)\in T'$. Then $\Phi(N_T)=N_{T'}$. There is exactly one map $\Psi\colon G_{F,T}\to G_{F',T'}$ with $\Psi\circ\pi_T=\pi_{T'}\circ\Phi$, and it is an isomorphism of topological groups.

In Lean, parts 1 and 3 are `GlobalTheory.T.galois_group_transport_isomorphism` and `GlobalTheory.T.galois_quotient_equiv_of_matching_places` (`GaloisGroupTransportIsomorphismStatement.lean:7–9` and `InertiaQuotientTransportStatement.lean:7–9`, same directory). Both state only existence, `∃ Φ : G F Ω ≃ₜ* G F' Ω', ∀ σ x, Φ σ (E x) = E (σ x)` and `∃ Ψ : GS Ω T ≃ₜ* GS Ω' T', ∀ σ, Ψ (toGS Ω T σ) = toGS Ω' T' (Φ σ)`. They are proved by `exists_galContinuousMulEquiv` and `exists_gsIso`. The second statement takes the matching of places and of inertia as hypotheses, for every pair $(A,A')$ with $Ex\in A'\iff x\in A$; `ptConcl_of_iso_of` supplies them from `ring_equiv_transports_decomposition` and `locHyp`, which is part 2. The fixed-field assertion of part 1 and the two uniqueness assertions are proved here.

**Proof.** Part 1 is Lemmas N and O, and part 2 is Lemma P. For part 3 we follow `exists_gsIso` (`InertiaQuotientTransport.lean:45–75`). Let $X\subseteq G_F$ be the union of the sets $I_A$ over all valuation rings $A$ lying over places $v\notin T$, and let $Y\subseteq G_{F'}$ be the analogous union for $T'$. Let $\tau\in I_A$ with $A$ over $v\notin T$, and put $A'=E(A)$ (`A.comap E.symm`, `:53–57`). By part 2, $A'$ lies over $e_V(v)$, which is not in $T'$, and $\Phi(\tau)\in I_{A'}$ (`:58`). So $\Phi(X)\subseteq Y$, and Lemma Q(1) gives $\Phi(N_T)\subseteq N_{T'}$ (`:47–58`). Symmetrically, for $A'$ over $v'\notin T'$ the ring $A=E^{-1}(A')$ (`A'.comap E`) lies over $e_V^{-1}(v')\notin T$, so $\Phi^{-1}(N_{T'})\subseteq N_T$ (`:59–72`). Hence $\sigma\in N_T\iff\Phi(\sigma)\in N_{T'}$ (`:73–74`), and Lemma Q(2) gives $\Psi$ with $\Psi(\pi_T\sigma)=\pi_{T'}(\Phi\sigma)$ by construction (`fun σ => rfl`, `:75`). Uniqueness holds because $\pi_T$ is surjective. $\square$

**Corollary 18.3.12.1 (the data that `ptConcl_of_iso_of` supplies).** [Lean, read] Keep the situation of the theorem. Let $S$ be a finite set of maximal ideals of $\mathcal O_F$, and let $A_v$ be a valuation ring lying over $v$ for each $v\in S$. Put $S'=e_V(S)$, $e_I=e_V|_S$ and $A'_{e_I(v)}=E(A_v)$, and let $\Psi$ be the isomorphism of part 3 for $(T,T')=(S,S')$. Let $R=\mathbb Z/\ell^r$, let $M$ be a discrete $R$-module with a continuous action $\pi$ of $G_{F,S}$ (`ρ` in the archive), let $\mu\colon G_{F,S}\to R^\times$ be a homomorphism, and put $\pi'=\pi\circ\Psi^{-1}$ and $\mu'=\mu\circ\Psi^{-1}$.
1. `eG`: $e=\Psi\colon G_{F,S}\to G_{F',S'}$ is an isomorphism of topological groups.
2. `hσ` and `hχ`: $\pi'(\Psi g)=\pi(g)$ and $\mu'(\Psi g)=\mu(g)$ for all $g$, and the action $\pi'$ is continuous.
3. `eI`: $e_I\colon S\to S'$ is a bijection, and $A'_{v'}$ lies over $v'$ for every $v'\in S'$.
4. `eD`: $e_{D,v}=\Phi|_{D_{A_v}}\colon D_{A_v}\to D_{A'_{e_I(v)}}$ is an isomorphism of topological groups for each $v\in S$.
5. `hD`: $\lambda'_{e_I(v)}(e_{D,v}(d))=\Psi(\lambda_v(d))$ for all $v\in S$ and $d\in D_{A_v}$.

Together with the continuity of the maps $\lambda_v$ and $\lambda'_{v'}$ (`continuous_locFam`), these are the hypotheses of Corollary 18.3.8.1(b) and of `shaG_transfer`, and, except for the matching of the local invariant maps, of `nineG_of_iso`.

**Proof.** Line numbers refer to `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean`. The proof obtains $\Phi$ (`:25`), the place maps (`:26`), the matching `hloc` of decomposition groups, inertia and Frobenius elements from `LocHyp` (`:27`), and the place transport `hpl` (`:28`). It sets $S'$ to the image of $S$ (`:33`) and checks $v\in S\iff e_V(v)\in S'$ (`:34–36`). The bijection $e_I$ is `Equiv.subtypeEquiv eV` (`:37`), giving (3) with `hPl'` (`:43–46`). The rings are `Av' v' = (Av (eI.symm v')).comap E.symm` (`:39`), and `hAv''` records $Ex\in A'_{e_I(v)}\iff x\in A_v$ (`:40–42`). $\Psi$ comes from part 3, applied with `hpl` and the inertia clause of `hloc` (`:48–49`); this gives (1). The transported character and representation are $\mu'=\mu\circ\Psi^{-1}$ and $\pi'=\pi\circ\Psi^{-1}$ (`:50–51,59–60`), and `hρ'` composes the continuity of $\pi$ with that of $\Psi^{-1}$ (`:61`). The two equations of (2) are `hχ` and `hρc`, proved by `simp` from these definitions (`:88–89`). The isomorphisms of (4) are `eD v := subgroupIso Φ _ _ (fun σ => (hloc _ _ (hAv'' v) σ).1)` (`:82–83`), that is, Lemma P(3) with the decomposition clause of `hloc` at the pair $(A_v,E(A_v))$. For (5), unfolding `locFam`, `locHom` and `subgroupIso_coe` turns $\lambda'_{e_I(v)}(e_{D,v}(d))$ into $\pi_{S'}(\Phi(d))$, which equals $\Psi(\pi_S(d))=\Psi(\lambda_v(d))$ by the defining property of $\Psi$. In Lean this is `hcomp` (`:84–87`), proved by `(hΨ d).symm`. $\square$

The remaining hypotheses of `PTConclAt F' Ω'`, applied at `:80`, are not part of $e_D$. They are the cyclotomic normalization `hμ'` (`:52–58`), the condition on $\ell$ (`:63`), the primitive root $E\zeta$ (`:64`), the normalized invariant family `inv'` (`:65–73`) and `ContainsAbove` for $S'$ (`:74–79`). They come from `ring_equiv_cyclotomic_character`, `totally_complex_of_ring_equiv`, `ring_equiv_transports_inertia` and `ring_equiv_transports_residue` (`:29–31,63,72`), whose proofs are not read. The concrete conclusions are by definition the abstract ones at $\phi_v=\lambda_v$: `NineTermExact` is `NineG` and `ShaDual` is `ShaG` (`nineTermExact_iff` and `shaDual_iff`, both `Iff.rfl`, `NineTermExactIff.lean:75–80`). This is how the data reach the two consumers. `poitou_tate_sequence_transport` (`:91–93`) receives $\Psi$, $e_I$, $e_D$, `hcomp`, `hχ` and `hρc`. It also needs the local invariant maps to match, $j'_{e_I(v)}[z']=j_v[z]$ whenever $z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=z(g,h)$ for all $g,h$. This is supplied at `:94–101` by the output `hinv'` of `ring_equiv_transports_inertia` (`:30–31`), whose proof is not read; there `Subtype.ext hg` identifies a given element $g'$ of the target with $e_D(g)$. `tate_shafarevich_transport` (`:102–103`) receives the same six data, and its proof `shaG_transfer` was read in §18.3.10. In the proof of the nine-term transport, `nineG_of_iso` (`PoitouTateSequenceTransport.lean:224–261`, in the directory of Lemma Q), $\Psi$ gives the global isomorphisms `equivH0OfIso eG` to `equivH2OfIso eG` for the module and its twisted dual (`:254–256`). $e_D$ gives the local isomorphisms `P0`–`P2` (`:125–132`), built from `equivH0OfIso`, `equivH1OfIso` and `equivH2OfIso` along `eD i` with the local compatibility `loc_transfer` (`:118–123`), which is derived from `hD` and `hσ` (named `hc` there). The squares of the ladder `nine_ladder` are `local_degree_zero_square` to `trace_pairing_degree_two_square` (`:257–261`). All six take `eD`, four of them also take `hD`, and the three trace-pairing squares take the invariant matching `hj`; their proofs are not read. The dualizing step `dual_sq` is proved inline (`:245–253`). `equivH1OfIso` uses the continuity of both $e_D$ and $e_D^{-1}$ to map continuous cochains in both directions (`src/ReciprocityTheory/NeutralLocalFields/LocalFieldComparison/TransportA.lean:18–22,47–61`). The cohomology moves from the model $(F',\Omega')$ to $(F,\Omega)$: `equivH1OfIso eG ρ' ρ hc` maps $H^1(G_{F',S'},M)$ to $H^1(G_{F,S},M)$.

**Remark (compatible completions).** [Expository] Let $j$ be an adapted embedding for $A$ in the sense of §18.3.8. Then $j'=j\circ E^{-1}$ is adapted for $A'=E(A)$, $K_{j'}=K_j$, and $i_{j'}(\Phi g)=i_j(g)$ for $g\in D_A$. Indeed $x\in E(A)\iff\lvert j(E^{-1}x)\rvert\le1$, and $K_{j'}=\mathbb Q_p(j(E^{-1}\iota'(F')))=\mathbb Q_p(j(\iota(F)))$ by `hE`. Also $i_j(g)\circ j'=j\circ g\circ E^{-1}=j'\circ\Phi(g)$, and uniqueness in Theorem 18.3.8(2) gives the formula. Any other adapted embedding for $A'$ is $\theta\circ j'$ with $\theta\in G_{\mathbb Q_p}$ (Lemma 1(4) of §18.3.8), and then $i_{\theta j'}(\Phi g)=\theta\,i_j(g)\,\theta^{-1}$ by Theorem 18.3.8(6). So under compatible completions $e_D$ is the identity of $G_{K_j}$. In general it is conjugation by an element of $G_{\mathbb Q_p}$ that need not lie in $G_{K_j}$, as both examples show. `ptConcl_of_iso_of` never chooses completions, and `decEquiv` plays no role in it. The remark is proved here and is not a Lean declaration.

**Remark (absolute groups and finite layers).** [Expository] The theorem is a statement about the absolute groups $G_F$ and $G_{F'}$, their decomposition groups and the quotients $G_{F,S}$, all of them infinite. The examples see only finite quotients. For a finite Galois extension $L/F$ in $\Omega$, the extension $E(L)/F'$ is finite Galois by Lemma N(5) and Theorem 7.13(d) of [Milne, *FT*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf), and Lemma N(2) gives
$$\Phi(\sigma)|_{E(L)}=E|_L\circ\sigma|_L\circ(E|_L)^{-1}.$$
So $\Phi$ induces isomorphisms $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)\to\operatorname{Gal}(E(L)/F')$ compatible with restriction, and these are what the examples compute. They determine $\Phi$, since an element of $G_F$ is determined by its restrictions to finite Galois subextensions. They depend only on $E|_L$, however, and they do not see the decomposition groups themselves. $D_{A_0}$ is isomorphic to $G_{\mathbb Q_5}$ by Theorem 18.3.8, while its image in $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ has two elements. And replacing $E$ by $E\circ g$ with $g\in\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L)$ leaves every computation on $L$ unchanged but replaces $E(A_v)$ by $E(gA_v)$, which may be a different valuation ring of $\Omega'$ over the same prime of $E(L)$.

**Remark (what is canonical).** [Expository] `galois_group_transport_isomorphism` asserts only that some $\Phi$ with $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)=E(\sigma x)$ exists, and `ptConcl_of_iso_of` obtains it by `obtain ⟨Φ, hΦ⟩` (`:25`). By Lemma N(2) any such $\Phi$ is conjugation by $E$, and by Theorem 18.3.12(3) $\Psi$ is determined by $\Phi$. So nothing is lost by the existential, and `locHyp` needs only `hΦ`. The freedom lies in $E$, and in the family $(A_v)$, over which `PTConclAt` is universally quantified (`src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/TemperedRepresentationTheory/GelfandSpectralNormalisation/NormalisedInvariants/PTConclAt.lean:10–22`). Every other extension $E_1$ of $e$ is $E\circ g$ for a unique $g\in G_F$, namely $g=E^{-1}E_1$. Then $\Phi$ changes to $\sigma\mapsto\Phi(g\sigma g^{-1})$, $\Psi$ to its composite with conjugation by $\pi_S(g)$, and $E(A_v)$ to $E(gA_v)$. Milne notes in the same way that the absolute Galois group is defined only up to inner automorphism [Milne, *FT*, p. 96, footnote 4](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/FT.pdf). The transported module changes by the isomorphism $\pi(\pi_S(g))^{-1}$, and Milne's Proposition 0.15 says that conjugation together with this map induces the identity on cohomology [Milne, *Arithmetic Duality Theorems*, Proposition 0.15 and the following paragraph, p. 11](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf), as in Corollary 18.3.8.1(c). The archive needs none of this, because the conclusion `PTConclAt F Ω` is a proposition and every choice proves it. On the main path the choice is made once. `ptConclAt_all_of` takes $K$, $e$ and $E$ from `exists_subfield_model` (`src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/ExistsSubfieldModel.lean:6–20`). There $K$ is the image of the embedding $F\to\overline{\mathbb Q}$ produced by `IsAlgClosed.lift` ([§6.4.15](06-stepanovs-method-and-multiplicity.md#6415-extending-a-real-embedding-to-the-host-field-the-algebraically-closed-lift)), $e$ is the corestriction, and $E$ is Mathlib's `IsAlgClosure.equivOfEquiv` for $\Omega$ and $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ (`Mathlib/FieldTheory/IsAlgClosed/Basic.lean:469–480`). Milne's set-up of the Poitou–Tate sequence likewise chooses, for each prime, an embedding $K^s\to K_v^s$, which identifies $G_v$ with a decomposition group [Milne, *ADT*, I §4, p. 48](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf). The archive's counterpart is the family $(A_v)$, and it transports every member by the same $E$.

**Remark (continuous versus abstract isomorphisms).** [Expository] Galois cohomology here is continuous cochain cohomology, so precomposition with $e$ or $e_{D,v}$ must preserve continuity of cochains, and the inverse transport needs the inverse to be continuous as well (`TransportA.lean:47–61`). For compact Hausdorff groups the continuity of the inverse would follow from that of the map, as in Step 6 of Theorem 18.3.8. The archive does not use this: `subgroupIso` takes the continuity of the inverse from $\Phi^{-1}$, and `quotientIso` from the quotient map. Example (F5) below shows that abstract isomorphisms do not suffice. The transport of the module is also built in. The archive keeps the same $M$ and moves the action, $\pi'=\pi\circ\Psi^{-1}$. When $M$ is a module of roots of unity, $E\colon\mu(\Omega)\to\mu(\Omega')$ is an isomorphism from $(M,\pi')$ to the natural module, because $\Phi(\sigma)(E\xi)=E(\sigma\xi)$.

**Failure examples.**
- *(F1) Without `hE`.* In the second example take $F=F'=\mathbb Q(\vartheta)$ and $e=\mathrm{id}$, keeping $E$. Then $E\circ\iota\ne\iota\circ e$, because $E(\vartheta)=\zeta\vartheta$. Conjugation by $E$ sends $\kappa$ to $\rho^2\kappa$ on $L_2$, and $\rho^2\kappa$ moves $\vartheta$ to $\zeta^2\vartheta$. So $\sigma\mapsto E\sigma E^{-1}$ maps $G_F$ onto $G_{E(F)}=G_{\mathbb Q(\zeta\vartheta)}$, not into $G_F$. In Lean the compatibility is the hypothesis discharged in `galConjFun` (`GaloisGroupTransportIsomorphism.lean:16–20`); without it the conjugate is not an element of `Ω' ≃ₐ[F'] Ω'`.
- *(F2) Mixed directions.* In the first example the ring $E^{-1}(A_0)$ also lies over $(2-i)$, because $e^{-1}=e$, but its Frobenius element on $L$ is $\rho\kappa$. So $D_{E^{-1}(A_0)}$ restricts to $\{1,\rho\kappa\}$ on $L$, while $\Phi(D_{A_0})$ restricts to $\{1,\rho^2\kappa\}$, and the hypothesis of `subgroupIso` fails for the pair $(D_{A_0},D_{E^{-1}(A_0)})$. Using $E^{-1}$ both in $\Phi$ and in the rings would be the legitimate transport along $(e^{-1},E^{-1})$. The failure comes from mixing the two directions, which `hAv''` (`PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:40–42`) rules out.
- *(F3) Separately chosen rings.* Choose the target ring at $(2-i)$ independently of $E$, say $\hat\rho(E(A_0))$ with $\hat\rho\in G_F$ extending $\rho$. On $L$ it gives the same prime as $E^{-1}(A_0)$, because $\rho E=E^{-1}$ on $L$, so (F2) applies to it. The map $\sigma\mapsto\hat\rho\,\Phi(\sigma)\,\hat\rho^{-1}$ does carry $D_{A_0}$ onto $D_{\hat\rho E(A_0)}$. But `hD` must hold with one global isomorphism for all places of $S$ at once. Suppose it has the form $\operatorname{inn}(\pi_{S'}(k))\circ\Psi$ with $k\in G_{F'}$, and keep $E(A_\eta)=A_\eta$ and $e_D=\Phi$ at $(1+i)$. As $D_{A_\eta}$ maps onto $\operatorname{Gal}(L/F)\simeq S_3$, `hD` at $(1+i)$ forces $k|_L$ to commute with all of $S_3$, so $k|_L=1$. At $(2-i)$ it forces $k|_L\,\rho^2\kappa\,k|_L^{-1}=\rho\,\rho^2\kappa\,\rho^{-1}=\rho\kappa$, which is false for $k|_L=1$. Transporting every $A_v$ by the same $E$ is what lets one $\Psi$ serve all places.
- *(F4) Index sets that do not match.* In the first example keep $S=\{(1+i),(3),(2+i)\}$ on the source side but use $T''=S$ on the target side instead of $S'$; so $(2+i)\in T''$ and $(2-i)\notin T''$, and the hypothesis `hT` of `exists_gsIso` fails (`InertiaQuotientTransport.lean:45`, used at `:58,68`). Then $\Phi(N_S)\not\subseteq N_{T''}$, so no map $\Psi$ with $\Psi\circ\pi_S=\pi_{T''}\circ\Phi$ exists. Let $M=F(y)$ with $y^2=2+i$. The polynomial $X^2-(2+i)$ is Eisenstein at $(2+i)$, so $(2+i)$ is totally ramified in $M$ with residue field $\mathbb F_5$ [Milne, *ANT*, Proposition 3.53, p. 66](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf). The basis $1,y$ of $\mathcal O_F[y]$ has discriminant $4(2+i)$, so no place outside $\{(1+i),(2+i)\}\subseteq T''$ ramifies [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 3.35, p. 60](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf), and the setting gives $N_{T''}\subseteq\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)$. Now let $A'$ lie over $(2+i)$, and choose $g\in G_F$ with $g(y)=-y$. The rings $g(A')$ and $A'$ lie over the only prime $P$ of $M$ above $(2+i)$, so $hg(A')=A'$ for some $h\in\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)$ by Theorem 18.3.10 over $M$. Then $d=hg$ lies in $D_{A'}$ and $d(y)=-y$. By Lemma L of §18.3.11 over $M$, every automorphism of $B/\mathfrak p_{A'}$ over $\mathcal O_M/P=\mathbb F_5$ is induced by some $u\in\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)$ with $u(\mathfrak p_{A'})=\mathfrak p_{A'}$, and such a $u$ lies in $D_{A'}$ by Lemmas G(4) and H(2) of §18.3.10. Choose $u$ with the same residue automorphism as $d$. Then $t=du^{-1}$ lies in $D_{A'}$ and acts trivially on $B/\mathfrak p_{A'}$, so $t\in I_{A'}$ by the remark on inertia in two languages in §18.3.11, and $t(y)=-y$. The ring $E^{-1}(A')$ lies over $e_V^{-1}((2+i))=(2-i)\notin S$, so $\Phi^{-1}(t)\in I_{E^{-1}(A')}\subseteq N_S$ by Lemma P. But $t\notin\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)\supseteq N_{T''}$.
- *(F5) An abstract isomorphism.* Let $\Gamma=\prod_{n\ge1}\mathbb Z/2$, a profinite group, and let $\mathcal U$ be a non-principal ultrafilter on the positive integers; it exists by Zorn's lemma. Let $\chi(x)$ be the value that the coordinates $x_n$ take for $n$ in a set belonging to $\mathcal U$. If $x_n=a$ on $U_1\in\mathcal U$ and $y_n=b$ on $U_2\in\mathcal U$, then $(x+y)_n=a+b$ on $U_1\cap U_2\in\mathcal U$, so $\chi$ is a homomorphism. It is not continuous. A continuous homomorphism to the discrete group $\mathbb Z/2$ vanishes on a basic open subgroup $\{x:x_1=\dots=x_k=0\}$, but the element with $x_n=1$ exactly for $n>k$ lies in that subgroup and has $\chi(x)=1$, because $\mathcal U$ contains every cofinite set. Put $\alpha(x)=x+\chi(x)e_1$, where $e_1$ is the first unit vector. Then $\alpha$ is a homomorphism and $\alpha(\alpha(x))=x+\chi(x)\chi(e_1)e_1=x$, because $\{1\}\notin\mathcal U$. So $\alpha$ is an abstract automorphism of $\Gamma$, equal to its own inverse. The first coordinate $p_1$ is a continuous homomorphism, a class in $H^1(\Gamma,\mathbb Z/2)$ for the trivial action, but $p_1\circ\alpha=p_1+\chi$ is not continuous. So precomposition with $\alpha$ does not even preserve continuous $1$-cocycles, and no transport of continuous cohomology as in Corollary 18.3.8.1(a)–(b) is possible along $\alpha$.

**Remark (formal details).** [Lean, read]
- The two statement files are wrappers `first | exact @… | …` under `set_option maxHeartbeats 1000000` (`GaloisGroupTransportIsomorphismStatement.lean:7–9`, `InertiaQuotientTransportStatement.lean:7–9`). The names `galConjFun`, `galConjHom`, `exists_galContinuousMulEquiv`, `map_mem_closedNormal`, `quotientIso`, `exists_gsIso`, `subgroupIso` and `isPlaceOver_transfer_fieldIso` live in `GlobalTheory.CompanionTheory.PTIso`, and the place maps in its subnamespace `Places`.
- `exists_gsIso` introduces the hypotheses `hE` and `hΦ` as `_` and never uses them (`InertiaQuotientTransport.lean:46`). It uses $\Phi$ only as an isomorphism of topological groups, together with the matchings `hP` and `hI` and the condition `hT`.
- The archive's global isomorphism `eG` is $\Psi$ on the quotients, and `eD` is $\Phi$ on subgroups of the absolute groups. Neither is obtained from the other alone; `hD` is the link, and it holds by unfolding definitions.
- `ring_equiv_transports_decomposition` (`RingEquivTransportsDecompositionStatement.lean:7–9`) is, despite its name, the transport of places (`IsPlaceOver`). The decomposition groups are matched by `locHyp`, which `ptConcl_of_iso_of` receives as the hypothesis `hL : LocHyp` (`PtfsHyp.lean:12–13`). `locHyp_holds` discharges it (`PoitouTateNormalisedOfNine.lean:11`), and `Bridge.lean:14–28` passes it on, as recorded in §18.3.10. Likewise `ring_equiv_transports_inertia` produces the transported family of local invariant maps, not a matching of inertia groups.
- $N_T$ is generated by inertia at finite places only, because $T$ ranges over `HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F)`. No condition is imposed at the archimedean places, so $G_{F,S}$ plays the role of Milne's $G_{S\cup S_\infty}$ [Milne, *ADT*, I §4, p. 48](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf); we use only the inclusion proved in the setting.
- The statements carry `[NumberField F]` and `[IsAlgClosure F Ω]`, but `exists_galContinuousMulEquiv` uses only algebraicity. The number field structure enters through `HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F)` in $N_T$, in the place map and in `IsPlaceOver`.
- The names `NS` and `GS` are not unique in the archive. `Neutral.B1` has its own `G`, `NS` and `GS` (`src/ReciprocityTheory/NeutralLocalFields/Duality.lean:938,999,1009`), and `Formalism.MainNotions.V.Lang` has `NS` and `GS` (`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/ValuationFrobeniusEpsilon/ScaledValuations/ValuationApplyLtOneIff.lean:124,134`). The files read here open `ReciprocityTheory.Gal` and neither of these namespaces, so their `NS` and `GS` are those of `Global.lean:128–141`.

### 18.3.13 Pulling back local invariants and trace pairings

**Motivation.** The nine-term sequence has three trace arrows. Each uses a cup product followed by a local invariant and a sum over the places. Transporting the groups and their representations gives a way to pull back the two inputs to a cup product. A scalar computed from the resulting class must also agree with the scalar on the other side. The archive calls this agreement `hj`. It has a simple source: given a family of linear invariants on the unprimed side, define the primed family by precomposing with pullback in degree two. This determines which local ring supplies the invariant for a given primed place. We use $\Phi$, $\Psi$ and $e_D$ from §18.3.12.

**Example 1 — a ramified character paired with an unramified one.** Put $F=F'=\mathbb Q(i)$ and let $e(i)=-i$. Take $v=(2+i)$, $v'=(2-i)$ and $\pi=2+i$, $\pi'=e(\pi)$. The embeddings into $\mathbb Q_5$ send $i$ to $\iota$ and $-\iota$, respectively, where $\iota^2=-1$ and $\iota\equiv3\pmod5$ by Hensel's lemma. In these coordinates the completion map is the identity. Both uniformizers have the same local value $2+\iota$, of valuation one since $(2+\iota)(2-\iota)=5$ and $2-\iota$ is a unit with residue $4$. Choose an extension $E$ as in §18.3.12 and take the target embedding to be the source embedding composed with $E^{-1}$, so that the associated rings are $A$ and $E(A)$.

For $K=F_v$, the quadratic extension $L=K(t)$ with $t^2=\pi$ is Eisenstein. With $v_L(t)=1$, the valuations of $a$ and $bt$ for $a,b\in K$ have opposite parity whenever both terms are nonzero. Thus $\mathcal O_L=\mathcal O_K+\mathcal O_Kt$, its maximal ideal is $(t)$ and reduction sends $a+bt$ to $\bar a\in\mathbb F_5$. The nonidentity automorphism $s(t)=-t$ acts trivially on this quotient. The decomposition and inertia groups of this finite extension are both $\{1,s\}$. On the primed side $t'=Et$ has square $\pi'$ and the same computations give $s'(t')=-t'$ and residue field $\mathbb F_5$. The map on inertia sends $s$ to $s'$ and the residue map is the identity on $\mathbb F_5$.

Take $R=\mathbb F_2$, $M=R$ with trivial action, and identify $\mu_2$ with $R$ by sending $-1$ to $1$. Let $k_\pi(g)$ be the exponent of $-1$ in $g(t)/t$, and let $u$ be the unramified character sending arithmetic Frobenius to $1$. The local degree-one pairing is represented by $z(g,h)=k_\pi(g)u(h)$, with the twisted-dual class in the first slot. Here the local invariant of $z$ is $1$ in $R$. Indeed $u=k_2$, since $X^2-2$ has no root in $\mathbb F_5$ and its two roots in $\mathbb F_{25}$ are exchanged by fifth powers. The Kummer cup has nontrivial Hilbert symbol if and only if $2$ is outside the norm group of $K(t)/K$. A preimage of the unit $2$ would be a unit, because the valuation of a quadratic norm here is $v_L$. A unit norm $a^2-\pi b^2$ has square residue, whereas $2$ is a nonsquare modulo $5$. The nonzero Brauer invariant $1/2$ therefore gives $1$ in $R$. This cup also has the order and value imposed by the archive's `IsNormalised` (see below). At the target, $k_{\pi'}(e_Dg)=k_\pi(g)$ follows from the action on $Et$, and $u'(e_Dh)=u(h)$ follows from the residue action. Hence $z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=z(g,h)$ as functions. The target norm calculation gives the same value $1$. [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, III §4, pp. 112–114](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf)

**Example 2 — the unramified quadratic extension and the full pairing on square classes.** Keep the two matched places, and use $L=K(b)$ with $b^2=2$. The quadratic polynomial is irreducible and separable modulo $5$. For integral $a,c$ with at least one unit, $a^2-2c^2$ is a unit, since the corresponding residue polynomial has no nontrivial zero. Factoring out a power of $\pi$ now gives $v_K(N(a+cb))=2\min(v_K(a),v_K(c))$ for nonzero $a+cb$ (use $v_K(0)=+\infty$). The ring of integers is $\mathcal O_K[b]$, its maximal ideal is generated by $\pi$ and the residue field is $\mathbb F_5[\bar b]$ with $\bar b^2=2$. We have $\bar b^5=4\bar b=-\bar b$. The nonidentity element $f(b)=-b$ induces arithmetic Frobenius, so the decomposition group has order two and its inertia is trivial. For $b'=Eb$, the residue isomorphism takes $a+c\bar b$ to $a+c\bar b'$ and $f$ to $f'$. In particular the pulled-back unramified character agrees on both Frobenius cosets. These are calculations on finite quotients of the local absolute groups.

Pair $u=k_2$ with $k_\pi$ in this order. The resulting cocycle is $w(g,h)=u(g)k_\pi(h)$. The valuation of every norm from the unramified extension is even, so $\pi$ is outside the norm group. The pairing again gives $1$ at each place. In fact $w$ and the previous $z$ differ by the coboundary of the function $g\mapsto u(g)k_\pi(g)$, which follows by expanding the product at $gh$ in characteristic two. With the matched target inputs, $w'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=w(g,h)$ even before taking the quotient.

Every square class of $K^\times$ is represented by $\pi^a2^b$ with $a,b\in\{0,1\}$. This follows by separating the valuation and using Hensel's lemma for units with square residue. The diagonal value for $\pi$ is zero: $N(t)=-\pi$ and a square root of $-1$ in $K$ has norm $-1$, so $\pi$ is itself a norm. The diagonal for $2$ is zero by the same argument with $b$ in place of $t$. Thus in the ordered bases $(k_\pi,u)$ and $(k_{\pi'},u')$, both pairings are $(a,b),(c,d)\mapsto ad+bc$ in $R$. For example $(k_\pi+u,k_\pi)$ gives $1$ and $(k_\pi+u,k_\pi+u)$ gives $0$ on both sides. These scalar checks use the invariant of the absolute local group. The finite quadratic groups were used to identify characters and test norms, without replacing the absolute degree-two cohomology by their degree-two cohomology. [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, III Theorem 4.4 and its proof](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf)

**Definitions and the role of residue fields.** Write $D_A=\operatorname{Dec}(A)$, $I_A=\operatorname{Ine}(A)$, and $k_A=A/\mathfrak m_A$. The subgroup $D_A$ acts on $k_A$ because it acts on $A$ and preserves its nonunits. Its kernel is $I_A$: for $x\in A$, equality of residue classes of $\sigma x$ and $x$ is exactly the strict valuation inequality in `Inertia` (`Formalism/ConjugateSelfDual/Statement.lean:25–29`, `ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/Global.lean:15–18,27–34,45–60`). The same residue test with $x^q$ is `IsFrob`.

For the matched rings, $E$ induces $\bar E:k_A\to k_{A'}$ because it bijects their units as well as their elements. On residues, $\bar E(\overline{\sigma x})=\overline{\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)}$ by the defining compatibility from §18.3.12. Consequently the two actions are intertwined by $\bar E$ and $e_D$ and $e_D(I_A)=I_{A'}$. Also $\bar E(\bar x^q)=\bar E(\bar x)^q$, which preserves the same arithmetic-Frobenius exponent. These are the residue interpretations of the two latter clauses of `LocHyp` (`PtfsHyp.lean:12–13`). In `ptConcl_of_iso_of`, inertia enters the formation of $\Psi$ outside $S$ via `NS`, and the normalization check uses the separate residue-transport interface. The local-invariant producer itself receives the **decomposition** clause of `LocHyp`.

For $R=\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z$, put $\chi_A=\mathrm{cycMod}|_{D_A}$ and let $R_A$ denote $R$ with the scalar action by $\chi_A$. The archive calls this `muLoc` and calls the type of all families $A\mapsto J_A$ with $J_A:H^2(D_A,R_A)\to R$ linear `InvFam`. A local trace here applies such a linear functional to a cup-product class. The normalization separately asks for value $1$ on a cocycle $\kappa(g)\chi(h)$ with $\kappa$ read from a chosen root of a uniformizer and $\chi$ additive, trivial on inertia and taking value $1$ on arithmetic Frobenius. The supplied family may be arbitrary in the producer. (`PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:17–37`.)

**Lemma R (pullback of continuous degree-two classes).** Let $d:D\to D'$ be a continuous group isomorphism with continuous inverse. Suppose actions on the same topological $R$-module satisfy $\alpha'(dg)m=\alpha(g)m$. Then $d^*$ gives a linear isomorphism from $H^2(D',\alpha')$ to $H^2(D,\alpha)$, taking $[z']$ to $[(g,h)\mapsto z'(dg,dh)]$.

**Proof.** Continuity is composition with $d\times d$. The four terms of the cocycle identity for the composite are $\alpha(g)z'(dh,dk)-z'(d(gh),dk)+z'(dg,d(hk))-z'(dg,dh)$. They are the four terms of the primed identity at $(dg,dh,dk)$, by compatibility and multiplicativity. The pullback of the coboundary of a continuous one-cochain $c'$ in the given coefficient module is the coboundary of $c'\circ d$, by the three-term differential $\alpha(g)c(h)-c(gh)+c(g)$. This gives the quotient map. With $d^{-1}$ the compatibility follows by substitution. The composites on representatives evaluate $d^{-1}d$ and $dd^{-1}$ in each variable, hence are identities. In the archive, `compatiblePairCocycles2` checks the four terms using the coefficient map, `compatiblePairH2` supplies the continuous coboundary witness to `Submodule.mapQ`, and `equivH2OfIso` checks inverse identities by quotient extensionality. See `SeparatedDvrSecondCohomologyMem.lean:68–102,244–265` and `TransportA.lean:15–30,63–83`.

**Theorem 18.3.13 (the conditional input to `hj`) [Lean, read].** Use the number fields, closures and compatible isomorphisms $e,E,\Phi$ of §18.3.12. Suppose for every pair $A,A'$ satisfying $Ex\in A'\iff x\in A$ that $\sigma\in D_A\iff\Phi(\sigma)\in D_{A'}$. For a prime $\ell$ and $r\ge0$, assume $\mathrm{cycMod}'(\Phi\sigma)=\mathrm{cycMod}(\sigma)$ for every $\sigma$. Given any $J\in\mathrm{InvFam}$, there exists $J'\in\mathrm{InvFam}'$ such that for each matched pair
$J'_{A'}=J_A\circ e_D^*$.
In particular, bijectivity of $J_A$ implies bijectivity of $J'_{A'}$, and if $z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=z(g,h)$ for every $g,h\in D_A$, then $J'_{A'}([z'])=J_A([z])$. The archive phrases the representative test using all $g',h'$ whose underlying absolute automorphisms are $\Phi(g),\Phi(h)$. This is equivalent because these underlying automorphisms determine the subtypes uniquely.

**Proof.** The statement file forwards directly to `InvTransport.exists_invFam` (`RingEquivTransportsInertiaStatement.lean:8–9`). In the production body, first fix $A'$ and form $A_0=A'.\mathrm{comap}(E)$, so $x\in A_0$ is definitionally $Ex\in A'$. Apply the decomposition hypothesis to $A_0,A'$ to restrict $\Phi$ by `subgroupIso`. For $g\in D_{A_0}$ this gives $\chi'_{A'}(e_Dg)=\chi_{A_0}(g)$ by the cyclotomic-character hypothesis. Since `charRep_apply` is multiplication by this scalar, the coefficient map in Lemma R is the identity. Define $J'_{A'}$ by composition with the linear map underlying the isomorphism of Lemma R. For an arbitrary matched pair $A,A'$, extensionality gives $A=A_0$. The first conclusion is composition of bijections. For the second, `equivH2OfIso_mk` replaces the pulled-back class by the class of the pulled-back representative. The producer checks equality inside the cocycle subtype by function extensionality at a pair $(g,h)$ using $g'=e_Dg$, $h'=e_Dh$. No choice of a representative of inertia or of a Frobenius lift enters this construction. (`RingEquivTransportsInertia.lean:11–42`, `GalCohDefs.lean:21–27`.)

**Filling the consumer slot.** At $v\in S$ put $A=A_v$, $A'=A'_{e_I(v)}$, $\lambda_v=\mathrm{locFam}(v)$ and use the corresponding primed map. By construction $A'=E(A)$ (`PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:33–46`). The transport consumer uses the local actions obtained by restricting the global quotient characters $\mu,\mu'$ along $\lambda_v,\lambda'_{e_I(v)}$. These agree pointwise with the respective actions $R_A,R'_{A'}$: `locFam_apply_eq` substitutes $\mu\circ\mathrm{toGS}=\mathrm{cycMod}$ and evaluates the scalar. The linear coefficient map $1_R$ sends each continuous cocycle to the identical function for this new action (`cocycles2Map`). It descends by sending the coboundary witness $c$ to the same function $c$. The trace map in the sequence is `invAt`, the composite of this quotient map `toMuLoc` with $J_A$.

The actual `hj` goal takes $z,z'$ in the two quotient-character cocycle spaces and assumes equality on pairs $(g,h)$ and $(e_Dg,e_Dh)$. Lines 94–101 of `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean` apply the second component of `hinv'` to their images under the two identity-coefficient maps, identify $g',h'$ with $e_Dg,e_Dh$ by subtype extensionality, then return the original pointwise hypothesis. Thus, with $j_v=\mathrm{invAt}(v)$, the supplied equality is precisely $j'_{e_I(v)}([z'])=j_v([z])$ (`PoitouTateSequenceTransport.lean:165–169,224–240`).

| Input | Consumer step | Output |
|---|---|---|
| $A'=E(A)$, decomposition matching, cyclotomic-character equality and $J$ | `ring_equiv_transports_inertia` at `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:29–31` | $J'$ and `hinv'` |
| Inertia matching from `hloc` and the matched finite place sets | Quotient transport at lines 48–49 | $\Psi$ from §18.3.12 |
| Bijectivity clause of `hinv'` and the residue normalization interface | Lines 65–73 | `hnorm'` needed by the primed conclusion |
| Representative clause of `hinv'`, `locFam_apply_eq` and $1_R$ on cocycles | Lines 90–101 | `hj` for `poitou_tate_sequence_transport` |
| `hj` | `nineG_of_iso` assembly, `PoitouTateSequenceTransport.lean:254–261` | Input to the three trace-pairing squares |

The local square, composed with the change of coefficient action on either side, is

$$\begin{array}{ccc}
 H^2(D'_{A'},R'_{A'}) & \xrightarrow{e_D^*} & H^2(D_A,R_A) \\
 J'_{A'}\downarrow & & \downarrow J_A \\
 R & \xrightarrow{1_R} & R
\end{array}$$

The upper arrow evaluates a primed representative on unprimed inputs via $e_D$. The maps from the quotient-character cohomologies into the top row are the two versions of `toMuLoc`.

**Why this is the trace-pairing input.** With $\lambda' e_D=\Psi\lambda$, the global coefficient compatibility gives local compatibility for $M$ and its cyclotomic twisted dual, whose action on a functional $f$ is $g\cdot f:m\mapsto\chi(g)f(g^{-1}m)$. Evaluation is equivariant. In degree $(1,1)$, with the dual cocycle $x$ first and the ordinary cocycle $y$ second, the representative evaluates $(g,h)$ as $x(g)(g\cdot y(h))$. Pullback commutes with this formula since the actions agree at $g$ and $e_Dg$. In degrees $(0,2)$ and $(2,0)$ the degree-zero argument is invariant, and the representative is simply evaluation of the two arguments with the degree-two argument at $(g,h)$. Substitution again commutes. Thus the cup representatives give exactly the hypothesis of `hj`. For a global argument, restriction commutes with pullback by $\lambda' e_D=\Psi\lambda$. Applying `hj` term by term and reindexing the finite sum by $e_I$ preserves the scalar trace pairing. This explains the three uses of the same input at representative level. (`GalCohDefs.lean:67–81,100–111`, `ContinuousConstSMulQuotient.lean:30–34,86–88,118–120`.)

**Failure with an unmatched place.** Keep the source $v=(2+i)$ but use the original embedding $i\mapsto\iota$ for the target as well. The target radicand $\pi'=2-i$ now has residue $4$ modulo $5$, so it is a square in $\mathbb Q_5$ by Hensel's lemma. Its local Kummer character is zero, whereas the source $k_\pi$ is nonzero on inertia. Pairing with $u$ gives $0$ at this wrong target and $1$ at the source. This exhibits both loss of the finite inertia action and failure of the representative hypothesis for those two cups. The two completions are abstractly $\mathbb Q_5$ in both pairings, so equality of the completion types alone does not repair the error.

**Failure with incompatible actions.** Even at the cochain level continuity and multiplicativity of the group map are insufficient. For the identity on the discrete group $C_2=\{1,s\}$ and coefficients $\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z$, use the trivial action on the primed side and $s\mapsto-1$ on the unprimed side. The primed cocycle with only $z'(s,s)=1$ nonzero satisfies the cocycle identity. After precomposition its unprimed differential at $(s,s,s)$ is $-1-1\ne0$. This explains the action equality required for the identity coefficient map.

**Reading boundary.** The production body, degree-two quotient maps and concrete handoff supplying `hj` are [Lean, read]. The remaining statement-level inputs are `ring_equiv_cyclotomic_character`, `ring_equiv_transports_residue` and `totally_complex_of_ring_equiv`. The producer of the normalized invariants and the producer of `PtfsHyp` remain [Open] for this reading, as do `nine_ladder`, `exact_transfer`, the three `local_degree_*_square` and the three `trace_pairing_degree_*_square` proof bodies. The calculations above use classical local class field theory. Source reading and the finite arithmetic checks do not constitute Lean compilation or verification of the Poitou–Tate or RH dependency cones.
