### 18.3.14 Transporting residue fields, prime norms and local Brauer normalizations

**Motivation.** Section 18.3.13 showed how a local invariant family $J\in\mathrm{InvFam}(F,\Omega,\ell,r)$ is carried across $(e,E,\Phi)$ by precomposing with degree-two continuous pullback: $J'_{A'}=J_A\circ e_D^*$. That construction supplies two things to `ptConcl_of_iso_of` (`src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/PublishedTheorems/PoitouTateDuality/PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:29–31,90–101`): bijectivity of $J'_{A'}$ whenever $J_A$ is bijective, and the representative matching `hj` used by the three trace pairings of the nine-term sequence. Yet `ptConcl_of_iso_of` cannot invoke the target hypothesis `hPT : PTConclAt F' Ω'` at line 80 until it also proves `hnorm' : InvNormalised (E ζ) inv'` (`src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/TemperedRepresentationTheory/GelfandSpectralNormalisation/NormalisedInvariants/PTConclAt.lean:13`, `src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/TemperedRepresentationTheory/GelfandSpectralNormalisation/NormalisedInvariants/PoitouTateExactSecond.lean:15–18`).

Why is bijectivity not enough? For $\ell^r>2$, the group of units $(\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z)^\times$ is nontrivial: multiplying $J_A$ at one place by $u_1\in(\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z)^\times$ and at another place by $u_2\ne u_1$ preserves linearity and bijectivity at every place, while destroying the global reciprocity law $\sum_{v\in S}j_v(\operatorname{res}_v x)=0$ (`NineTermExact.sum_eq_zero`, `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/PublishedTheorems/GaloisCohomology₂/PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:203–220`) and the transgression kernels `tr0`, `tr1`, `tr2`. Even when $\ell^r=2$, `PTConclAt` does not assume abstractly that $H^2(D_A,R_A)$ has cardinality two; it pins down the linear functional $J_A$ by requiring `IsNormalised ζ A v (inv A)` (`PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:28–37`), namely $J_A([z])=1$ on every cocycle $z(g,h)=\kappa(g)\chi(h)$ built from a local uniformizer $\varpi\in v\setminus v^2$, an $\ell^r$-th root $y^{\ell^r}=\varpi$ read against $\zeta$, and an unramified additive character $\chi$ taking value $1$ on arithmetic Frobenius of exponent $q=N(v)=\lvert\mathcal O_F/v\rvert$.

Transporting this normalization from $(F,\Omega,\zeta)$ to $(F',\Omega',E\zeta)$ is the job of `GlobalTheory.T.ring_equiv_transports_residue` (`src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/RingEquivTransportsResidueStatement.lean:8–9`), whose production body is `GlobalTheory.CompanionTheory.PTIso.Norm.isNormalised_transfer` (`src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/RingEquivTransportsResidue.lean:10–54`). Reading `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean` as a whole reveals a sharp division of labor: three pieces of arithmetic data unpacked at lines 26–27 — preservation of ideal squares `hV2` ($x\in v^2\iff e_{\mathcal O}x\in e_V(v)^2$), preservation of prime norms `hN` ($\operatorname{normV}(e_V v)=\operatorname{normV}(v)$), and transport of arithmetic Frobenius `(hloc A A' hA).2.2` — have **no other consumer** anywhere in `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean`; all three exist solely to feed line 72. This subsection reads `isNormalised_transfer`, the consumer block `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:64–80`, and the Mathlib proof bodies behind integral-closure restriction, ideal-power transport, localizations, residue-field isomorphisms and primitive-root transport that §18.3.12 left at statement level.

**Setting.** Keep the number fields $F,F'$, algebraic closures $\Omega,\Omega'$, compatible ring isomorphisms $e\colon F\xrightarrow{\sim}F'$ and $E\colon\Omega\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega'$ satisfying $E(\operatorname{algebraMap}_F^\Omega x)=\operatorname{algebraMap}_{F'}^{\Omega'}(e(x))$ (`hE`), topological group isomorphism $\Phi\colon G_F\xrightarrow{\sim}G_{F'}$ with $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)=E(\sigma x)$ (`hΦ`), and local restriction $e_D\colon D_A\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{A'}$ from §§18.3.12–18.3.13. Let $\ell$ be a prime, $r\ge 1$, and $R=\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z$. Write $B=\overline{\mathbb Z}_\Omega$ and $B'=\overline{\mathbb Z}_{\Omega'}$ for the integral closures of $\mathbb Z$ in $\Omega$ and $\Omega'$.

Both worked examples below take $\ell=3$ and $r=1$, so $R=\mathbb F_3$ has two distinct nonzero scalars $1\ne 2$. Unlike the $\ell^r=2$ examples of §18.3.13, where $-1=+1$ in $\mathbb F_2$ hides sign and root-of-unity choices, working over $\mathbb F_3$ exposes every orientation in the theorem. Put $F=F'=\mathbb Q(\omega)$ where $\omega\in\Omega$ satisfies $\omega^2+\omega+1=0$, with ring of integers $\mathcal O_F=\mathbb Z[\omega]$ and field norm $N_{F/\mathbb Q}(a+b\omega)=a^2-ab+b^2$ [Paper] [Milne, *ANT*, Theorem 6.4(b), p. 99](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf). Let $e\colon F\xrightarrow{\sim}F'$ be complex conjugation $e(a+b\omega)=a+b\omega^2=(a-b)-b\omega$, extended to a ring automorphism $E\colon\Omega\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega'$. Take $\zeta=\omega$ as the chosen primitive cube root of unity on the source side. Then the target primitive root forced by `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:64–65` is
$$E(\zeta)=\omega^2=\zeta^2\ne\zeta .$$
Because $\omega$ and $\omega^2$ both lie in $F=F'$, every $\sigma\in G_F$ fixes $\omega$ and every $\tau\in G_{F'}$ fixes $\omega^2$ (and hence $\omega=(\omega^2)^2$), so $\mathrm{cycMod}_{F,3,1}\equiv 1$ and $\mathrm{cycMod}_{F',3,1}\equiv 1$ in $\mathbb F_3^\times$, and the local coefficient modules $R_A$ and $R'_{A'}$ carry the trivial actions of $D_A$ and $D_{A'}$. [Expository]

**Example 1 — a split prime above $7$ over $\mathbb F_3$.** [Expository] Over $\mathbb F_7$, the minimal polynomial factors as $X^2+X+1\equiv(X-2)(X-4)\pmod 7$. Put $\pi=3+\omega\in\mathcal O_F$ and $\pi'=e_{\mathcal O}(\pi)=3+\omega^2=2-\omega\in\mathcal O_{F'}$. Since $\pi\pi'=(3+\omega)(3+\omega^2)=9-3+1=7$ and $N_{F/\mathbb Q}(\pi)=N_{F/\mathbb Q}(\pi')=7$, we have $7\mathcal O_F=vv'$ with distinct maximal ideals $v=(\pi)$ and $v'=e_V(v)=(\pi')\ne v$.

*Localizations, uniformizers and residue fields.* Localizing at $v$ and $v'$ inverts $\mathcal O_F\setminus v$ and $\mathcal O_{F'}\setminus v'$, yielding discrete valuation rings $(\mathcal O_F)_v$ and $(\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'}$ with maximal ideals $\mathfrak m_v=\pi(\mathcal O_F)_v$ and $\mathfrak m_{v'}=\pi'(\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'}$, and $e_{\mathcal O,v}(\alpha/\beta)=e_{\mathcal O}(\alpha)/e_{\mathcal O}(\beta)$ maps $\mathfrak m_v^n$ onto $\mathfrak m_{v'}^n$ for every $n\ge 0$. Take the primed test uniformizer $\varpi'=\pi'=2-\omega\in v'$. Because $N_{F'/\mathbb Q}(\varpi')=7$ is not divisible by $7^2$, $\varpi'\notin (v')^2=(\pi'^2)=((3-5\omega))$. Its pullback along $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$ is $\varpi=e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')=3+\omega=\pi$, which satisfies $\varpi\in v\setminus v^2$ ($\operatorname{ord}_v(\varpi)=1$).

Modulo $v$, $\omega\equiv -3\equiv 4\pmod 7$, so $\psi_v(a+b\omega)=a+4b\bmod 7$ identifies $\kappa(v)=\mathcal O_F/v$ with $\mathbb F_7$. Modulo $v'$, $\omega\equiv 2\pmod 7$, so $\psi_{v'}(a+b\omega)=a+2b\bmod 7$ identifies $\kappa(v')=\mathcal O_{F'}/v'$ with $\mathbb F_7$. Under these identifications the induced residue isomorphism $\bar e_v\colon\kappa(v)\xrightarrow{\sim}\kappa(v')$, $x+v\mapsto e_{\mathcal O}(x)+v'$, sends $a+b\omega+v$ to
$$\psi_{v'}\bigl((a-b)-b\omega\bigr)\equiv (a-b)-2b=a-3b\equiv a+4b=\psi_v(a+b\omega)\pmod 7.$$
Thus $N(v')=\lvert\kappa(v')\rvert=7=\lvert\kappa(v)\rvert=N(v)$, and while $\psi_{v'}(\omega)=2\ne 4=\psi_v(\omega)$, the transported root $E(\zeta)=\omega^2$ has residue $\psi_{v'}(\omega^2)=4=\psi_v(\zeta)$!

*Completions, Kummer cocycle and finite-field Frobenius.* By Hensel's lemma, $X^2+X+1=0$ has two roots $\eta_1,\eta_2\in\mathbb Z_7$ with $\eta_1\equiv 4\pmod 7$ ($\eta_1\equiv 18\pmod{343}$, $\eta_1\equiv 82681\pmod{7^6}$) and $\eta_2=-1-\eta_1\equiv 2\pmod 7$ ($\eta_2\equiv 34967\pmod{7^6}$). Embedding $F\hookrightarrow\mathbb Q_7$ by $\varphi_v(\omega)=\eta_1$ gives $\varphi_v(\pi)=3+\eta_1\equiv 21\pmod{343}$, of $7$-adic valuation $1$, and the compatible target embedding $\varphi_{v'}=\varphi_v\circ e^{-1}$ sends $\omega\mapsto\eta_2$ and $\pi'=3+\omega^2\mapsto 3+\eta_1=\varphi_v(\pi)$. Choose $j\colon\Omega\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb Q}_7$ extending $\varphi_v$, put $A=\{x\in\Omega:\lvert j(x)\rvert_7\le 1\}$, and put $A'=E(A)=\{x'\in\Omega':\lvert j(E^{-1}x')\rvert_7\le 1\}$, which lies over $v'$.

Choose $y'\in\Omega'$ with $(y')^3=\varpi'=3+\omega^2$, and set $y=E^{-1}(y')\in\Omega$, so $y^3=e^{-1}(\varpi')=3+\omega=\varpi$. Over $F_v\cong\mathbb Q_7$, $F_v(y)$ is totally ramified of degree $3$ ($X^3-\varphi_v(\pi)$ is Eisenstein). Define $\kappa'\colon D_{A'}\to\mathbb F_3$ relative to $E(\zeta)=\omega^2$ by $g'(y')=(\omega^2)^{\kappa'(g')}y'$. For $g\in D_A$ and $g'=e_D(g)=E\circ g\circ E^{-1}$, applying $E^{-1}$ gives
$$g(y)=E^{-1}\bigl(g'(y')\bigr)=E^{-1}\bigl((\omega^2)^{\kappa'(e_Dg)}y'\bigr)=\omega^{\kappa'(e_Dg)}y=\zeta^{\kappa'(e_Dg)}y,$$
so the pulled-back Kummer character $\kappa=\kappa'\circ e_D$ is the exact Kummer character of $y$ relative to $\zeta=\omega$.

For the unramified factor, the cubes in $\mathbb F_7$ are $\{0,1,6\}$, so $X^3-2$ is irreducible over $\mathbb F_7$. Choose $\beta\in\Omega$ with $\beta^3=2$ and put $\beta'=E(\beta)\in\Omega'$, so $(\beta')^3=E(2)=2$. Then $F_v(\beta)/F_v$ is unramified of degree $3$ with residue field $\mathbb F_7(\bar\beta)\cong\mathbb F_{343}$. On $\mathbb F_{343}$, the $7$-th power arithmetic Frobenius acts on $\bar\beta$ by
$$\bar\beta^7=\bar\beta^6\bar\beta=(\bar\beta^3)^2\bar\beta=4\bar\beta .$$
At $A$ over $v$, $4=\psi_v(\omega)=\bar\zeta$ in $\kappa(v)\cong\mathbb F_7$, and reduction modulo $\mathfrak m_A$ is injective on cube roots of $2$ since $7\ne 3$, so every $\sigma\in D_A$ satisfying $\operatorname{IsFrob}_F(A,7,\sigma)$ sends $\beta\mapsto\omega\beta=\zeta^1\beta$. At $A'$ over $v'$, the same identity $(\bar\beta')^7=4\bar\beta'$ holds in $\mathbb F_7(\bar\beta')$, and now $4=\psi_{v'}(\omega^2)=\overline{E(\zeta)}$ in $\kappa(v')\cong\mathbb F_7$! Thus every $\sigma'\in D_{A'}$ satisfying $\operatorname{IsFrob}_{F'}(A',7,\sigma')$ sends $\beta'\mapsto\omega^2\beta'=(E\zeta)^1\beta'$. Defining $\chi'\colon D_{A'}\to\mathbb F_3$ by $g'(\beta')=(E\zeta)^{\chi'(g')}\beta'$, we see that $\chi'$ is an additive homomorphism, vanishes on $I_{A'}$, takes value $1$ on $\operatorname{IsFrob}_{F'}(A',7,\cdot)$, and pulls back to $\chi=\chi'\circ e_D$ satisfying $g(\beta)=\zeta^{\chi(g)}\beta$, $\chi|_{I_A}=0$, and $\chi(\sigma_{\mathrm{Frob}})=1$. Hence $z(g,h)=z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=\kappa(g)\chi(h)$ satisfies `IsNormalised ζ A v iA`, so $i'_{A'}([z'])=i_A([z])=1\in\mathbb F_3$.

More generally, every cube class in $F_v^\times/(F_v^\times)^3\cong\mathbb F_3^2$ is represented uniquely by $\pi^a 2^b$ ($a,b\in\{0,1,2\}$), and on $F_{v'}^\times/(F_{v'}^\times)^3$ by $(\pi')^a 2^b$. Writing the tame cubic symbol $\{x,y\}_v\equiv\bigl((-1)^{v(x)v(y)}x^{v(y)}/y^{v(x)}\bigr)^{(7-1)/3}\pmod{\mathfrak m_v}$ as a power of $\zeta$ at $v$ and of $E(\zeta)$ at $v'$ (noting $(-1)^2=1$), we obtain for $(\pi^{a_1}2^{b_1},\pi^{a_2}2^{b_2})$ the residue $2^{2(b_1a_2-b_2a_1)}\equiv 4^{-(b_1a_2-b_2a_1)}=\bar\zeta^{-(a_1b_2-b_1a_2)}$ modulo $v$, and $\overline{E(\zeta)}^{-(a_1b_2-b_1a_2)}$ modulo $v'$. Under the archive's sign convention (Remark 2 below), the normalized pairing on all $9\times 9=81$ pairs of cube classes is $(a_1,b_1),(a_2,b_2)\mapsto a_1b_2-b_1a_2\in\mathbb F_3$ on both sides, taking value $1\cdot 1-0\cdot 0=1$ on $(\varpi,2)$ and $(\varpi',2)$ [Paper] [Milne, *CFT*, III §3–§4, pp. 108–114](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf).

**Example 2 — an inert prime above $2$ with nontrivial residue automorphism and $N(v)=4\ne 2$.** [Expository] Keep $F=F'=\mathbb Q(\omega)$, $e(\omega)=\omega^2$, $\ell=3,r=1$, and $\zeta=\omega$. Over $\mathbb F_2$, $X^2+X+1$ has no root ($0^2+0+1=1$ and $1^2+1+1=1$), so $v=2\mathcal O_F$ is inert in $F$. Here $e_V(v)=e_{\mathcal O}(2\mathcal O_F)=2\mathcal O_{F'}=v$: the prime ideal itself does **not** move. Yet two features invisible at a degree-one split prime appear immediately:
1. The residue field $\kappa(v)=\mathbb Z[\omega]/(2)=\mathbb F_2(\bar\omega)\cong\mathbb F_4$ has cardinality
   $$q=N(v)=N(e_V(v))=4\ne 2,$$
   distinct from the residue characteristic $p=2$.
2. Although $e_V(v)=v$, the induced residue automorphism $\bar e_v\colon\kappa(v)\xrightarrow{\sim}\kappa(v)$ is **not** the identity: it sends $\bar\omega\mapsto\overline{\omega^2}=1+\bar\omega=\bar\omega^2$, which is the nontrivial Frobenius automorphism of $\mathbb F_4/\mathbb F_2$.

*A non-invariant uniformizer and the $\mathbb F_{64}/\mathbb F_4$ Frobenius.* In $(\mathcal O_F)_v=(\mathbb Z[\omega])_{(2)}$, the maximal ideal is $2(\mathcal O_F)_v$ and $v^2=(4)$. Choose the primed uniformizer $\varpi'=-2\omega\in v\setminus v^2$ (since $-\omega\in\mathbb Z[\omega]^\times$). Unlike $2$, $\varpi'$ is moved by $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$:
$$\varpi=e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')=-2\omega^2=2+2\omega\ne\varpi',$$
and $\varpi\in v\setminus v^2$ again has $v$-adic order $1$. Choose $(y')^3=\varpi'$ in $\Omega'$ and set $y=E^{-1}(y')\in\Omega$, so $y^3=\varpi$; exactly as in Example 1, $\kappa=\kappa'\circ e_D$ satisfies $g(y)=\zeta^{\kappa(g)}y$ when $\kappa'$ is read relative to $E(\zeta)=\omega^2$.

To build the unramified cubic character over $F_v=\mathbb Q_2(\omega)$, observe that in $\mathbb F_4^\times=\{1,\bar\omega,\bar\omega^2\}$ every element satisfies $\bar x^3=1$, so $1$ is the unique cube in $\mathbb F_4^\times$, and both $X^3-\bar\omega$ and $X^3-\bar\omega^2$ are irreducible over $\mathbb F_4$ (equivalently, $\Phi_9(Z)=Z^6+Z^3+1$ is irreducible over $\mathbb F_2$ because $2$ has multiplicative order $6$ modulo $9$). Choose $\gamma\in\Omega$ with $\gamma^3=\omega=\zeta$ (a primitive $9$-th root of unity) and put $\gamma'=E(\gamma)\in\Omega'$, so $(\gamma')^3=E(\omega)=\omega^2=E(\zeta)$. Because $2\nmid 9$, $F_v(\gamma)/F_v$ is unramified of degree $3$, with residue field $\mathbb F_4(\bar\gamma)\cong\mathbb F_{64}$ at a valuation ring $A$ over $v$ and $\mathbb F_4(\bar\gamma')\cong\mathbb F_{64}$ at $A'=E(A)$ over $e_V(v)=v$.

Now test the Frobenius exponent. An element of $G_F$ fixes $F=\mathbb Q(\omega)$, so on residues it must fix $\kappa(v)=\mathbb F_4$; the $2$-nd power map $z\mapsto z^2$ moves $\bar\omega$ to $\bar\omega^2$ and therefore lifts to **no** element of $G_F$. By contrast, the arithmetic Frobenius of exponent $q=N(v)=4$ fixes $\mathbb F_4$ pointwise and acts on $\bar\gamma$ and $\bar\gamma'$ by
$$\bar\gamma^4=\bar\gamma^3\bar\gamma=\bar\omega\bar\gamma=\bar\zeta\bar\gamma,\qquad (\bar\gamma')^4=(\bar\gamma')^3\bar\gamma'=\bar\omega^2\bar\gamma'=\overline{E(\zeta)}\,\bar\gamma'.$$
Since reduction modulo $\mathfrak m_A$ (respectively $\mathfrak m_{A'}$) is injective on $\mu_9$, every $\sigma\in D_A$ with $\operatorname{IsFrob}_F(A,4,\sigma)$ satisfies $\sigma(\gamma)=\zeta\gamma$, and every $\sigma'\in D_{A'}$ with $\operatorname{IsFrob}_{F'}(A',4,\sigma')$ satisfies $\sigma'(\gamma')=(E\zeta)\gamma'$. On $\mathbb F_{64}=\mathbb F_2(\bar\gamma)$, the residue map $\bar E_A$ sends $\bar\gamma\mapsto\bar\gamma'=\bar\gamma^{\pm 2}$ (specifically, if $E(\gamma)=\gamma^2$ or $\gamma^5$ or $\gamma^8$, $\bar E_A$ restricts on $\mathbb F_{64}$ to an odd power of the absolute $2$-Frobenius, commuting with $z\mapsto z^4$ on all $64$ elements of $\mathbb F_{64}$). Defining $\chi'(g')$ by $g'(\gamma')=(E\zeta)^{\chi'(g')}\gamma'$ gives $\chi'|_{I_{A'}}=0$, $\chi'(\sigma'_{\mathrm{Frob}})=1$, and pullback $\chi=\chi'\circ e_D$ satisfying $g(\gamma)=\zeta^{\chi(g)}\gamma$ and $\chi(\sigma_{\mathrm{Frob}})=1$. At $q=4$, the tame exponent is $(q-1)/3=1$ (and $(-1)^1=(-1)^3$ is a cube in $F_v^\times$), so on the $9$ tame cube classes $\varpi^a\omega^b$ and $(\varpi')^a(\omega^2)^b$ both normalized pairings again evaluate to $a_1b_2-b_1a_2\in\mathbb F_3$, giving $1\in\mathbb F_3$ on $(\varpi,\omega)$ and $(\varpi',\omega^2)$.

**Definitions (residue maps, localizations and normalization predicates).** [Lean, read]
1. (*Integral-closure isomorphism and place bijection.*) For a ring isomorphism $e\colon F\simeq+* F'$ of number fields, `eO := NumberField.RingOfIntegers.mapRingEquiv e` (`Mathlib/NumberTheory/NumberField/Basic.lean:194–206`) is the restricted ring isomorphism $\mathcal O_F\simeq+* \mathcal O_{F'}$, and `eV := placeEquiv eO` (`src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean:21–47`) is the bijection $\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_F)\simeq\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_{F'})$ sending $v$ to $\operatorname{Ideal.map} e_{\mathcal O}(v)$, where `HeightOneSpectrum R` packages `(asIdeal, isPrime, ne_bot)` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/DedekindDomain/Ideal/Lemmas.lean:493–496`). The prime norm is $\operatorname{normV}(v)=\operatorname{Nat.card}(\mathcal O_F/v)$ (`src/Formalism/ConjugateSelfDual/Statement.lean:23`).
2. (*Quotient and localization residue maps.*) For an ideal $I\subset R$ and $J=\operatorname{Ideal.map} f(I)\subset S$ under $f\colon R\simeq+* S$, `Ideal.quotientEquiv I J f rfl` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Quotient/Operations.lean:632–652`) is the ring isomorphism $R/I\simeq+* S/J$ sending $x+I\mapsto f(x)+J$. For prime ideals $I=f^{-1}(J)$, `Localization.localRingEquiv I J f hIJ` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/Localization/AtPrime/Basic.lean:292–298`) is the local ring isomorphism $R_I\simeq+* S_J$, `IsLocalization.AtPrime.equivQuotMaximalIdeal` (`:560–587`) is the residue-field isomorphism $R/I\simeq+* R_I/\mathfrak m_{R_I}$ when $I$ is maximal, and for any local ring isomorphism $g\colon R_1\simeq+* R_2$, `IsLocalRing.ResidueField.mapEquiv g` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/LocalRing/ResidueField/Basic.lean:135–146`) is the induced isomorphism $(R_1/\mathfrak m_{R_1})\simeq+* (R_2/\mathfrak m_{R_2})$.
3. (*Local and global normalization predicates.*) Fix $\zeta\in\Omega$, $A\in\operatorname{ValuationSubring}\Omega$, $v\in\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_F)$, and a linear map $i_A\colon H^2(D_A,R_A)\to R$ where $R_A=\mathrm{muLoc}(F,\ell,r,A)$. Then `IsNormalised ζ A v iA` (`PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:28–37`) is the proposition that for **every** $\varpi\in\mathcal O_F$, $y\in\Omega$, $\kappa,\chi\colon D_A\to R$, and $z\in Z^2(D_A,R_A)$ satisfying
   (i) $\varpi\in v$, (ii) $\varpi\notin v^2$, (iii) $y^{\ell^r}=\operatorname{algebraMap}_F^\Omega(\varpi)$, (iv) $g(y)=\zeta^{\kappa(g)}y$ for all $g\in D_A$ (with exponent lifted to $\{0,\dots,\ell^r-1\}$ via `.val`), (v) $\chi(gh)=\chi(g)+\chi(h)$ for all $g,h\in D_A$, (vi) $\chi(g)=0$ whenever $g\in I_A$, (vii) $\chi(g)=1$ whenever $\operatorname{IsFrob}_F(A,\operatorname{normV}(v),g)$, and (viii) $z(g,h)=\kappa(g)\chi(h)$ for all $g,h\in D_A$,
   one has $i_A([z])=1$. For a full family $\mathrm{inv}\in\mathrm{InvFam}(F,\Omega,\ell,r)$, `InvNormalised ζ inv` (`PoitouTateExactSecond.lean:15–18`) asserts that for every $A$ and every $v$ with `IsPlaceOver A v`, $\mathrm{inv}(A)$ is bijective and satisfies `IsNormalised ζ A v (inv A)`.

**Lemma S (integral closures, prime powers, localizations and norm preservation).** [Lean, read] Let $e\colon F\simeq+* F'$ be a ring isomorphism of number fields.
1. $e$ restricts to a canonical ring isomorphism $e_{\mathcal O}\colon\mathcal O_F\simeq+* \mathcal O_{F'}$ with $(e_{\mathcal O}(x):F')=e(x:F)$ for all $x\in\mathcal O_F$ and inverse $(e^{-1})_{\mathcal O}$ (`RingOfIntegers.mapRingEquiv`, `heO`).
2. Pushforward of ideals along $e_{\mathcal O}$ defines a bijection $e_V\colon\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_F)\simeq\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_{F'})$ with inverse induced by $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$, such that for every $v$, every $x\in\mathcal O_F$, and every $n\ge 1$ (in particular $n=1,2$),
   $$x\in v^n\iff e_{\mathcal O}(x)\in e_V(v)^n .$$
3. For each $v$ and $v'=e_V(v)$, $e_{\mathcal O}$ induces both a local DVR isomorphism $e_{\mathcal O,v}\colon(\mathcal O_F)_v\simeq+* (\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'}$ carrying $\mathfrak m_v^n$ onto $\mathfrak m_{v'}^n$ and a constructive ring isomorphism of finite residue fields $\bar e_v\colon\mathcal O_F/v\simeq+* \mathcal O_{F'}/v'$ commuting with the canonical residue identifications $\mathcal O_F/v\simeq(\mathcal O_F)_v/\mathfrak m_v$ and $\mathcal O_{F'}/v'\simeq(\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'}/\mathfrak m_{v'}$. In particular:
   (a) an element $\varpi\in\mathcal O_F$ is a uniformizer of the DVR $(\mathcal O_F)_v$ ($\varpi\in\mathfrak m_v\setminus\mathfrak m_v^2$) if and only if $\varpi\in v\setminus v^2$, and this property holds for $\varpi$ at $v$ if and only if it holds for $e_{\mathcal O}(\varpi)$ at $v'$;
   (b) $\operatorname{normV}(e_V(v))=\operatorname{normV}(v)$ (`hN`).

**Proof.** (1) By definition $\mathcal O_F=\operatorname{integralClosure}\mathbb Z F$ (`Mathlib/NumberTheory/NumberField/Basic.lean:103–105`). Any ring homomorphism $f\colon F\to+* F'$ is uniquely a $\mathbb Z$-algebra homomorphism (`f.toIntAlgHom`), so if $P\in\mathbb Z[X]$ is monic with $P(x)=0$, then $P(f(x))=f(P(x))=0$ (`map_isIntegral_int`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/IntegralClosure/IsIntegral/Basic.lean:185–188`). Thus `RingOfIntegers.mapRingHom f` (`Basic.lean:181–190`) sends $\langle x,h_x\rangle$ to $\langle f(x),\operatorname{map\_isIntegral\_int} f h_x\rangle$, preserving $0,1,+,\cdot$ by subtype extensionality, with underlying value $f(x)$ by `rfl`. Applying this to $e$ and $e^{-1}$ yields mutually inverse ring homomorphisms by `e.right_inv` and `e.left_inv` (`mapRingEquiv`, `:194–206`).

(2) In `RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean:11–60`, for any ring isomorphism $f\colon R\simeq+* S$ and ideal $I\subset R$, `Ideal.map_comap_of_equiv` (`Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Maps.lean:481–483`) identifies $\operatorname{map} f(I)=\operatorname{comap} f^{-1}(I)$. Hence $y\in\operatorname{map} f(I)\iff f^{-1}(y)\in I\iff\exists x\in I,\,f(x)=y$ (`mem_map_of_equiv`, `:505–513`), and injectivity of $f$ gives $x\in I\iff f(x)\in\operatorname{map} f(I)$ (`mem_iff_apply_mem_map`, `RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean:11–18`). When $v$ is a nonzero prime ideal of the Dedekind domain $\mathcal O_F$, $\operatorname{map} e_{\mathcal O}(v)=\operatorname{comap} e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(v)$ is prime because preimages of primes are prime (`Ideal.map_isPrime_of_equiv`, `Maps.lean:1055–1059`), and nonzero because $e_{\mathcal O}$ is injective (`Ideal.map_eq_bot_iff_of_injective`, `:1061–1063`). Symmetry `Ideal.map_symm` (`:492–493`) gives $\operatorname{map} e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\operatorname{map} e_{\mathcal O}(v))=v$, so `placeEquiv eO` is a bijection (`RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean:34–47`). Moreover, pushforward preserves ideal products (`Ideal.map_mul`, `Maps.lean:648–658`), making `Ideal.mapHom eO` a semiring homomorphism on ideals (`:662–667`), so $\operatorname{map} e_{\mathcal O}(v^n)=(\operatorname{map} e_{\mathcal O}(v))^n=e_V(v)^n$ (`Ideal.map_pow`, `:669–670`). Applying `mem_iff_apply_mem_map` to $v$ and $v^2$ yields `hV` and `hV2` (`RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean:53–58`), and the same proof works for every $n\ge 1$.

(3) Since $x\in v\iff e_{\mathcal O}(x)\in v'$, we have $v=\operatorname{comap} e_{\mathcal O}(v')$, so $e_{\mathcal O}(\mathcal O_F\setminus v)\subseteq\mathcal O_{F'}\setminus v'$ (`Localization.le_comap_primeCompl_iff`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/Localization/AtPrime/Basic.lean:226–231`). Universal localization mapping produces `Localization.localRingHom v v' eO` and its inverse from $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$, forming the local ring isomorphism $e_{\mathcal O,v}\colon(\mathcal O_F)_v\simeq+* (\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'}$ (`Localization.localRingEquiv`, `:241–298`). Because $\mathcal O_F$ is a Dedekind domain and $v\ne 0$ is prime (hence maximal, `HeightOneSpectrum.isMaximal`, `Lemmas.lean:504`), $(\mathcal O_F)_v$ is a discrete valuation ring (`IsLocalization.AtPrime.isDiscreteValuationRing_of_dedekind_domain`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/DedekindDomain/Dvr.lean:128–138` [Lean]) with maximal ideal $\mathfrak m_v=v(\mathcal O_F)_v$ (`map_eq_maximalIdeal`, `AtPrime/Basic.lean:524–526`), and $(\mathfrak m_v^n)\cap\mathcal O_F=v^n$ for all $n\ge 0$ (`under_maximalIdeal_pow`, `:538–544`, proved from prime-complement cancellation in $\mathcal O_F/v^n$). For $n=1,2$ this gives $\varpi\in\mathfrak m_v\setminus\mathfrak m_v^2\iff\varpi\in v\setminus v^2$, and (2) transports both memberships across $e_{\mathcal O}$, proving (3a).

For the residue fields, `Ideal.quotientMap v' eO` lifts $(x\mapsto e_{\mathcal O}(x)+v')$ through $\mathcal O_F/v$ because $v\le\operatorname{comap} e_{\mathcal O}(v')$, and pairing it with `quotientMap v eO.symm` gives the constructive ring isomorphism $\bar e_v=\operatorname{Ideal.quotientEquiv}(v,v',e_{\mathcal O},\mathrm{rfl})\colon\mathcal O_F/v\simeq+* \mathcal O_{F'}/v'$ (`Quotient/Operations.lean:596–652`), whose action on cosets is `rfl`. Composing with `equivQuotMaximalIdeal` (`AtPrime/Basic.lean:560–587`, which sends $x+v\mapsto\operatorname{algebraMap}(x)+\mathfrak m_v$ by `rfl`) commutes on every coset representative $x\in\mathcal O_F$: both paths send $x+v$ to $\operatorname{algebraMap}(e_{\mathcal O}x)+\mathfrak m_{v'}$, which equals `IsLocalRing.ResidueField.mapEquiv` (`ResidueField/Basic.lean:135–143`) applied to $e_{\mathcal O,v}$. Finally, `RingIsomorphismTransportsPlaces.lean:59–60` applies `Nat.card_congr` (`Mathlib/SetTheory/Cardinal/Finite.lean:91–92`) to $\bar e_v.\mathrm{toEquiv}$ and takes `.symm`, giving $\operatorname{normV}(e_V(v))=\operatorname{normV}(v)$. $\square$

**Lemma T (equivariant residue towers, finite-field Frobenius and primitive roots).** [Lean, read] Assume $e,E,\Phi$ satisfy `hE` and `hΦ`, and let $A\subset\Omega$ and $A'\subset\Omega'$ be valuation subrings satisfying $E(x)\in A'\iff x\in A$ (`hA`, equivalently $A=A'.\mathrm{comap}(E.\mathrm{toRingHom})$, `Mathlib/RingTheory/Valuation/ValuationSubring.lean:858–866`).
1. For every $v\in\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_F)$, $\operatorname{IsPlaceOver}(A,v)\iff\operatorname{IsPlaceOver}(A',e_V(v))$ (`isPlaceOver_transfer_fieldIso`, `src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/RingEquivTransportsDecomposition.lean:18–35`). When this holds, $E$ restricts to a local ring isomorphism $E|_A\colon A\simeq+* A'$ sending $\mathfrak m_A$ onto $\mathfrak m_{A'}$ and $B\cap\mathfrak m_A$ onto $B'\cap\mathfrak m_{A'}$, and the induced residue-field isomorphism $\bar E_A\colon k_A\simeq+* k_{A'}$ (`ResidueField.mapEquiv`) forms a commuting square with $\bar e_v\colon\kappa(v)\simeq+* \kappa(e_V(v))$ and the canonical injections $\iota_{A,v}\colon\kappa(v)\hookrightarrow k_A$, $\iota_{A',v'}\colon\kappa(v')\hookrightarrow k_{A'}$:
   $$\bar E_A\circ\iota_{A,v}=\iota_{A',v'}\circ\bar e_v .$$
2. Put $q=\operatorname{normV}(v)$ and $q'=\operatorname{normV}(e_V(v))$. Then $q'=q$, $\bar E_A(z^q)=\bar E_A(z)^{q'}$ for all $z\in k_A$ (and on every finite Galois residue layer $\kappa_L/\kappa(v)$ inside $k_A$), $\bar E_A(\overline{\sigma x})=\overline{\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)}$ for all $\sigma\in D_A$ and $x\in A$, and for every $\sigma\in G_F$:
   $$\operatorname{IsFrob}_F(A,\operatorname{normV}(v),\sigma)\iff\operatorname{IsFrob}_{F'}\bigl(A',\operatorname{normV}(e_V(v)),\Phi(\sigma)\bigr).$$
3. For every $k\ge 0$ and $\zeta\in\Omega$, $\operatorname{IsPrimitiveRoot}(\zeta,k)\iff\operatorname{IsPrimitiveRoot}(E(\zeta),k)$ (`IsPrimitiveRoot.map_of_injective` and `map_iff_of_injective`, `Mathlib/RingTheory/RootsOfUnity/PrimitiveRoots.lean:296–318`).

**Proof.** (1) Part `isPlaceOver_transfer_fieldIso` was read in §18.3.12 (`RingEquivTransportsDecomposition.lean:9–35`): nonzeros satisfy $v_A(y)<1\iff y^{-1}\notin A$, which `hA` and `map_inv₀` carry across $E$, and `hE`, `heO`, `hV` identify the restriction to $\mathcal O_F$ with that to $\mathcal O_{F'}$. By `ValuationSubring.valuation_le_one_iff` and `valuation_lt_one_iff` (`ValuationSubring.lean:183–185,216–220`), $x\in A\iff v_A(x)\le 1$ and $a\in\mathfrak m_A\iff v_A(a)<1$; hence $E$ bijects $A$ onto $A'$ and $\mathfrak m_A$ onto $\mathfrak m_{A'}$ (`valuation_le_one_transport`, `valuation_lt_one_transport`, `src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/PublishedTheorems/PoitouTateDuality/MemDecompositionSubgroupIff.lean:24–35`), and also $B$ onto $B'$ by `map_isIntegral_int`, sending $\mathfrak p_A=\mathfrak m_A\cap B$ onto $\mathfrak p_{A'}=\mathfrak m_{A'}\cap B'$. Applying `IsLocalRing.ResidueField.mapEquiv` (`ResidueField/Basic.lean:135–143`) to $E|_A$ gives $\bar E_A(\bar x)=\overline{E(x)}$ for $x\in A$. When $\operatorname{IsPlaceOver}(A,v)$ holds, $\mathfrak m_A\cap\mathcal O_F=v$, so $\iota_{A,v}\colon\mathcal O_F/v\to A/\mathfrak m_A$ is injective (`Ideal.algebraMap_quotient_injective`, `Quotient/Operations.lean:756–761`). For $x\in\mathcal O_F$, `hE` and `heO` give $E(\operatorname{algebraMap}_F^\Omega x)=\operatorname{algebraMap}_{F'}^{\Omega'}(e_{\mathcal O}x)$, and reducing modulo $\mathfrak m_{A'}$ yields $\bar E_A(\iota_{A,v}(x+v))=\iota_{A',v'}(\bar e_v(x+v))$.

(2) Lemma S(3b) gives $q'=q$, and since $\bar E_A$ is a ring homomorphism, $\bar E_A(z^q)=\bar E_A(z)^q=\bar E_A(z)^{q'}$. For $\sigma\in D_A$ and $x\in A$, $\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)=E(\sigma x)$ (`hΦ`) reduces modulo $\mathfrak m_{A'}$ to $\overline{\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)}=\bar E_A(\overline{\sigma x})$. In `MemDecompositionSubgroupIff.lean:56–62`, the third conjunct of `locHyp` proves $\operatorname{IsFrob}_F(A,q,\sigma)\iff\operatorname{IsFrob}_{F'}(A',q,\Phi(\sigma))$ for every natural number $q$: decomposition membership is `hdec` (`:38–48`), and for $y=E(x)\in A'$ ($v_A(x)\le 1$ by `valuation_le_one_transport`),
$$\Phi(\sigma)(Ex)-(Ex)^q=E(\sigma x-x^q)$$
by `hΦ`, `map_pow`, and `map_sub`, whose $A'$-valuation is $<1$ if and only if $v_A(\sigma x-x^q)<1$ by `valuation_lt_one_transport` (`:58–62`). Rewriting $q=\operatorname{normV}(v)$ to $q'=\operatorname{normV}(e_V(v))$ by Lemma S(3b) gives the stated equivalence.

(3) In `PrimitiveRoots.lean:296–304`, if $\zeta^k=1$ and $\operatorname{orderOf}(\zeta)=k$, then $(E\zeta)^k=E(\zeta^k)=E(1)=1$, and whenever $(E\zeta)^m=1=E(1)$, we have $E(\zeta^m)=E(1)$, so injectivity of $E$ gives $\zeta^m=1$, hence $k\mid m$. Applying the same lemma to $E^{-1}$ gives the converse (`:305–318`). $\square$

The equivariant residue and localization cube proved in Lemmas S and T is summarized by two commuting squares of rings and fields, where the right-hand column carries the intertwined actions of $D_A\xrightarrow{e_D}D_{A'}$ and the common Frobenius exponent $q=N(v)=N(v')$:
$$\begin{array}{ccccccc}
\mathcal O_F & \hookrightarrow & (\mathcal O_F)_v & \twoheadrightarrow & \kappa(v) & \xhookrightarrow{\iota_{A,v}} & k_A = A/\mathfrak m_A \\
e_{\mathcal O}\downarrow\wr & & e_{\mathcal O,v}\downarrow\wr & & \bar e_v\downarrow\wr & & \wr\downarrow\bar E_A \\
\mathcal O_{F'} & \hookrightarrow & (\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'} & \twoheadrightarrow & \kappa(v') & \xhookrightarrow{\iota_{A',v'}} & k_{A'} = A'/\mathfrak m_{A'}
\end{array}$$

**Lemma U (pullback of normalization test tuples).** [Lean, read] Assume the setup of Lemma T, let $e_{\mathcal O},e_V$ satisfy `heO`, `hI` ($n=1$), `hI2` ($n=2$), and `hN`, assume $\mathrm{cycMod}_{F',\ell,r}(\Phi\sigma)=\mathrm{cycMod}_{F,\ell,r}(\sigma)$ for all $\sigma\in G_F$ (`hc`), and assume the local equivalences `hD` of `LocHyp` at $(A,A')$. Put $e_D=\operatorname{subgroupIso}(\Phi,D_A,D_{A'})$. Then for every primed tuple $(\varpi',y',\kappa',\chi',z')$ satisfying clauses (i)–(viii) of `IsNormalised (E ζ) A' (eV v)`, the pulled-back unprimed tuple
$$\varpi:=e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi'),\quad y:=E^{-1}(y'),\quad \kappa:=\kappa'\circ e_D,\quad \chi:=\chi'\circ e_D,\quad z:=e_D^*z'\in Z^2(D_A,R_A)$$
satisfies all eight clauses (i)–(viii) of `IsNormalised ζ A v`.

**Proof.** This is lines 13–46 of `isNormalised_transfer` (`RingEquivTransportsResidue.lean`). First, lines 13–22 define `eD` by `subgroupIso Φ` using `(hD a).1`, note `((eD g : Dec A') : G F' Ω') = Φ g` by `rfl` (`hcoe`), and define `zz` ($=z$) by `compatiblePairCocycles2` along `eD` and `LinearMap.id`, discharging action compatibility $\mathrm{muLoc}'(e_Dg)(m)=\mathrm{muLoc}(g)(m)$ by rewriting with `hc` (Lemma R of §18.3.13). Put `hEy : E (E.symm y') = y'` (`E.apply_symm_apply y'`, `:23`) and `hϖ : eO (eO.symm ϖ') = ϖ'` (`eO.apply_symm_apply ϖ'`, `:24`). Now check (i)–(viii) in the exact order of lines 28–46:
- *(i) $\varpi\in v$ (`:28`):* By `hI` at $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')$ and `hϖ`, $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')\in v\iff \varpi'\in e_V(v)$, which is `h1`.
- *(ii) $\varpi\notin v^2$ (`:29`):* By `hI2` at $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')$ and `hϖ`, $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')\in v^2\iff \varpi'\in e_V(v)^2$, so `h2` gives $\varpi\notin v^2$.
- *(iii) $y^{\ell^r}=\operatorname{algebraMap}_F^\Omega(\varpi)$ (`:30–31`):* Since $E$ is injective (`E.injective`), it suffices to apply $E$ to both sides:
  $$E\bigl((E^{-1}y')^{\ell^r}\bigr)=(E(E^{-1}y'))^{\ell^r}=(y')^{\ell^r}\stackrel{h_3}{=}\operatorname{algebraMap}_{F'}^{\Omega'}(\varpi')\stackrel{h_\varpi,\mathrm{heO}}{=}\operatorname{algebraMap}_{F'}^{\Omega'}\bigl(e(e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}\varpi')\bigr)\stackrel{h_E}{=}E\bigl(\operatorname{algebraMap}_F^\Omega(e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}\varpi')\bigr).$$
- *(iv) $g(y)=\zeta^{\kappa(g)}y$ for $g\in D_A$ (`:32–35`):* Again apply `E.injective`. Using `← hΦ`, `hEy`, `← hcoe`, and `h4 (eD g)`:
  $$E\bigl(g(E^{-1}y')\bigr)=\Phi(g)\bigl(E(E^{-1}y')\bigr)=(e_Dg)(y')=(E\zeta)^{\kappa'(e_Dg)}y'=E\bigl(\zeta^{\kappa'(e_Dg)}E^{-1}y'\bigr),$$
  by `map_mul`, `map_pow`, and `hEy`.
- *(v) $\chi(gh)=\chi(g)+\chi(h)$ (`:36–38`):* Since $e_D$ is a group homomorphism (`map_mul`), $\chi'(e_D(gh))=\chi'(e_Dg\cdot e_Dh)=\chi'(e_Dg)+\chi'(e_Dh)$ by `h5`.
- *(vi) $\chi|_{I_A}=0$ (`:39–40`):* If $g\in I_A$ (whose carrier is definitionally `Inertia A`), forward inertia transport `(hD g).2.1.1` places $\Phi(g)=(e_Dg:G_{F'})$ in $I_{A'}$, and `h6 (eD g)` gives $\chi'(e_Dg)=0$.
- *(vii) $\chi(g)=1$ on $\operatorname{IsFrob}_F(A,\operatorname{normV}(v),g)$ (`:41–44`):* Given `hg : IsFrob A (normV v) g`, forward Frobenius transport `((hD g).2.2 (normV v)).1 hg` gives `IsFrob A' (normV v) (Φ g)`. Rewriting `normV (eV v)` to `normV v` by `rw [hN]` turns this into `IsFrob A' (normV (eV v)) (eD g)`, and `h7 (eD g)` evaluates $\chi'(e_Dg)=1$.
- *(viii) $z(g,h)=\kappa(g)\chi(h)$ (`:45–46`):* By definition of `compatiblePairCocycles2` with `LinearMap.id`, $z(g,h)=z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=\kappa'(e_Dg)\chi'(e_Dh)$ by `h8 (eD g) (eD h)`. $\square$

**Theorem 18.3.14 (`ring_equiv_transports_residue` / `isNormalised_transfer`).** [Lean, read] Under the hypotheses of Lemma U, let $\zeta\in\Omega$, $v\in\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_F)$, $i_A\colon H^2(D_A,R_A)\to R$, and $i'_{A'}\colon H^2(D_{A'},R'_{A'})\to R$ be linear maps satisfying the representative pullback relation (`hrel`): whenever $z\in Z^2(D_A,R_A)$ and $z'\in Z^2(D_{A'},R'_{A'})$ satisfy $z'(g',h')=z(g,h)$ for all $g,h\in D_A$ and $g',h'\in D_{A'}$ with $(g':G_{F'})=\Phi(g)$ and $(h':G_{F'})=\Phi(h)$, one has $i'_{A'}([z'])=i_A([z])$. Then
$$\operatorname{IsNormalised}(\zeta,A,v,i_A)\implies\operatorname{IsNormalised}\bigl(E(\zeta),A',e_V(v),i'_{A'}\bigr).$$

**Proof.** `RingEquivTransportsResidueStatement.lean:8–9` forwards directly to `GlobalTheory.CompanionTheory.PTIso.Norm.isNormalised_transfer` (`RingEquivTransportsResidue.lean:10–54`). Let $(\varpi',y',\kappa',\chi',z')$ be any tuple at $(E(\zeta),A',e_V(v))$ satisfying (i)–(viii), and form $(\varpi,y,\kappa,\chi,z)$ as in Lemma U (`z` is `zz` in Lean). Applying `hn : IsNormalised ζ A v iA` to this unprimed tuple yields `key : iA [zz] = 1` (`:25–46`). To deduce `iA' [z'] = 1`, lines 47–53 rewrite `iA' [z']` to `iA [zz]` via `hrel zz z'`: given $g,h\in D_A$ and $g',h'\in D_{A'}$ with underlying automorphisms $(g':G_{F'})=\Phi(g)$ and $(h':G_{F'})=\Phi(h)$, subtype extensionality (`Subtype.ext`) forces $g'=e_Dg$ and $h'=e_Dh$ (`:50–51`); substituting `e1` and `e2` reduces $z'(g',h')=z(g,h)$ to $z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)=z'(e_Dg,e_Dh)$, which holds by `rfl` (`:52–53`). Hence $i'_{A'}([z'])=i_A([z])=1$. $\square$

**Corollary 18.3.14.1 (filling `hζ'`, `hnorm'` and `hS'` in `ptConcl_of_iso_of`).** [Lean, read] In `PositivityTheory.PTN.ptConcl_of_iso_of` (`PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:22–104`), suppose `hinv : InvNormalised ζ inv` and `hS : ContainsAbove S l` hold on $(F,\Omega)$, and let `inv'` and `hinv'` be the output of `ring_equiv_transports_inertia` (§18.3.13). Then:
1. `E ζ` is a primitive $\ell^r$-th root of unity in $\Omega'$ (`hζ'`, `:64`).
2. `inv'` satisfies `InvNormalised (E ζ) inv'` across **all** valuation subrings $A'$ of $\Omega'$ and **all** height-one primes $v'$ of $\mathcal O_{F'}$ (`hnorm'`, `:65–73`).
3. $S'=S.\mathrm{map}(e_V.\mathrm{toEmbedding})$ satisfies `ContainsAbove S' l` (`hS'`, `:74–79`), completing the argument list of `hPT` at line 80.

**Proof.** Line 64 proves `hζ'` as `hζ.map_of_injective E.injective` (Lemma T(3)). At lines 65–73, fix any $A'\in\operatorname{ValuationSubring}\Omega'$ and $v'\in\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_{F'})$ with `hP : IsPlaceOver A' v'`. Surjectivity of $e_V$ (`eV.surjective v'`, `:67`) writes $v'=e_V(v)$ for a unique $v$. Setting `A := A'.comap E.toRingHom` (`:68`) gives `hA : ∀ x, E x ∈ A' ↔ x ∈ A` by `ValuationSubring.mem_comap` (`:69`). Applying the reverse direction `.2` of `hpl` (`ring_equiv_transports_decomposition`) to `hP` produces `hP0 : IsPlaceOver A v` (`:70`), so `hinv A v hP0` yields both `Bijective (inv A)` and `IsNormalised ζ A v (inv A)`. Bijectivity of `inv' A'` is `(hinv' A A' hA).1 (hinv A v hP0).1` (`:71`), and `IsNormalised (E ζ) A' (eV v) (inv' A')` is Theorem 18.3.14 applied to `(hinv' A A' hA).2` and `(hinv A v hP0).2` (`:72–73`). Finally, at lines 74–79, if $v'\in\operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum}(\mathcal O_{F'})$ contains $(\ell:\mathcal O_{F'})$, write $v'=e_V(v)$; since $e_{\mathcal O}(\ell)=\ell$ (`map_natCast`), `hV` pulls membership back to $(\ell:\mathcal O_F)\in v$, so $v\in S$ by `hS`, hence $e_V(v)\in S'$ by `Finset.mem_map_of_mem`.

With §§18.3.12–18.3.14 in place, every hypothesis unpacked in `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean` has a verified destination:

The production bodies of the two character and real-place calls are read in [§18.3.15](#18315-cyclotomic-characters-and-total-complexity-under-field-isomorphisms). $\square$

**Remark 1 (choices versus canonicity across the four levels).** [Expository] The transport chain mixes canonical functors with genuine choices, and conflating them produces immediate errors:
1. *Canonical from $e\colon F\simeq+* F'$ alone (no extension to $\Omega$ needed).* The integral-closure isomorphism $e_{\mathcal O}$, the place bijection $e_V$, the ideal-power equivalences $x\in v^n\iff e_{\mathcal O}x\in e_V(v)^n$, the DVR localization isomorphisms $e_{\mathcal O,v}\colon(\mathcal O_F)_v\simeq+* (\mathcal O_{F'})_{v'}$, the finite residue-field isomorphisms $\bar e_v\colon\kappa(v)\simeq+* \kappa(v')$, the norm equality $N(e_V(v))=N(v)$, and the finite-field arithmetic Frobenius $z\mapsto z^{N(v)}$ on any algebraic extension of $\kappa(v)$ depend **only** on $e$. Even at the Lean implementation level, `Ideal.quotientEquiv` (`Quotient/Operations.lean:632–652`) is constructive (`rfl` on cosets, invoking no choice axiom); `placeMap` is marked `noncomputable` only because `Ideal.map` passes through `Submodule.span`.
2. *Non-canonical choice of closure isomorphism $E\colon\Omega\simeq+* \Omega'$, canonical consequences once $E$ is fixed.* Extending $e$ to $E$ (`exists_subfield_model` via `IsAlgClosure.equivOfEquiv`, §18.3.12) is **non-canonical**: any other extension compatible with $e$ has the form $E\circ\gamma$ for $\gamma\in G_F$. Once a single $E$ is fixed, however, the valuation-ring pullback $A=E^{-1}(A')$ (`A'.comap E.toRingHom`), the local residue isomorphism $\bar E_A\colon k_A\simeq+* k_{A'}$, the group transport $\Phi(\sigma)=E\circ\sigma\circ E^{-1}$, its local restriction $e_D\colon D_A\simeq_{\mathrm{top}}D_{A'}$, the target root $E(\zeta)$, and the pulled-back family $J'_{A'}=J_A\circ e_D^*$ are **uniquely determined** by $(e,E)$. In particular, while `Av : S → ValuationSubring Ω` is an arbitrary choice of one valuation ring over each $v\in S$, lines 66–70 of `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean` make **no** choice of a valuation ring over $v$: `InvNormalised` quantifies universally over every $(A',v')$ with `IsPlaceOver A' v'`, and $A=E^{-1}(A')$ is canonical from $A'$ and $E$.
3. *Genuine dependence on the primitive root $\zeta\in\Omega$.* In classical local class field theory, $\operatorname{inv}_K\colon H^2(K,\mu_{\ell^r})\xrightarrow{\sim}\frac{1}{\ell^r}\mathbb Z/\mathbb Z$ is canonical without choosing roots of unity, whereas identifying $\mu_{\ell^r}$ with $R_A=\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z(\mathrm{cycMod})$ via $a\mapsto\zeta^a$ **depends on $\zeta$** [Paper] [Milne, *CFT*, III §4, Step 4, p. 113](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf). Explicitly, `muLoc` itself is independent of $\zeta$ (for any $c\in(\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z)^\times$ and $\widetilde\zeta=\zeta^c$, $g(\widetilde\zeta)=(g\zeta)^c=\widetilde\zeta^{\mathrm{cycMod}(g)}$), but in `IsNormalised` replacing $\zeta$ by $\widetilde\zeta=\zeta^c$ keeping $(\varpi,y,\chi)$ fixed changes $g(y)=\zeta^{\kappa(g)}y=\widetilde\zeta^{\widetilde\kappa(g)}y$ by $\widetilde\kappa=c^{-1}\kappa$, hence $\widetilde z=c^{-1}z$. Requiring $\widetilde i_A([\widetilde z])=1$ and $i_A([z])=1$ forces $\widetilde i_A([z])=c\cdot\widetilde i_A([c^{-1}z])=c=c\cdot i_A([z])$, so $\widetilde i_A=c\cdot i_A$! That is why `PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:64–65` must normalize `inv'` against **`E ζ`**, not against an independently chosen root in $\Omega'$.
4. *Why universal quantification inside `IsNormalised` eliminates dependence on $(\varpi,y,g_{\mathrm{Frob}})$.* Inside `IsNormalised ζ A v iA`, the uniformizer $\varpi\in v\setminus v^2$, the radical $y$ ($y^{\ell^r}=\varpi$), and the Frobenius lift $g_{\mathrm{Frob}}$ are universally quantified rather than chosen. Classically all admissible witnesses represent the **same** cohomology class $[z]\in H^2(D_A,R_A)$:
   - replacing $g_{\mathrm{Frob}}$ by $g_{\mathrm{Frob}}\iota$ ($\iota\in I_A$) leaves $\chi$ unchanged because $\chi$ is an additive homomorphism vanishing on $I_A$ and $D_A/I_A$ is topologically generated by the residue Frobenius (`inv_mul_mem_Ine_of_isFrob`, `src/ReciprocityTheory/GaloisValuations/Global.lean:239–262`);
   - replacing $y$ by $\widetilde y=\zeta^a y$ ($a\in R$) gives $g(\widetilde y)=\zeta^{\mathrm{cycMod}(g)a+\kappa(g)-a}\widetilde y$, shifting $\kappa(g)$ to $\widetilde\kappa(g)=\kappa(g)+(\mathrm{cycMod}(g)-1)a$ and hence adding to $z(g,h)=\kappa(g)\chi(h)$ the exact $1$-cochain coboundary $(d^1(a\chi))(g,h)=\mathrm{cycMod}(g)a\chi(h)-a\chi(gh)+a\chi(g)=(\mathrm{cycMod}(g)-1)a\chi(h)\in B^2(D_A,R_A)$, so $[\widetilde z]=[z]$;
   - replacing $\varpi$ by $u\varpi$ with $u\in(\mathcal O_F)_v^\times$ adds $[\kappa_u\cup\chi]=0$, because every unit of $F_v$ is a norm from the unramified degree-$\ell^r$ extension cut out by $\chi$ ([Milne, *CFT*, III Proposition 1.9, p. 102](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf); cf. [§11.4.3.5](11-the-window-bound-vwin.md#11435-norms-of-local-units-in-unramified-extensions)).
   Formal elegance in `isNormalised_transfer`: because `IsNormalised` is universally quantified on both sides, Lean needs none of these three invariance theorems during transport — pulling back one arbitrary primed witness produces one valid unprimed witness, on which `hn` immediately evaluates $i_A([z])=1$.

**Remark 2 (cup-product order and sign convention).** [Expository] In Milne's normalization of local reciprocity ($\operatorname{inv}_K : H^2(K, K^{\mathrm{al}\times}) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, sending the unramified fundamental class built from arithmetic Frobenius and a uniformizer $\varpi$ to $+1/n \bmod \mathbb Z$), Proposition 3.6 and Lemma 3.7 give $\operatorname{inv}_K(\varpi\cup\delta\chi)=\chi(\phi_K(\varpi))=1/\ell^r\bmod\mathbb Z$ when $\chi(\operatorname{Frob}_K)=1/\ell^r$ [Paper] [Milne, *CFT*, III Proposition 1.9, Proposition 3.6 and Lemma 3.7, pp. 102, 109](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf). Passing across the Kummer connecting map $\delta_{\mathrm{Kum}}(\varpi)=\kappa_\varpi\in H^1(K,\mu_{\ell^r})$ and graded-commutativity $\kappa_\varpi\cup\chi=-\chi\cup\kappa_\varpi$ gives $\operatorname{inv}_K(\kappa_\varpi\cup\chi)=-1/\ell^r\bmod\mathbb Z$. In `IsNormalised` (`PoitouTateNineTermTransgressionZeroApply.lean:36–37`), the archive places $\kappa$ first and $\chi$ second (`z(g, h) = κ g * χ h`) and sets `inv [z] = +1` in $\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z$ — that is, its normalized invariant equals $-\ell^r\operatorname{inv}_K$ (explicitly recorded in the statement of `hasse_kummer_unram`, `src/Development/WeilBound/CoreStatements/PublishedTheorems/PoitouTateDuality/ExistsUniformizerOrd.lean:16–25` [Lean], whose body is outside this reading). Scaling every local invariant simultaneously by the global unit $-1\in(\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z)^\times$ preserves bijectivity, global sum-zero reciprocity, and exactness of the nine-term sequence; what matters globally is that **every** place uses the same sign and root $\zeta$ (respectively $E\zeta$).

**Remark 3 (strict failure examples: wrong target prime, wrong direction, and dropped `hI2`).** [Expository]
1. *(F1) Wrong target prime ($v''\ne e_V(v)$, even with equal prime norms).* In Example 1 ($F=F'=\mathbb Q(\omega)$, $p=7$, $7\mathcal O_F=vv'$ with $v=(3+\omega)$ and $v'=e_V(v)=(3+\omega^2)$), suppose one tests normalization of $J'_{A'}$ at the **unmoved** prime $v''=v=(3+\omega)$ instead of $v'=e_V(v)$. Note that $N(v'')=7=N(v)$, so norm preservation `hN` still holds numerically! Yet `hI` fails at once: $\varpi''=3+\omega\in v''\setminus (v'')^2$ pulls back under $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$ to
   $$e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi'')=3+\omega^2\equiv 3+2=5\not\equiv 0\pmod v .$$
   Thus $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi'')\notin v$ is a **$v$-adic unit** ($\operatorname{ord}_v=0\ne 1$) and a non-cube in $\mathbb F_7^\times$. Its cubic Kummer character $\kappa_{3+\omega^2}$ is unramified on $D_A$, so both factors of $\kappa_{3+\omega^2}\cup\chi$ factor through $D_A/I_A\cong\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ (whose $H^2$ with torsion coefficients vanishes, equivalently the tame symbol has valuation exponents $a_1=a_2=0$, giving $a_1b_2-b_1a_2=0$), and $J_A([\kappa_{3+\omega^2}\cup\chi])=0\ne 1$ in $\mathbb F_3$! Geometrically, $A'=E(A)$ lies over $v'$, not $v''$ (`eq_of_isPlaceOver`, `Global.lean:231–237`), since $v_{A'}(3+\omega)=v_A(3+\omega^2)=1\not<1$.
2. *(F2) Wrong direction (non-involutive field automorphism and untransported root or residue).*
   - *(F2a) Forward $e_{\mathcal O}$ instead of $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$ when $e\ne e^{-1}$.* In Examples 1–2, $e^2=1$ masks the difference between $e_{\mathcal O}$ and $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}$ on elements. To separate them strictly, take the cyclic cubic field $F=F'=\mathbb Q(\alpha)$ with $\alpha=\zeta_7+\zeta_7^{-1}$ ($\alpha^3+\alpha^2-2\alpha-1=0$, $\mathcal O_F=\mathbb Z[\alpha]$, discriminant $49$) and the order-$3$ automorphism $e(\alpha)=\alpha^2-2$ ($e^3=1$, $e\ne e^{-1}$). Modulo $p=29$, $X^3+X^2-2X-1\equiv(X-3)(X-7)(X-18)$, so $29\mathcal O_F=\mathfrak p_3\mathfrak p_{18}\mathfrak p_7$ with $\mathfrak p_r=(29,\alpha-r)$. Since $3^2-2=7$, $7^2-2\equiv 18$, and $18^2-2\equiv 3\pmod{29}$, we have $e_{\mathcal O}(\alpha-3)=\alpha^2-5\in\mathfrak p_{18}$, and $e_V$ cycles the three primes in order:
     $$\mathfrak p_3\xrightarrow{\;e_V\;}\mathfrak p_{18}\xrightarrow{\;e_V\;}\mathfrak p_7\xrightarrow{\;e_V\;}\mathfrak p_3 .$$
     For $v=\mathfrak p_3$ and $v'=e_V(\mathfrak p_3)=\mathfrak p_{18}$, take $\varpi'=\alpha-18\in\mathfrak p_{18}\setminus\mathfrak p_{18}^2$. The inverse $e_{\mathcal O}^{-1}(\varpi')=e_{\mathcal O}^2(\alpha-18)$ lies in $\mathfrak p_3\setminus\mathfrak p_3^2$, whereas applying the **forward** map $e_{\mathcal O}(\varpi')=\alpha^2-20$ lands in $\mathfrak p_7=e_V^2(\mathfrak p_3)$ and reduces modulo $\mathfrak p_3$ ($\alpha\equiv 3$) to $3^2-20=-11\equiv 18\not\equiv 0\pmod{29}$ — a unit in $(\mathcal O_F)_{\mathfrak p_3}$ ($\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak p_3}=0\ne 1$), breaking `hI` and collapsing the local pairing at $\mathfrak p_3$ to $0$.
   - *(F2b) Untransported root $\zeta$ instead of $E(\zeta)$, or $1_{\mathbb F_4}$ instead of $\bar e_v$ over $\mathbb F_3$.* Return to $F=F'=\mathbb Q(\omega)$, $e(\omega)=\omega^2$, $\ell=3,r=1$, and $\zeta=\omega$. By Theorem 18.3.14, $J'_{A'}=J_A\circ e_D^*$ satisfies `IsNormalised (E ζ) A' v' J'_{A'}` with $E(\zeta)=\omega^2$. If one tests **`IsNormalised ζ A' v' J'_{A'}`** using the untransported root $\zeta=\omega$, then for the same $(y',\chi')$ we have $g'(y')=\omega^{\widetilde\kappa'(g')}y'=(\omega^2)^{2\widetilde\kappa'(g')}y'$, so $\widetilde\kappa'(e_Dg)=2\kappa(g)$ in $\mathbb F_3$ (`2 * 2 = 1 mod 3`), $\widetilde z'=2z'$, and linearity gives
     $$J'_{A'}([\widetilde z'])=J_A([2z])=2\,J_A([z])=2\ne 1\quad\text{in }\mathbb F_3!$$
     The exact same factor $2\ne 1$ appears at the inert prime $v=(2)$ of Example 2 if one forgets that $\bar e_v\colon\mathbb F_4\to\mathbb F_4$ acts by $\bar\omega\mapsto\bar\omega^2$ and pairs $\varpi'$ with $\gamma$ ($\gamma^3=\omega$, where $\gamma^4=\omega\gamma=(E\zeta)^2\gamma$) while reading the Kummer exponent against $E(\zeta)=\omega^2$.
3. *(F3) Dropping the ideal-square clause `hI2` ($\varpi\notin v^2$).* In Example 1, $\varpi'=(\pi')^3\in (v')^2\subset v'$ satisfies $\varpi'\in v'$ (`h1`) and fails $\varpi'\notin (v')^2$ (`h2`). Choosing $y'=\pi'\in F'$ gives $(y')^3=\varpi'$ (`h3`), $g'(y')=y'$, so $\kappa'\equiv 0$, $z'\equiv 0$, and $J'_{A'}([z'])=0\ne 1$; without $\varpi\notin v^2$, `IsNormalised` would be inconsistent (`0 = 1`) at every place! Likewise, under a ramified ring homomorphism such as $\mathbb Z\hookrightarrow\mathbb Z[i]$ at $2=-i(1+i)^2$, the uniformizer $2\in(2)\setminus(4)$ maps into $(1+i)^2$, showing why ideal-square preservation `hI2` uses the bijectivity of $e_{\mathcal O}$.

### 18.3.15 Cyclotomic characters and total complexity under field isomorphisms

**Motivation.** The residue and normalization transport in [§18.3.14](#18314-transporting-residue-fields-prime-norms-and-local-brauer-normalizations) uses an isomorphism of algebraic closures. The Poitou–Tate comparison uses the action of the absolute Galois group on roots of unity to specify a scalar action. Transport along a field isomorphism must preserve that scalar. It must also carry the archimedean premise at $\ell=2$ to the target field. The first task uses a lift to the closures, while the second uses precomposition on embeddings.

**Example 1 — eighth roots over $\mathbb Q(i)$.** [Expository] Let $t=\exp(2\pi i/32)$, $i=t^8$, and let $e$ conjugate $\mathbb Q(i)$. In $\mathbb Q(t)$ choose $E(t)=t^3$ and $\sigma(t)=t^5$. These substitutions define automorphisms. The odd powers of $t$ are the roots of $X^{16}+1$, which becomes Eisenstein at $2$ after shifting by $1$. The substitutions restrict to $e$ and the identity on $\mathbb Q(i)$, respectively, and extend to the algebraic numbers by the lemma below. On this finite layer $3^{-1}=11\bmod32$ and the conjugate acts by $3\cdot5\cdot11\equiv5\bmod32$. With $\zeta=t^4$, its scalar on $\mu_8$ is $5$ on both sides. The source radical equation $t^8=i$ gives $\sigma(t)/t=\zeta$. The transported radical $t^3$ has eighth power $-i$ and ratio $t^{15}/t^3=\zeta^3$. The exponent is $1$ in $E\zeta=\zeta^3$, or $3$ in $\zeta$. Using the old generator with exponent $1$ predicts the wrong ratio. Another lift starts with $t\mapsto t^7$, since $7\equiv3\bmod4$. Its conjugation still acts by $5$ on this layer. The transported ratio is then $\zeta^7$.

**Example 2 — ninth roots over $\mathbb Q(\omega)$.** [Expository] Let $s=\exp(2\pi i/27)$ and $\omega=s^9$. The primitive $27$th roots are the roots of $X^{18}+X^9+1$. Modulo $3$ this polynomial equals $(X-1)^{18}$, by factoring $X^{27}-1$ and reducing the factors. After shifting by $1$ the constant is $3$, so Eisenstein proves irreducibility. The automorphisms $E(s)=s^2$ and $\sigma(s)=s^4$ of $\mathbb Q(s)$ extend to the closure. They restrict to $\omega\mapsto\omega^2$ and the identity on $\mathbb Q(\omega)$. Since $2^{-1}=14\bmod27$, conjugation gives $2\cdot4\cdot14\equiv4\bmod27$. On $\eta=s^3$ and $E\eta$, the cyclotomic exponent is $4\bmod9$, reducing to $1\bmod3$. The source ratio for the radical $s^9=\omega$ is $\eta$. The target radical $s^2$ has ninth power $\omega^2$ and ratio $s^8/s^2=\eta^2$. The exponent in $E\eta=\eta^2$ is $1$, whereas that in $\eta$ is $2$. To recover the transported exponent from the old coordinates, multiply by $2^{-1}=5\bmod9$. Multiplying by $2$ instead gives $4$, exposing the inverse-direction error.

**Definitions.** Let $F,F'$ be number fields with algebraic closures $\Omega,\Omega'$ and a unital field isomorphism $e:F\to F'$. Identify each field with its image in its closure. Write $G_F=\operatorname{Aut}(\Omega/F)$ with the Krull topology, and similarly for $F'$. The group $\mu_n(\Omega)$ consists of the roots of $X^n-1$ for $n\ge1$. A primitive root has exact order $n$. A real embedding is an embedding into $\mathbb R$. Infinite places are complex embeddings modulo complex conjugation. A place is real when its representative has image in $\mathbb R$, and complex otherwise. Total complexity means every infinite place is complex.

**Lemma 18.3.15A — extension and conjugation.** [Expository] There is an isomorphism $E:\Omega\to\Omega'$ extending $e$. Every lift induces a unique topological group isomorphism $C_E:G_F\to G_{F'}$ satisfying $C_E(\sigma)(Ex)=E(\sigma x)$ for all $\sigma,x$. Any two lifts differ by right composition with an element of $G_F$.

**Proof.** Order embeddings of intermediate fields of $\Omega/F$ into $\Omega'$ extending $e$ by extension. A chain has an upper bound given by its union, so choose a maximal embedding $f:K\to\Omega'$ by Zorn's lemma. If $x\in\Omega\setminus K$, applying $f$ to the coefficients of its minimal polynomial gives an irreducible polynomial over $f(K)$. Choose a root in $\Omega'$. Evaluating at it embeds the polynomial quotient defining $K(x)$ and extends $f$, contradicting maximality. The image of the resulting map on $\Omega$ is algebraically closed. Every element of $\Omega'$ is algebraic over $F'$ and hence over this image, so the map is onto. The same argument starting from a prescribed intermediate embedding proves the extensions used in the examples. This adapts Stacks Lemma 9.10.5 by identifying the base fields through $e$. [Stacks Project, Tag 09GU](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/09GU)

Define $C_E(\sigma)=E\circ\sigma\circ E^{-1}$. It fixes each $e(a)$ for $a\in F$ since $\sigma a=a$. Cancellation gives the group law and inverse $C_{E^{-1}}$. Surjectivity of $E$ gives uniqueness from the action identity. The inverse image of the pointwise stabilizer of a finite intermediate field $K'$ is the pointwise stabilizer of $E^{-1}(K')$, which is finite over $F$ by transport of a basis. These stabilizers form an identity neighborhood basis. The same argument for $E^{-1}$ and translation prove continuity in both directions. For two lifts $E_1,E_2$, the map $\gamma=E_1^{-1}E_2$ fixes $F$ and $C_{E_2}(\sigma)=C_{E_1}(\gamma\sigma\gamma^{-1})$.

The model bridge at Bridge.lean:14–19 uses a copy inside the algebraic closure of $\mathbb Q$ and applies the comparison to bring the target conclusion back to $F$. The archive constructs the lift through Mathlib's scalar transport in ExistsSubfieldModel.lean:17–20. Its conjugation proof transports a finite basis to check the fixing subgroup. [§18.3.12](#18312-transporting-galois-groups-along-a-field-isomorphism-conjugation-decomposition-groups-and-quotients)

**Theorem 18.3.15.** For every pair of number fields, closures, and isomorphism as above, the following hold.

1. For every lift $E$, every $\sigma\in G_F$, prime $\ell$, and integer $r\ge0$, the cyclotomic exponent of $C_E(\sigma)$ on $\mu_{\ell^r}(\Omega')$ equals that of $\sigma$ on $\mu_{\ell^r}(\Omega)$ as a unit modulo $\ell^r$. The full $\ell$-adic characters agree under $C_E$ as well.
2. Precomposition with $e$ bijects the real embeddings of $F'$ with those of $F$. It also bijects their infinite places, preserving the type. In particular $F$ is totally complex if and only if $F'$ is.

**Proof of (1).** In an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, $X^n-1$ has $n$ distinct roots. Let $d$ be the least common multiple of their orders. For each prime factor of $d$, choose a root realizing the maximal power of that prime in its order and take its prime-power part. The product of those parts has order $d$ because the parts commute and their orders are coprime. All $n$ elements solve $X^d-1$, so $n\le d$, and $d\mid n$. Thus there is a generator $\zeta$ of order $n$. Any automorphism $\sigma$ takes it to $\zeta^a$ of order $n$, with $a$ a unit modulo $n$. If $u$ is a unit modulo $n$, the generator $\zeta^u$ therefore gives the same exponent. For every power $\zeta^k$ the action is raising to $a$. Composition multiplies exponents. At $n=1$ there is only one class.

Injectivity of $E$ preserves exact order. For $n=\ell^r$, let $a,b$ be the source and target representatives in $[0,n)$. Then $(E\zeta)^b=C_E(\sigma)(E\zeta)=E(\sigma\zeta)=E(\zeta^a)=(E\zeta)^a$. Exact order gives $n\mid a-b$, hence $a=b$. This is [Lean, read] the proof of `cycMod_intrinsic` in src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/RingEquivCyclotomicCharacter.lean:19–31. The helper `apply_eq_pow_cycMod_val` at lines 11–17 specializes Mathlib's `cyclotomicCharacter.spec` to the ring automorphism underlying $\sigma$. It unfolds `cycMod` as the reduction of `cycZ` and supplies the action formula for any root with $n$th power one. The production body detects equality on a primitive root via `pow_inj` on bounded representatives, then uses injectivity of those representatives and extensionality of units.

The finite character is determined by restriction to $F(\mu_{\ell^r})$, the splitting field of a separable polynomial. Its kernel is the pointwise stabilizer of this field, so the character into the discrete finite unit group is continuous. If $\xi$ has order $\ell^{r+1}$, then $\xi^\ell$ has order $\ell^r$. Applying $\sigma$ to $\xi^\ell$ proves compatibility under reduction. Therefore these classes define a unit of $\mathbb Z_\ell$, with inverse given by the compatible inverse classes. The character is continuous by continuity of each component and the inverse limit topology. The reductions jointly detect an $\ell$-adic integer, so equality at every layer proves the full-character equality. This is an expository consequence of the finite theorem, and identifies the full character with `cycZ` by its reductions.

A change of lift conjugates the input by $\gamma$. All the character targets are abelian, so the character values are independent of the lift. For a fixed $\sigma$, both target automorphisms agree on each $F'(\mu_{\ell^r})$ since they fix $F'$ and have identical powers on the generating roots. They agree on the union as well. The full automorphism of $\Omega'$ and the transported element $E\zeta$ can depend on the lift.

**Proof of (2).** If $j':F'\to\mathbb R$ is an embedding, $j'\circ e$ embeds $F$. Conversely $j:F\to\mathbb R$ gives $j\circ e^{-1}$. These operations are inverse. The same construction works for embeddings into $\mathbb C$ and commutes with complex conjugation, so it passes to infinite places. Surjectivity of $e$ gives equality of images for $j'\circ e$ and $j'$, preserving whether the image lies in $\mathbb R$. Thus if $F$ is totally complex, a real place of $F'$ would pull back to a real place of $F$, a contradiction. The inverse isomorphism gives the converse. [Lean, read] The forward implication is exactly the argument of `isTotallyComplex_of_ringEquiv` in src/Development/AutomorphicSpectrum/Proofs/BlockDecomposition/TotallyComplexOfRingEquiv.lean:9–16. It rewrites complex as the negation of real and pulls the hypothetical real place back via InfinitePlace.isReal_comap_iff.

**Remarks.**

- **Character consumers.** [Lean, read] PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:29 obtains `hcyc` for the conjugation from line 25. Line 31 passes it to the local invariant comparison of §18.3.13. At lines 48–58, write $q,q'$ for the global Galois quotient maps and $\Psi$ for the inherited quotient equivalence with $q'\Phi=\Psi q$. The source equation is $\mu q=\chi_{\ell^r,F}$. Defining $\mu'=\mu\circ\Psi^{-1}$ gives $\mu'(q'\Phi\sigma)=\mu(q\sigma)=\chi_{\ell^r,F}(\sigma)=\chi_{\ell^r,F'}(\Phi\sigma)$. Surjectivity of $\Phi$ proves the target equation. Line 72 passes `hcyc` to the normalization comparison of §18.3.14.
- **Archimedean consumer.** [Lean, read] PTConclAt.lean:10–17 requires $\ell\ne2\ \lor\ \operatorname{IsTotallyComplex}(F)$ and $0<r$. PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:63 acts by the identity on the first disjunct and by the forward implication of (2) on the second. Line 80 supplies `hl'` to `hPT` on $F'$. Odd $\ell$ satisfies the first disjunct. At $\ell=2$ the premise requires total complexity, which the field isomorphism preserves. In the examples, real images of $i$ and $\omega$ would give a square equal to $-1$ and $(u+1/2)^2=-3/4$, respectively, so both bases satisfy the premise even at $2$.
- **Root coordinates.** [Expository] In the automorphism action formula both sides change by the same power when one changes the generator. But applying $E$ to a radical equation $g(y)/y=\zeta^\kappa$ gives the same exponent $\kappa$ with the new generator $E\zeta$. In a common closure, if $E\zeta=\zeta^u$, conversion to the old generator multiplies by $u$ and conversion back by $u^{-1}$. Thus line 64 transports the supplied primitive root and line 80 uses the resulting $E\zeta$, even though the character itself is intrinsic.
- **Condition boundary.** [Expository] The inclusion $\mathbb Q\to\mathbb Q(i)$ shows why an embedding cannot replace the isomorphism in the real-place equivalence. A real embedding exists on the source side and none exists on the target side. The power argument applies to any modulus in characteristic zero, while the Lean comparison uses prime powers and the Poitou–Tate input also requires positive $r$.
- **Reading boundary.** The two production proofs and the direct suppliers in the source ledger receive local R3. Residue, inertia and local Brauer transport are inherited. For normalized local invariant existence see [§18.3.6](#1836-the-unramified-local-condition-and-its-dual). The nine-term and Sha branches are treated in [§18.3.16](#18316-transporting-the-poitoutate-nine-term-sequence) and [§18.3.17](#18317-transporting-the-tateshafarevich-kernels-in-degrees-one-and-two). **Review.** Same-source preliminary acceptance covers the finite cyclotomic powers, the real-place comparison and the stated consumer. The quotient action and the transported root supply the next comparison in [§18.3.16](#18316-transporting-the-poitoutate-nine-term-sequence).

### 18.3.16 Transporting the Poitou–Tate nine-term sequence

**Motivation.** With the cyclotomic factorization and transported root of [§18.3.15](#18315-cyclotomic-characters-and-total-complexity-under-field-isomorphisms) in place, the field isomorphism in the model comparison goes from $F$ to $F'$, while exactness is supplied over $F'$ and needed over $F$. Cohomology pulls back along the group isomorphism. A functional must retain its value on a transported test class, so the functional and its test argument require different rules.

**Example 1 (cyclic relabelling).** [Expository] Over $R=\mathbb F_5$, let the generator $s$ of $C_5$ act on $R^2$ by $T(x,y)=(x+y,y)$. Send $s$ to $t^2$ in another copy of $C_5$, where $t$ acts by $T^3$. Since $T^5=1$, this intertwines the actions. A cocycle with $f(s)=(u,v)$ is forced by recursion to satisfy
$$f(s^k)=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}T^j(u,v)=(ku+\tbinom{k}{2}v,kv).$$
The sum for $k=5$ vanishes. Splitting the sum proves the cocycle identity, including when the exponents wrap around. For $f'(t)=(u',v')$ the same formula has $3\binom{k}{2}$ in place of $\binom{k}{2}$. The coboundary values at the generators are $(y,0)$ and $(3y,0)$, so the class coordinate is the second coordinate on both sides.

Pullback by $s\mapsto t^2$ gives $(2u'+3v',2v')$. The inverse $t\mapsto s^3$ gives $(3u+3v,3v)$. Both composites have diagonal entries $6$ and off-diagonal entry $15$, hence are the identity modulo $5$. For $(u',v')=(1,1)$ the values at $t^0,\ldots,t^4$ are $(0,0),(1,1),(0,2),(2,3),(2,4)$. Reading indices $0,2,4,1,3$ gives $(0,0),(0,2),(2,4),(1,1),(2,3)$ on the source. Reading back at $0,3,1,4,2$ gives the original list. A unit target functional evaluates the target class $1$ to $1$. Its source coordinate must be $3$, giving $3\cdot2=1$ on the source class. Copying the class scalar $2$ gives $4$.

**Example 2 (a finite exact complex).** [Expository] Over $\mathbb F_5$ take nine terms $R,R^2,R^2,R^2,R^2,R^2,R^2,R^2,R$, with zero at each end. Set $i(h)=(h,0)$, $n(a,b)=(b,0)$, $q(a,b)=b$. The primed arrows are $i,n,n,n,n,n,n,q$. Regard the coordinates in positions 3, 6 and 9 as functional coordinates in a dual basis. All ordinary verticals and the corresponding equivalences of underlying test modules scale by $2$ from primed to unprimed. Thus the functional verticals scale by $3$. Set the unprimed arrows to
$$i,\quad 4n,\quad 4n,\quad n,\quad 4n,\quad 4n,\quad n,\quad 4q.$$
The first square sends $h$ to $(2h,0)$ on both paths. In the next six squares the first coordinates are $3b,2b,2b,3b,2b,2b$, with second coordinate zero. The tail gives $3b$. These follow by multiplying the displayed scalars, using $4\cdot2=3$ and $4\cdot3=2$. In reverse, the ordinary verticals scale by $3$ and the functional ones by $2$. For each of the eight squares, writing $s_j$ for the forward vertical scalar and $k_j$ for the lower arrow scalar gives $s_{j+1}^{-1}k_js_j=1$. This recovers every upper arrow. For example the reverse tail gives $3^{-1}(4q)(2a,2b)=b$.

Both heads are injective. At every internal term the incoming image and outgoing kernel are the line $(h,0)$. A preimage of $(h,0)$ under $kn$ is $(0,k^{-1}h)$, and under $i$ it is $h$. The upper and lower tails lift $h$ to $(0,h)$ and $(0,4h)$, respectively. Both rows are therefore exact. Copying the scalar $2$ onto all the dual arrows leaves the first square intact and first fails at square 2. At $(0,1)$ the two paths give $(3,0)$ and $(2,0)$. The fourth square still commutes and the fifth fails the same way. At the fifth square the upper pairing on $(a,b),(u,v)$ is $bu$ and the lower pairing is $4bu$. Its evaluation on the transported inputs is $4(2b)(2u)=bu$, so the scalar identity holds even when the wrongly chosen functional arrow fails. The tail with that choice gives $3$ versus $2$ at $(0,1)$.

**Definitions and hypotheses.** For every pair of number fields and chosen closures, fix one isomorphism $e:F\to F'$ with lift $E$, as in *PtConclOfIsoOf*, with the inherited local comparison hypothesis *LocHyp*. Take any prime $\ell$, $r>0$, $R=\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z$, and assume $\ell\ne2$ or $F$ totally complex. Fix a finite set $S$ containing all primes above $\ell$, valuation rings $A_v$ above its primes, a primitive $\ell^r$-th root $\zeta$, and an invariant family normalized at $\zeta$. Fix a cyclotomic factor $\mu$ on $\Gamma=G_{F,S}$ whose pullback to the absolute Galois group is the finite cyclotomic character. Fix any finite discrete $R$-module $M$ with continuous action $\rho$ of $\Gamma$. Use the absolute Galois conjugation $\Phi$ of $E$, the induced quotient equivalence $\Psi:\Gamma\to\Gamma'$, the induced prime relabelling $e_I:S\to S'$, and the restriction $e_D(v):D_v\to D'_{e_Iv}$ of $\Phi$. At $e_Iv$ use the image of $A_v$ under $E$. The primed data use the same $M$, $\rho'=\rho\circ\Psi^{-1}$, $\mu'=\mu\circ\Psi^{-1}$, root $E\zeta$ and the corresponding normalized invariants. The continuous localization maps satisfy
$$\phi'_{e_Iv} e_D(v)=\Psi\phi_v.$$
This follows from the quotient compatibility applied to the underlying absolute automorphism of a decomposition-group element. These comparisons are inputs from the preceding work. [Galois transport](#18312-transporting-galois-groups-along-a-field-isomorphism-conjugation-decomposition-groups-and-quotients) · [Local invariant comparison](#18313-pulling-back-local-invariants-and-trace-pairings) · [Residue and normalization](#18314-transporting-residue-fields-prime-norms-and-local-brauer-normalizations)

Let $N=\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R(\mu))$ with action $(g\lambda)(m)=\mu(g)\lambda(\rho(g^{-1})m)$, and define $N'$ similarly. Their underlying modules agree. The equality $\Psi(g^{-1})=\Psi(g)^{-1}$ and both action identities imply $\Psi(g)\lambda=g\lambda$ under this identification. Write $B^\vee=\operatorname{Hom}_R(B,R)$ for the linear dual of a cohomology module. Define $X_k=H^k(\Gamma,M)$, $Y_k=\prod_{v\in S}H^k(D_v,M)$, $B_k=H^k(\Gamma,N)$ and $W_k=B_{2-k}^\vee$ for $0\le k\le2$, with corresponding primed terms. All cochains are continuous. Write $j_v$ for the scalar trace given by $\operatorname{invAt}_v$ on $H^2(D_v,R(\mu\circ\phi_v))$. The inherited comparison, called *hj* in the source, says $j'_{e_Iv}([z'])=j_v([z])$ whenever $z'(e_D(v)g,e_D(v)h)=z(g,h)$ for all $g,h\in D_v$.

**Lemma 18.3.16A (nine vertical isomorphisms).** [Lean, read] The maps point from primed to unprimed. Let $\beta_k:B'_k\to B_k$ denote pullback by $\Psi$ on twisted dual coefficients.

| Position | Unprimed term | Vertical map on primed inputs |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | $X_0$ | $a_0$ keeps the invariant element |
| 2 | $Y_0$ | $P_0$ takes the same invariant element from coordinate $e_Iv$ at $v$ |
| 3 | $W_0=H^2(\Gamma,N)^\vee$ | $w_0(\lambda')=\lambda'\circ\beta_2^{-1}$ |
| 4 | $X_1$ | $a_1[f']=[g\mapsto f'(\Psi g)]$ |
| 5 | $Y_1$ | $P_1$ sends the representative $f'_{e_Iv}$ to $d\mapsto f'_{e_Iv}(e_D(v)d)$ at $v$ |
| 6 | $W_1=H^1(\Gamma,N)^\vee$ | $w_1(\lambda')=\lambda'\circ\beta_1^{-1}$ |
| 7 | $X_2$ | $a_2[z']=[(g,h)\mapsto z'(\Psi g,\Psi h)]$ |
| 8 | $Y_2$ | $P_2$ sends the representative $z'_{e_Iv}$ to $(g,h)\mapsto z'_{e_Iv}(e_D(v)g,e_D(v)h)$ at $v$ |
| 9 | $W_2=H^0(\Gamma,N)^\vee$ | $w_2(\lambda')=\lambda'\circ\beta_0^{-1}$ |

**Proof.** For an intertwining topological group isomorphism $u:G\to G'$ let $U_k$ precompose in each argument and let $U_0$ be the identity on coefficients. These maps are linear and continuous on cochain arguments by continuity of the finite product of $u$. In degree zero, $U_1d'm(g)=u(g)m-m=gm-m$. The degree-one differential on the pullback is
$$d(U_1f')(g,h)=u(g)f'(u(h))-f'(u(g)u(h))+f'(u(g))=(U_2d'f')(g,h).$$
For a degree-two cochain the differential is
$$u(g)z'(u(h),u(k))-z'(u(g)u(h),u(k))+z'(u(g),u(h)u(k))-z'(u(g),u(h)),$$
which is $d'z'(u(g),u(h),u(k))$. Hence cocycles pull back to cocycles, and a coboundary pulls back to the boundary of the pulled-back primitive. The action identity at $u^{-1}(g')$ gives the same argument for the inverse. Both composites cancel on cochains, hence on classes. For $\Psi$ this proves the claims for $a_k$ and $\beta_k$. For $e_D(v)$ the action identities follow from localization compatibility. The inverse to $P_k$ at $v'$ takes the component at $e_I^{-1}v'$ and precomposes by $e_D(e_I^{-1}v')^{-1}$. The two composites cancel at every coordinate. Finally $w_k^{-1}(\lambda)=\lambda\circ\beta_{2-k}$ by cancellation. In particular $(w_k\lambda')(\beta_{2-k}b')=\lambda'(b')$.

**Lemma 18.3.16B (eight adjacent squares).** Let $g_k:X_k\to Y_k$ be product restriction. Define $t_k:Y_k\to W_k$ by summing the scalar traces of the local cup of the $M$ class with the restricted global dual class in complementary degree. For $k=1$ the restricted dual class goes first in the cup. For $k=0,2$ the $M$ class goes first. In particular, if $b_v$ denotes the restriction of a dual test class $b$ to $D_v$, the functionals on $b$ are
$$t_0(x)(b)=\sum_v j_v(x_v\cup b_v),\qquad
t_1(x)(b)=\sum_v j_v(b_v\cup x_v),\qquad
t_2(x)(b)=\sum_v j_v(x_v\cup b_v).$$
The test degrees are $2,1,0$, respectively. Given any primed connecting maps $d'_1:W'_0\to X'_1$ and $d'_2:W'_1\to X'_2$, set $d_1=a_1d'_1w_0^{-1}$ and $d_2=a_2d'_2w_1^{-1}$.

| Arrow | Domain and codomain | Square on primed inputs |
|---|---|---|
| $g_0$ | $X_0\to Y_0$ | $g_0a_0=P_0g'_0$ |
| $t_0$ | $Y_0\to W_0$ | $t_0P_0=w_0t'_0$ |
| $d_1$ | $W_0\to X_1$ | $d_1w_0=a_1d'_1$ |
| $g_1$ | $X_1\to Y_1$ | $g_1a_1=P_1g'_1$ |
| $t_1$ | $Y_1\to W_1$ | $t_1P_1=w_1t'_1$ |
| $d_2$ | $W_1\to X_2$ | $d_2w_1=a_2d'_2$ |
| $g_2$ | $X_2\to Y_2$ | $g_2a_2=P_2g'_2$ |
| $t_2$ | $Y_2\to W_2$ | $t_2P_2=w_2t'_2$ |

**Proof.** The three restriction identities are the named inputs *local_degree_zero_square*, *local_degree_one_square* and *local_degree_two_square*. Their contracts compare the same invariant element in degree zero, the same evaluation at $\Psi(\phi_v d)=\phi'_{e_Iv}(e_D(v)d)$ in degree one, and that evaluation in each argument in degree two. The named inputs *trace_pairing_degree_zero_square*, *trace_pairing_degree_one_square* and *trace_pairing_degree_two_square* give
$$t_k(P_kx')(\beta_{2-k}b')=t'_k(x')(b').$$
This uses the same prime relabelling and the same two families $j,j'$ as above. At $k=0$, an arbitrary $b\in B_2$ equals $\beta_2(\beta_2^{-1}b)$, so evaluating the left side at $b$ gives $t'_0(x')(\beta_2^{-1}b)$. At $k=1$, take an arbitrary $b\in B_1$ and use $\beta_1^{-1}b$ to get $t'_1(x')(\beta_1^{-1}b)$. At the tail an arbitrary $b\in B_0$ gives $t'_2(x')(\beta_0^{-1}b)$. Equality at every test class proves the three functional squares. The connector squares follow by cancellation of $w_0^{-1}w_0$ and $w_1^{-1}w_1$. In particular $X_2$ receives $a_2d'_2w_1^{-1}$ and maps out by degree-two restriction. The squares at the two zero ends commute by uniqueness of the zero maps.

**Lemma 18.3.16C (exactness under the ladder).** [Lean, read] Over any commutative ring, let $A'\xrightarrow{f'}B'\xrightarrow{g'}C'$ and $A\xrightarrow f B\xrightarrow g C$ have linear isomorphisms $a,b,c$ from primed to unprimed, with $fa=bf'$ and $gb=cg'$. Then either pair is exact at its middle term if and only if the other is. Under the fixed field data, if the primed nine-term contract holds, then the unprimed contract holds with the connectors of Lemma 18.3.16B. Transport along the inverse ladder gives the converse.

**Proof.** Write $y=b(y')$. By injectivity of $c$, $gy=0$ if and only if $g'y'=0$. If the primed pair is exact, the latter holds precisely when $y'=f'x'$ for some $x'$. Such a witness gives $y=f(ax')$. Conversely if $y=fx$, use surjectivity to write $x=ax'$, and then cancel $b$ from $b(y')=b(f'x')$. This proves exactness downstairs. The inverse squares $f'a^{-1}=b^{-1}f$ and $g'b^{-1}=c^{-1}g$ give the reverse proof.

Apply this in succession at $Y_0,W_0,X_1,Y_1,W_1,X_2,Y_2$, to the pairs $(g_0,t_0),(t_0,d_1),(d_1,g_1),(g_1,t_1),(t_1,d_2),(d_2,g_2),(g_2,t_2)$. For head injectivity write two inputs as $a_0x',a_0y'$. Equality of their images gives $P_0g'_0x'=P_0g'_0y'$, so injectivity twice gives equality of the inputs. At the tail choose $y'$ with $t'_2y'=w_2^{-1}w$ for any $w\in W_2$. Then $t_2(P_2y')=w$. Using inverses proves the converse at the endpoints. The reverse connector witnesses $a_1^{-1}d_1w_0$ and $a_2^{-1}d_2w_1$ recover the original witnesses.

**Notes.**

- Commutativity is necessary. In Example 2 replace the second lower arrow by zero, dropping just its square. The lower row remains a complex and all the other squares commute. At position 2 the kernel becomes $R^2$ while the incoming image remains a line. The archive asks for existence of connecting witnesses. Once upper witnesses and verticals are specified, commutativity determines the lower witnesses.
- [Lean, read] *PtConclOfIsoOf.lean:80* calls the target hypothesis with $E\zeta$ and returns exactness and a separate Sha output. Lines 82–89 fix the equivalences and action identities. The forward direction of the primed *nineTermExact_iff* at line 93 produces *NineG*. Lines 94–101 supply *hj*: the identity coefficient maps from *locFam_apply_eq* preserve cocycle values when entering *muLoc*, and subtype extensionality identifies the local arguments with their images under $e_D$. Hence the inherited invariant comparison applies to the same representatives. The wrapper calls *nineG_of_iso*, which constructs *dual_sq* by evaluating on all transported test classes and invokes *nine_ladder*. The latter defines both connectors and invokes *exact_transfer* seven times. Line 91 uses the reverse direction of the unprimed *nineTermExact_iff* to return *NineTermExact*. The Sha consumer at lines 102–103 uses a separate theorem.
- [Paper] Milne I.4.10(b) gives the classical order and endpoints. The preceding definition identifies the trace-type map as dual to complementary-degree localization under local duality. The classical convention adjoins archimedean places and uses Tate groups there. These vanish at complex places and at real places for odd $\ell$, where $2$ acts invertibly. The linear dual in the finite $\ell^r$-torsion interface identifies with the character dual via $a\mapsto a/\ell^r+\mathbb Z$: each character lands in the subgroup killed by $\ell^r$, and additive maps of such modules are $R$-linear. Milne credits Tate's theorem and Poitou's independent partial results. This subsection establishes transport of the archive's specified normalized maps. [Milne, *Arithmetic Duality Theorems*, I §4, pp. 56–57, 66](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf)

Cyclotomic, archimedean, residue, inertia and Brauer-normalization comparisons are inherited. The separate cyclotomic subsection §18.3.15 passed its own same-source review. Independent review, Lean kernel checking, the single-field Poitou–Tate producer and the separate Sha proof remain outside this reading.
